% VOLUME I - CHAPTER 4 FINAL CLASSICAL MASTERPIECE (2026-06-14)
% Geometric-dimension voice; math frozen.

\chapter{Source Realizability and Spectral Truncation}
\label{ch:04}
\setcounter{section}{-1}

\section{Introduction}
\label{sec:ch40}

Every antenna laboratory eventually confronts the same practical question. A feed network, aperture, or array of fixed mechanical outline is connected at a fixed frequency: how many \emph{distinct} far-zone radiation patterns can that hardware actually produce, and how many of those patterns can a finite measurement sphere recover to a declared tolerance? Chapters~1--3 established the language---angular structure on outgoing fields, retarded export from bounded sources, and flux-normalized harmonic coordinates \cite{maxwell1873}. Chapter~\ref{ch:04} uses that language to state what finite geometry \emph{permits}. A complete harmonic listing may display arbitrarily many indices; electrical size $k_{0}D_{s}$, support diameter, and aperture area fix how many independent radiative channels bounded impressed currents can populate and how many a finite instrument can observe \cite{chu1948}. The wattmeter on $\Gamma_R$ closes the loop: every coefficient dispute is ultimately a flux dispute once outgoing selection has been enforced \cite{poynting1884}.
% PHYSICAL-HISTORY:sec:ch40

Maxwell's retarded export underpins every realizability question posed in this chapter \cite{maxwell1873}. Poynting flux on $\Gamma_R$ remains the wattmeter standard against which synthesized harmonic targets must close \cite{poynting1884}. Stratton linked impressed currents, equivalent apertures, and far-zone patterns through the radiation integrals that method-of-moments practice discretizes on real feeds \cite{stratton1941}. Chu's bandwidth and minimum-$Q$ arguments made electrical size an integer-counting resource for independent radiative channels on bounded supports \cite{chu1948}. Harrington's synthesis and inversion culture sharpened the distinction between what a complete chart can list and what bounded metal and dielectric can actually drive \cite{harrington2001}. Finite support compresses the radiation operator spectrally; finite probe count compresses it again at the instrument---both limits were implicit in classical antenna theory long before orbital-mode headlines blurred synthesis with recovery \cite{hansen1988}. The historical line converges here on the Volume~I thesis: \emph{finite geometry limits realizable, accessible, and observable angular electromagnetic complexity}, made operational through effective rank, tail energy, and the separation of synthesizable from recoverable sets on declared hardware \cite{pal2026}. Every compact-compact-range measurement record that quotes harmonic length without $(k_0 D_s,\varepsilon,N)$ repeats a mistake the classical masters already warned against \cite{stratton1941}.

% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch40

\begin{physicalvignette}[Standard-gain horn trio for gain transfer]
Consider a standard-gain horn trio used for gain transfer. Technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mouth outline that fixes electrical size before any coefficient table is printed. The first walk-through of the range is tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.

Standing structure in the near zone can mislead the eye---reactive sheets, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the realizability question is posed only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.

Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.

The horn trio makes the laboratory sequence explicit: declare the source region, enforce outgoing selection on the exterior sphere, and only then attach flux-normalized angular labels \cite{stratton1941}.

Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
\end{physicalvignette}

\ref{ch:03} organized the harmonic catalog; \ref{ch:04} isolates a distinction every compact-range engineer already practices but harmonic post-processing rarely reports on its summary header. \emph{Representable} means the coefficient vector is admissible in a complete chart; \emph{synthesizable} means a bounded source on declared support can actually radiate it; \emph{recoverable} means a finite rank-$N$ instrument readout can estimate it to tolerance; \emph{measurable} means the estimate survives calibration on $\Gamma_R$ with declared flux gauge \cite{stratton1941}. Confusing them produces rank gaps and watt failures on $\Gamma_R$. A circular aperture in a ground plane, a pyramidal horn mouth, and a compact planar radiator all radiate through retarded Maxwell export, yet each geometry presents a different electrical size and therefore a different rank ceiling \cite{collin2001}. Measurement adds a second ceiling: a scan sphere with $N$ probes cannot resolve more than $N$ independent far-zone components even when synthesis on the feed succeeds \cite{harrington2001}.

\centering

 \node[ch1box, minimum width=36mm, below=of geo] (rad) {Radiation operator\\$\mathcal{R}_\omega$};
 \node[ch1box, minimum width=36mm, below=of rad] (svd) {Singular-value\\cascade};
 \node[ch1box, minimum width=36mm, below=of svd] (rank) {Effective rank\\$d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$};

\caption{\ref{ch:04} spine: finite geometry launches Maxwell export; compact support compresses the export map into a decaying singular-value ladder; $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ counts geometry-permitted channels above tolerance; only those modes are synthesizable and recoverable on finite instruments \cite{maxwell1873}.}
\label{fig:ch4.master-revision-spine}

Figure~\ref{fig:ch4.master-revision-spine} compresses the laboratory reading order into one chain. Finite source geometry launches export through $\mathcal{R}_\omega$; compact support compresses that map into a decaying singular-value ladder; $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ counts channels above a watt or noise floor; only those channels are both synthesizable on the feed and recoverable on declared instruments \cite{maxwell1873}. The sections follow laboratory order---sources, equivalence, radiation operators, localization, multipole coupling, truncation, null spaces, scaling laws, consequences, and worked examples---without re-proving the harmonic coordinates established in Chapter~\ref{ch:03} \cite{stratton1941}.

\subsection{Realizability and accessibility limits}
\label{sec:ch401}

Consider a $\lambda/10$ disk aperture in an infinite ground plane fed at $10\,\mathrm{GHz}$, measured on a spherical near-field range with $N=32$ dual-polarization probes \cite{stratton1941}. The disk can synthesize a clean main lobe and a structured sidelobe family because its electrical radius $k_{0}a$ permits several low-$\ell$ sectors to carry exported power \cite{chu1948}. The same pattern, sampled on only thirty-two fixed directions, may fail to recover sidelobe detail to the declared tolerance: synthesis succeeded on $\Omega_s$, yet recovery on the instrument did not \cite{devaney2004}. Conversely, a dense near-field grid on a sub-wavelength patch cannot invent radiative channels the patch never coupled, no matter how many probe positions the scanner visits \cite{jackson1999}. Generation on the source support and recovery on the measurement screen are therefore \emph{independent} ceilings on the same Maxwell export \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch401

\begin{physicalvignette}[Open-ended circular waveguide probe on SNF arm]
Consider an open-ended circular waveguide probe on a spherical near-field arm. Frequency, cable loss, and the flange outline that fixes electrical size are logged before the first scan \cite{hansen1988}. Probe standoff and turntable boresight set the measurement geometry against which any later harmonic fit must be judged \cite{harrington2001}.

Realizability asks what a finite feed can radiate; accessibility asks what a finite instrument can recover. On sparse scan spheres the two ceilings differ in every qualified report: synthesis may succeed on $\Omega_s$ while recovery on $N$ probes does not \cite{devaney2004}.

Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
\end{physicalvignette}

Harrington's synthesis culture and Chu's electrical-size counting made the generation--recovery split a laboratory habit long before harmonic post-processing collapsed the two into one headline number \cite{chu1948}. The significance of that picture becomes clearer when coefficients are written down. \emph{$\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$} asks whether a bounded impressed current on declared compact support can populate a target harmonic vector $\mathbf{c}$. \emph{$\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}$} asks whether a finite-rank instrument with $N$ effective degrees of observation can \emph{estimate} that vector to declared tolerance \cite{stratton1941}. Neither question is answered by the length of a harmonic listing: a mode chart that retains $\ell\le 20$ is not evidence of twenty independent radiators on a $\lambda/8$ patch \cite{harrington2001}. Dense spherical near-field grids can resolve reactive sheets near the feed without enlarging $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$; clever feeds cannot recover angular structure a sparse scan never sampled \cite{hansen1988}.

Fix \(\omega>0\) under the \(\exp(-\jj\omega t)\) convention, linear isotropic homogeneous
exterior regions unless stated otherwise, and outgoing radiation condition on
\(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) as in Section~\ref{sec:ch24} \cite{stratton1941}. Let
\(\mathcal{C}:\mathcal{V}\to\mathbb{C}^{I}\) be a mode chart with
\(\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{adm}}\) and coefficient index set \(I\) as in
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.representation-map} \cite{stratton1941}. Impressed currents lie
in the source class \(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\) of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch201}; measurement
screens \(\mathcal{M}\) are finite rank as in Section~\ref{sec:ch29} \cite{harrington2001}. All
field states are discussed on \(\operatorname{ran}\mathcal{R}_\omega\) after application of
\(\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.

For finite \(\Jimp\), the radiative state
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.state-from-source}
\mathfrak{F}
=
\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]
\end{equation}
carries the exported Maxwell pair before any angular label is attached
\cite{stratton1941}. Physically, Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.state-from-source} is the transverse field pair on $\Gamma_R$ once reactive storage has been stripped by outgoing selection: the object a wattmeter integrates, not a coefficient table awaiting a fit \cite{hansen1988}. On a compact range the same state is what probe calibration references after near-to-far transformation \cite{stratton1941}. The mode chart assigns coordinates
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.coefficient-vector}
\mathbf{c}
=
\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{F})
=
\{c_\alpha\}_{\alpha\in I},
\qquad
\mathbf{c}(\Jimp)
=
\mathcal{C}\big(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]\big),
\end{equation}
with each \(c_\alpha\) a linear functional on \(\mathfrak{F}\) once a flux-normalized basis
\(\{\mathbf{f}_\alpha\}\) is fixed in Section~\ref{sec:ch35} \cite{hansen1988}.
The radiation operator fixes \emph{what} radiates; the listing fixes \emph{how} the same object is indexed.
Both subsets below are conditions on \(\mathbf{c}\), not on raw \(\mathcal{S}_\omega\) images \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{def:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set}
The \emph{realizable coefficient set} \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) associated with chart \(\mathcal{C}\) is the set of harmonic vectors a bounded impressed current can actually radiate---not merely list in a complete chart:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
=
\left\{
\mathbf{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{I}:
\exists\,\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_\Omega\ \text{with}\
\mathcal{C}\big(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]\big)=\mathbf{c}
\right\}.
\end{equation}
Physically, \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) is the synthesis ceiling: vectors outside this set may appear in a complete chart yet cannot be driven without enlarging support, changing frequency, or altering the material class \cite{stratton1941}. The limitation originates not from solver tolerance but from compact support and Maxwell coupling on $\Omega_s$ \cite{chu1948}.
\phantomsection\label{def:ch4.accessibility-envelope}
For the same chart, the \emph{accessible sector} \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})\) is the subset of coefficients a finite measurement system with $N$ effective degrees of observation can recover to declared tolerance---the recovery ceiling distinct from synthesis:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.accessibility-envelope}
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})
=
\left\{
\mathbf{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{I}:
\exists\,\text{calibrated estimate}\
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\ \text{with}\
\|\widehat{\mathbf{c}}-\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathcal{M}}\le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}
\right\},
\end{equation}
where \(\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{M}}\) is the estimation norm induced by
\(\mathcal{M}\circ\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{C}\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.measurement-on-coefficients}
and \(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}\) is the declared measurement tolerance
\cite{harrington2001}.
The practical consequence is immediate: observable angular structure is a filtered image of realizable structure, and synthesizable is already narrower than mathematical admissibility on $\mathbb{C}^{I}$ \cite{devaney2004}.
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\VolIFigInclude{ch4-synthesis-$\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}$-sets}
\caption{range membership acts on synthesis: bounded impressed currents map into
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{I}\) through
\(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) and \(\mathcal{C}\). probe rank acts on recovery: finite-rank
\(\mathcal{M}\) defines the envelope \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})\) of
estimated coefficients. The two subsets need not coincide.}
\label{fig:ch4.synthesis-accessibility-sets}
\end{figure}
On a compact range, $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ is what a finite dipole, horn, array, or aperture can actually radiate; $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$ is what a scan sphere with $N$ probes can recover to declared tolerance \cite{stratton1941}. A dish may synthesize sidelobe structure that a sparse range cannot resolve; dense sampling cannot invent channels geometry never coupled \cite{hansen1988}. Design problems seek $\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$; inverse problems seek stable estimates of $\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$ \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.proper-restriction}
For every nontrivial bounded \(\Omega\) and every flux-normalized \(\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{adm}}\)
on outgoing states,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.nested-restriction}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\mathbb{C}^{I},
\qquad
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\mathbb{C}^{I},
\end{equation}
with strict inclusion in both memberships for electrically small or aperture-limited geometries at
fixed \(\omega\) \cite{stratton1941}. Moreover, neither set is determined by
\(\mathcal{C}\) alone: \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}\) depends on \(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\) and
\(\mathcal{R}_\omega\); \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) depends on \(\mathcal{M}\) and the
observable modal sector of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.accessible-modal-sector}
\cite{harrington2001}.
The inclusions Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.nested-restriction} follow from
Definitions~\ref{def:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set} and~\ref{def:ch4.accessibility-envelope}.
Strictness of \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\subsetneq\mathbb{C}^{I}\) follows because
\(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) is rank-limited on bounded \(\Omega\): only a finite-dimensional
sector of modal coefficients carries appreciable weight at fixed \(k_0 a\) when support is compact
\cite{stratton1941}. Strictness of
\(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})\subsetneq\mathbb{C}^{I}\) follows from finite rank
of \(\mathcal{M}\) and the non-reconstructable sets of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch293}
\cite{harrington2001}. Dependence on \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) and \(\mathcal{M}\) is immediate from
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set} and~\eqref{eq:ch4.accessibility-envelope}; dependence
on \(\mathcal{C}\) enters only through the coordinate chart, not through the physics of
\(\operatorname{ran}\mathcal{R}_\omega\) \cite{devaney2004}.
(\ref{prop:ch4.proper-restriction}) is the first quantitative statement of the
central distinction: geometry limits \emph{generation}; rank-$N$ recovery limits
\emph{recovery} \cite{hansen1988}. A coefficient vector may be synthesizable yet
unrecoverable when aperture rank is low; recoverable combinations may include modes that synthesis
cannot excite when geometry forbids the required coupling \cite{harrington2001}. The
remainder of Chapter~4 replaces the rank-limited subsets
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) and \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})\)
with radiation operators, spectral compression laws, and effective electromagnetic dimension on finite
domains.
For a $\lambda/8$ circular aperture in a ground plane, only low $\ell$ sectors carry appreciable exported power;
assigning dominant weight to $\ell=8$ at fixed $k_{0}a$ contradicts the tail decay of
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-tail-energy}, not merely a numerical fit \cite{stratton1941}.
If the same pattern is sampled on a sphere with $N=32$ fixed probes, modes below the noise floor of the
estimation norm are realizable in $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ yet absent from
$\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$ \cite{harrington2001}.
The two subsets need not coincide.
Electromagnetic dimension is not chosen; it is imposed by geometry \cite{chu1948}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch402} records how electrical size $k_0 D_s$ compresses $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ and $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$ spectrally before singular-value ladders are developed in Section~\ref{sec:ch43} \cite{chu1948}.
\subsection{Finite geometry as a spectral constraint}
\label{sec:ch402}
Electrical size $k_0 D_s$ changes when frequency changes even if the mechanical CAD outline is unchanged \cite{jackson1999}. Tail decay and maximum supported $\ell$ track wavelength, not industrial part numbers \cite{hansen1988}. Every rank bound must quote $(\Omega_s,k_0)$ alongside outline drawings; sidelobe shoulders rise when $k_0a$ increases without enlarging support \cite{stratton1941}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch402
\begin{physicalvignette}[Reflector with subreflector and strut scattering]
Consider a prime-focus reflector with subreflector and strut scattering. Electrical size $k_0 D$ rises with dish diameter even when the mechanical CAD outline is unchanged in the drawing office; rank and tail laws track wavelength, not part numbers \cite{jackson1999}. Strut shadows redistribute low-order coupling but cannot assign watts to angular orders the enclosed feed never coupled at the declared frequency \cite{stratton1941}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
\end{physicalvignette}
Spectral compression is the plain-language name for a geometric fact already familiar on ranges: high angular orders carry less exported power when the source is electrically small \cite{jackson1999}. Truncation is therefore imposed by support and wavelength before any solver chooses a cutoff \cite{devaney2004}. Compact support \(\Omega_{s}\) and finite observation apertures do not merely reduce the number of
adjustable feed parameters; they impose a \emph{spectral compression} on both
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) and \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})\)
by collapsing high-\(\ell\) weight before synthesis or recovery is attempted
\cite{stratton1941}. The truncating projector
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.ellmax-truncation-projector} and remainder energy
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy} already separate retained and tail sectors on
\(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\); the next display quantifies how that split propagates into
radiative dimension as a rank law on realizable listings \cite{hansen1988}.
Fix a ball \(B_{a}\) containing \(\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\), wavenumber \(k_{0}=\omega\sqrt{\varepsilon\mu}\),
and a flux-normalized VSH listing with indices \((\ell,m,\sigma)\) as in Section~\ref{sec:ch34}
\cite{stratton1941}. Let \(\ell_{\max}\) denote any finite angular cut and
\(\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\) the coefficient-space projector of
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.ellmax-truncation-projector} \cite{harrington2001}. The \emph{remainder operator} \(\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\) retains angular orders strictly above \(\ell_{\max}\)---the tail sector whose watts Chapter~3 separated in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy} \cite{hansen1988}. Geometry enters through
\(k_{0}a\) and through aperture diameter \(D_{\mathrm{ap}}\) when observation rank is finite
\cite{jackson1999}.
For sources in \(B_{a}\), far-zone coefficients obey accelerated decay when \(\ell\gg k_{0}a\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Tail energy
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.spectral-tail-energy}
\|\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}^{2}
=
\sum_{\ell>\ell_{\max}}\sum_{m,\sigma}|\widehat{c}_{\ell m}^{(\sigma)}|^{2}
\end{equation}
from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy} is therefore not an arbitrary fitting tolerance:
it is the radiative power carried by angular orders the source geometry cannot sustain at the declared
\(k_{0}a\) \cite{hansen1988}. On a $\lambda/10$ disk at $10\,\mathrm{GHz}$, tail watts above $\ell\approx k_0 a$ are already below many declared measurement tolerances before any optimizer is invoked \cite{chu1948}. When \(\ell_{\max}\) is chosen below the geometry-implied
ceiling, \(\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\mathbf{c}\) lies inside \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) but
\(\mathbf{c}\) itself may not---synthesis and listing are misaligned by a predictable tail, not by noise
\cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{def:ch4.spectral-compression}
Let \(\mathbf{c}(\Jimp)=\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp])\). The \emph{spectral compression} of
geometry \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) on listing \(\mathcal{C}\) is the smallest \(\ell_{\max}\) such that
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def}
\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{c}(\Jimp)\|_{\mathrm{rad}}^{2}}
\le \eta_{\mathrm{tail}},
\end{equation}
where \(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}}\) is the declared \emph{tail budget}: the fraction of radiated power permitted to reside beyond the chosen truncation index \(\ell_{\max}\) \cite{hansen1988}, and \(\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{rad}}\) is the radiative
norm of Section~\ref{sec:ch35} \cite{harrington2001}.
Viewed on a compact range, Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def} turns finite support into a quantitative cut rule:
electrical size fixes how many angular orders carry appreciable synthesized power before tail error
dominates design targets \cite{stratton1941}. Compression is geometry acting on rank, not a
post hoc sparse fit \cite{harrington2001}.
When modes order decreasing singular values of the compact source--radiation map,
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.compression-error-bound} bounds
\(\|\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}\) by \(\sigma_{\ell_{\max}+1}\), the first suppressed
singular value \cite{harrington2001}. Coefficient tails and operator tails coincide in the VSH gauge fixed
in Section~\ref{sec:ch35}; changing \(\mathcal{C}\) rotates the tail vector without changing radiated
power on \(\operatorname{ran}\mathcal{R}_\omega\) \cite{devaney2004}. Section~\ref{sec:ch43}
develops the operator-composition form of the same law.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.compressed-synthesis}
For every admissible \(\mathcal{C}\) and finite \(\ell_{\max}\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.compressed-synthesis-set}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(\ell_{\max})}(\mathcal{C})
=
\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\mathbb{C}^{I},
\end{equation}
with strict inclusion on the left when \(\ell_{\max}<\infty\) and nontrivial radiative content above the cut
\cite{hansen1988}. Moreover,
\(\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(\ell_{\max})}(\mathcal{C})\le\dim\operatorname{ran}\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\),
with equality only when synthesis saturates every retained mode \cite{harrington2001}.
The inclusions follow from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set} and linearity of
\(\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\) on radiative dimension \cite{stratton1941}. Strictness follows from
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy} when tail coefficients are nonzero on
\(\operatorname{ran}\mathcal{R}_\omega\) \cite{hansen1988}. The dimension bound is the rank of
\(\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\) on the image of the synthesis map \cite{harrington2001}.
(\ref{prop:ch4.compressed-synthesis}) is the first realizability statement in which geometry
appears as a \emph{radiative dimension} rather than as a boundary condition alone: finite support compresses
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) before aperture or noise enter
\cite{stratton1941}. Sections~\ref{sec:ch43}--\ref{sec:ch46} replace
\(\ell_{\max}\) by singular-value thresholds and effective electromagnetic dimension.
when \(\Omega_{s}=B_{a}\) and \(\mathcal{C}\) is a VSH listing, the tail projector
\(\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\) of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.ellmax-truncation-projector} induces a monotone
family \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(\ell_{\max})}(\mathcal{C})\) nested in \(\ell_{\max}\)
\cite{hansen1988}. For fixed \(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}}\), the smallest admissible cut obeys
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.ellmax-from-tail}
\ell_{\max}(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}},k_{0}a)
\ \sim\
k_{0}a
\mathcal{O}\!\big(\log(1/\eta_{\mathrm{tail}})\big),
\end{equation}
using the engineering scaling of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch362} as a $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ bound
\cite{hansen1988}. Enlarging support
at fixed frequency is the primary mechanism for enlarging \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\):
phase-only retuning on fixed \(a\) cannot manufacture high-\(\ell\) weight forbidden by
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def} \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.support-monotone-enlargement}
If \(a_{1}<a_{2}\) with \(\Omega_{s}^{(j)}=B_{a_{j}}\) and identical \(\mathcal{C}\), then
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(\ell_{\max})}(\mathcal{C};a_{1})
\subseteq
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(\ell_{\max})}(\mathcal{C};a_{2})\)
for every \(\ell_{\max}\) and strict inclusion holds once \(k_{0}a_{2}\) exceeds the bandlimit
threshold of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.duality-bandlimit-scaling} \cite{jackson1999}.
Larger balls admit strictly richer \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) under
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.support-screen}; compactness of \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) on bounded supports yields a
larger radiative rank at fixed electrical size of \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) as \(a\) increases \cite{stratton1941}.
Tail bounds Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} relax in \(k_{0}a\), enlarging the image of
\(\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\) on \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}]\)
\cite{hansen1988}.
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.support-monotone-enlargement} states the geometric converse of spectral compression:
enlarging mechanical support enlarges the nested family of synthesizable patterns when electrical size increases
\cite{stratton1941}. Aperture diameter and enclosing volume enter through the same
\(D_{s}=\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})\) when equivalence reduction of Section~\ref{sec:ch42} is valid
\cite{collin2001}.
\emph{Problem.} A source on \(B_{a}\) with \(k_{0}a=1.2\) must synthesize a VSH pattern whose normalized
coefficients satisfy \(|\widehat{c}_{\ell m}^{(\sigma)}|\ge 0.1\) for all \(\ell\le 6\) and all \(m\).
Is \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\) realizable at \(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}}=10^{-2}\)?
\emph{Step 1.} Estimate \(\ell_{\max}(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}},k_{0}a)\) from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.ellmax-from-tail}:
for \(k_{0}a=1.2\), appreciable weight typically cuts near \(\ell_{\max}\approx 2\)--\(3\) at the declared tail
\emph{Step 2.} Compare to the plateau at \(\ell=6\): Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} suppresses
\(|\widehat{c}_{6m}^{(\sigma)}|\) by several orders relative to \(\ell=1\) on the same ball
\emph{Step 3.} Conclude \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\notin\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) unless
\(a\) is enlarged or \(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}}\) is relaxed; raising \(\ell_{\max}\) in post-processing does not override
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.compressed-synthesis-set}.
Spectral compression is a geometric ceiling on synthesis, not a post-analysis filter.
Jackson's multipole decay laws and Harrington's rank arguments on bounded sources supply the historical spine for Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def}: the tail budget is a watt fraction, not a curve-fit knob . Subsection~\ref{sec:ch403} composes Chapters~\ref{ch:01}--\ref{ch:03} into the synthesis operator $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ before impressed-source admissibility is fixed in Section~\ref{sec:ch41} .
\subsection{Realizability composition}
\label{sec:ch403}
Chapter~3 listed modes on outgoing fields; \ref{ch:04} asks which of those modes receive watts from bounded metal and dielectric \cite{stratton1941}. The harder question is not chart completeness but population: which listed directions are actually excited by impressed currents on $\Omega_s$, and which remain formal indices in a complete basis \cite{harrington2001}? Singular-value cliffs on the actual feed---not the cardinality of a harmonic listing---diagnose rank; tail sectors below the range noise floor may remain unpopulated even when formal coefficients are assigned \cite{devaney2004}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch403
\begin{physicalvignette}[Ceramic-backed planar radiator on a compact ground plane]
Consider a ceramic-backed planar radiator on a compact ground plane. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Chapter~3 organized harmonic coefficients on outgoing fields; the laboratory question here is which of those coefficients receive measurable watts from bounded sources on declared support \cite{stratton1941}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch403} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\ref{ch:01}--\ref{ch:03} fixed admissible Maxwell fields, the radiation operator $\mathcal{R}_\omega$, and
flux-normalized harmonic coordinates \cite{stratton1941}. None of those
results is reproved below: \ref{ch:04} composes them into synthesis rank and measurement recovery on
$\mathbb{C}^{I}$ \cite{devaney2004}. Basis change rotates listings without enlarging $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ \cite{hansen1988}.

The composed operator $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega=\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega$ is the complete synthesis-to-coefficient map laboratories implement: impressed currents excite retarded fields, outgoing projection removes non-radiative storage, and flux-normalized modes assign watt-calibrated coefficients \cite{stratton1941}.
Recall $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega=\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega$: the synthesis operator maps admissible impressed currents $\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$ to harmonic coefficients $\mathbf{c}$ on chart $\mathcal{C}$ \cite{stratton1941}. Radiative rank $r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)$ counts independent far-zone patterns above tolerance $\varepsilon$ on that map; effective rank $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ is its aligned-gauge name \cite{harrington2001}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.gamma-composition-consumed}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega
=
\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega
=
\mathcal{C}\circ\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_\omega,
\end{equation}
with $\mathcal{S}_\omega$ acting on $\mathcal{J}_\Omega$ and $\mathcal{C}$ on $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$
\cite{stratton1941}. Non-radiating components in $\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega$ never appear in
$\mathbf{c}$; calibration applies after synthesis through
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.measurement-on-coefficients} \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.synthesis-not-chart-invariant}
A bounded change of harmonic basis on a fixed radiative sector rotates $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$
but does not enlarge $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ \cite{hansen1988}.
Section~\ref{sec:ch41} opens the impressed-source foundation.
Three threads from \ref{ch:01}--\ref{ch:03} carry forward without re-proof: finite geometry and electrical size fix radiative dimension and truncation ceilings before any coordinate listing is interpreted \cite{chu1948}; admissibility on $\mathbb{C}^{I}$ is not synthesizable on $\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$, and neither equals what a finite instrument can recover \cite{maxwell1873}; the impressed-current-to-measurement sequence records both limits while using the harmonic coordinates established in \ref{ch:03} \cite{harrington2001}.
\section{Admissible Electromagnetic Sources}
A complete representation need not be a realizable representation; a realizable pattern must first be representable---the converse is false \cite{stratton1941}.
\label{sec:ch41}
Section~\ref{sec:ch41} fixes the source-side Maxwellian model every range membership bound presupposes: impressed currents, finite energy, passivity, causality, and compact support before $\mathcal{R}_\omega$ and harmonic charts are applied \cite{stratton1941}.
Every $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ bound begins with a finite impressed current on declared compact support: $\mathcal{D}_J=1$ is a property of sources and apertures, not of coefficient vectors on paper \cite{stratton1941}.
Rank claims trace to $\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$, not harmonic listing length \cite{devaney2004}. Compact planar radiators and compact-range calibrators test Maxwell-closed before multipole headlines \cite{hansen1988}.
\medskip
synthesizable on finite hardware presupposes a source-side Maxwellian model: every
\(\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) must be traceable to impressed data
\(\Jimp\) that satisfy charge continuity, boundary compatibility, finite-energy completion, and
compact support before \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) and \(\mathcal{C}\) are applied
\cite{stratton1941}. Section~\ref{sec:ch41} fixes that model. Subsection~\ref{sec:ch411}
names impressed currents and excitation tuples as the synthesis primitive; Subsection~\ref{sec:ch412}
adds the energy, passivity, causality, and support conditions that separate laboratory-realizable
excitation from formal Maxwell data; Subsection~\ref{sec:ch413} admits sheet and filamentary
channels by distributional extension; Subsection~\ref{sec:ch414} defines the \emph{realizable source
space} \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) whose image under \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\circ\mathcal{C}\) is
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{devaney2004}.

\subsection{Impressed currents and excitation}
\label{sec:ch411}
% CH04-NARR-EXP:04.41
A coax-fed horn and a patch on the same ground plane differ in coupling geometry, yet both reduce to impressed $\Jimp$ on declared compact support before harmonic indices attach \cite{stratton1941}. Rank audits therefore begin with the feed tuple---port, sheet, or volume---not with the length of a modal listing \cite{harrington2001}.
A coax-fed horn and a patch on the same ground plane differ in coupling geometry, yet both reduce to impressed $\Jimp$ on declared compact support before harmonic indices attach \cite{stratton1941}. At this point one may ask what synthesis actually controls: not the length of a harmonic listing, but the feed tuple---port excitation, aperture sheet, or volume current---that Maxwell maps into $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$ \cite{harrington2001}. Amplifiers drive impressed currents; harmonic indices describe export only after outgoing radiation condition and flux normalization \cite{stratton1941}. Every claim in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set} therefore reduces to a physical source on $\Omega_s$: antennas are fed by currents, apertures by equivalent surface densities, and numerical meshes by voxel sources that must still satisfy $\divg\Jimp=-\jj\omega\rho$ in the harmonic convention \cite{stratton1941}.

What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.

Impressed currents are what amplifiers drive on antennas and apertures . Admissibility is finite power, compact support, and passive exterior unless declared otherwise .

Fix \(\omega>0\), domain \(\Omega\Subset\mathbb{R}^{3}\) with boundary \(\Gamma\), and impressed data
in the tuple sense of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.data-specification-tuple} \cite{stratton1941}. Volume
currents lie in \(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\) of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.source-space-refined}; outgoing fields are
discussed only after \(\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\) as in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch201} \cite{harrington2001}.
Harmonic listing \(\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{adm}}\) is fixed thereafter
\cite{hansen1988}.
Laboratory excitation is specified by volume impressed current \(\Jimp\), associated charge density
\(\rho\) linked by harmonic continuity, and boundary data compatible with
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.boundary-data-operator} \cite{stratton1941}. Collect these as
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple}
\mathfrak{E}
=
\left(
\Jimp,\ \rho,\ \mathfrak{g}_\Gamma
\right),
\qquad
\divg\Jimp=-\jj\omega\rho\ \text{on}\ \Omega,
\end{equation}
with \(\mathfrak{g}_\Gamma\) the tangential or impedance data induced on \(\Gamma\) by the feed and
scatterer \cite{harrington2001}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple} is the synthesis-side
specialization of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.data-specification-tuple}: the tuple is admissible when
\(\mathcal{D}_{J}[\Jimp]=1\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.source-admissibility}, but realizability further
requires that the tuple produce nonempty radiative export on \(\operatorname{ran}\mathcal{R}_\omega\)
when a pattern is claimed \cite{stratton1941}.
Define the \emph{synthesis map} from impressed data to radiative states by
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.synthesis-map}
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega:\mathcal{J}_\Omega\to\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}},
\qquad
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega[\Jimp]
=
\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]
=
\mathfrak{F}.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-map} is Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.state-from-source} with the
$\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ interpretation made explicit: \(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega\) is the operator whose
range supplies every field state that finite impressed sources may \emph{generate} in the outgoing
sector \cite{stratton1941}. Composition with \(\mathcal{C}\) yields the coefficient
map
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.coefficient-synthesis-map}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega
=
\mathcal{C}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega:
\mathcal{J}_\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{I},
\qquad
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]=\mathbf{c}(\Jimp),
\end{equation}
The map $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ records synthesis in coefficient space: feed currents map to harmonic coefficients through retarded radiation and flux-normalized reading. Its rank---not the cardinality of the harmonic listing---counts independent far-zone patterns a finite source can populate at tolerance $\varepsilon$ \cite{stratton1941}.
so that Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set} reads
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_\Omega]\) once the
domain is restricted to realizable currents in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch414} \cite{devaney2004}.

\centering

Design specifies \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\); synthesis inverts
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) only on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\)
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch414} \cite{devaney2004}.
Three excitation classes recur in synthesizable analysis:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.excitation-class-volume}
\mathfrak{E}^{(\mathrm{vol})}:
\Jimp\in L^{2}(\Omega_{s})^{3}\ \text{or}\ \HCurl(\Omega),
\quad
\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\subseteq\Omega_{s},
\end{equation}
Collin reminds us that TE/TM bookkeeping in guides is meaningless without first fixing which impressed tuple the amplifier actually drives. \cite{hansen1988}
A smaller MoM matrix does not enlarge radiative degrees of freedom: fewer unknowns can represent the same export cone, not a larger one. \cite{stratton1941}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.excitation-class-aperture}
\mathfrak{E}^{(\mathrm{ap})}:
\mathbf{J}_{s}\ \text{on aperture}\ \Sigma_{a},
\quad
\text{embedded via Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.distributional-source-extension}},
\end{equation}
The significance of the next relation becomes clearer when one imagines a coax-fed horn, a patch on a ground plane, or a filamentary wire on a compact enclosure radiator. Each reduces to $\Jimp$ on $\Omega_s$ before harmonic indices attach. \cite{stratton1941}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.excitation-class-port}
\mathfrak{E}^{(\mathrm{port})}:
\text{guided mode current at feed port}\ \Gamma_{\mathrm{feed}},
\end{equation}
each subject to Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple} and boundary conditions
\cite{stratton1941}. Volume classes feed Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-map}
directly; aperture and port classes enter through equivalence reductions of Section~\ref{sec:ch42}
\cite{harrington2001}. Subsection~\ref{sec:ch411} does not construct equivalent sources---it
records that every claimed \(\mathbf{c}\) must factor through one of these excitation classes.
\phantomsection\label{prin:ch4.excitation-primitive}
\emph{Synthesis--measurement separation.} The set $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ of synthesizable coefficient vectors and the set $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$ of recoverable vectors are both subsets of a complete chart; neither implies the other. Synthesis rank and measurement rank are independent ceilings on the same Maxwell export \cite{stratton1941}.
No coefficient vector \(\mathbf{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{I}\) is physically realizable unless it is the image
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]\) of impressed data \(\Jimp\) in a declared excitation class
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-volume}--\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-port} satisfying
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple} and producing outgoing export on
\(\operatorname{ran}\mathcal{R}_\omega\) \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{prin:ch4.excitation-primitive}) is the source-side mirror of
(\ref{def:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set}): listing without excitation is coordinate fiction
\cite{hansen1988}. Phase-only pattern targets that skip \(\Jimp\) are checked against
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\), not against \(\mathcal{C}\) alone \cite{harrington2001}.
Because \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) and \(\mathcal{C}\) are linear on their domains,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.synthesis-superposition}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\!\left[\sum_{n}a_{n}{\Jimp}_{n}\right]
=
\sum_{n}a_{n}\,\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[{\Jimp}_{n}],
\end{equation}
for admissible \(a_{n}\in\mathbb{C}\) and \({\Jimp}_{n}\in\mathcal{J}_\Omega\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-superposition} is synthesis superposition in a
\emph{fixed} basis; it is not coupling induced by broken symmetry---that topology is owned by
Section~\ref{sec:ch45} \cite{harrington2001}. Near-field reactive superposition of sources can
cancel far-zone export while leaving large local storage; such combinations lie in \(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\)
but may map to \(\mathbf{0}\) under \(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega\) when \(\Jimp\in\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
When \(\rho\equiv0\) on \(\Omega_{s}\), Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple} reduces to
\(\divg\Jimp=0\) and \(\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_\Omega^{(0)}\) of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.charge-neutral-source}
\cite{stratton1941}. Charge-neutral synthesis respects
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.charge-neutral-synthesis}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]\ \text{unchanged under}\ 
\Jimp\mapsto\Jimp+\Jimp^{\mathrm{(NR)}},\ 
\Jimp^{\mathrm{(NR)}}\in\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega,
\end{equation}
so identical \(\mathbf{c}\) may arise from distinct \(\Jimp\) differing by non-radiating additions
\cite{harrington2001}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.charge-neutral-synthesis} is the first
synthesis non-uniqueness law: range membership of \(\mathbf{c}\) does not determine a unique current
distribution \cite{devaney2004}. Inverse design must quotient \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) by
\(\ker\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) developed in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch414}.
on compact \(\Omega_{s}\), \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) is compact on \(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\); hence
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) is compact into \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) when \(I\) is countable
\cite{stratton1941}. For any finite-dimensional truncation \(I_{N}\subset I\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.gamma-fredholm}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\omega,N}
=
\mathcal{P}_{N}\circ\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega:
\mathcal{J}_\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{I_{N}},
\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\omega,N}<\infty,
\end{equation}
with \(\mathcal{P}_{N}\) the coordinate truncation of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch402}
\cite{hansen1988}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.gamma-fredholm} is the operator form of
spectral compression: synthesis is intrinsically finite rank before measurement readouts act
\cite{stratton1941}. Section~\ref{sec:ch43} replaces \(\mathcal{P}_{N}\) by singular-value thresholds on the
full chain \(\Jimp\mapsto\mathfrak{F}\mapsto\mathbf{c}\) \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.synthesis-not-open}
For every compact \(\Omega_{s}\) and admissible \(\mathcal{C}\), \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_\Omega]\)
has empty interior in the standard topology of \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) when \(I\) is infinite; in particular,
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) is nowhere dense in \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Compactness of \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) on bounded sources implies compactness of
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on bounded subsets of \(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\); the image of a compact operator
on the unit ball is precompact, hence cannot contain an open set in an infinite-dimensional radiative dimension
\cite{harrington2001}.
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.synthesis-not-open} is a structural inevitability result: pattern optimization over all
\(\mathbf{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{I}\) optimizes over a set whose interior is empty---feasible targets lie on a thin
set of patterns a source can radiate determined by \(\Omega_{s}\) and \(\omega\) \cite{devaney2004}.
Two compact feeds \({\Jimp}_{1},{\Jimp}_{2}\) on \(\Omega_{s}\) produce
\(\mathbf{c}=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[{\Jimp}_{1}]+\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[{\Jimp}_{2}]\) by
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-superposition} \cite{harrington2001}. 
\emph{Problem.} A design requests \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\) with
\(|\widehat{c}_{\ell m}^{(\sigma)}|=0\) for all \(\ell>2\) at \(k_{0}a=1.5\). Is the target necessarily
realizable on a single compact \(\Omega_{s}\)?
\emph{Step 1.} Compute electrical size \(k_{0}D_{s}/2\) for the proposed \(\Omega_{s}\)
\emph{Step 2.} Compare to spectral compression Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def}: if tail budget
\(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}}\) forces \(\ell_{\max}<2\), \(\ell=2\) content is geometrically suppressed even when
formally allowed in \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\).
\emph{Step 3.} Test \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\in\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}]\) via
(\ref{thm:ch4.synthesis-surjectivity}), not via pattern fitting alone.
Realizable feeds partition into volume, aperture, port, and hybrid classes through
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-volume}--\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-port} .
Each class induces a distinct rank signature on \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\): volume classes typically maximize
\(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) for fixed \(\Omega_{s}\); aperture classes minimize unknowns via equivalence;
port classes constrain \(\mathfrak{E}\) to guided modes . Hybrid tuples superpose per Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-superposition} but remain subject to a single joint \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}\) screen
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.excitation-rank-ordering}
At fixed exterior \(\mathbf{c}\) and equivalence-valid enclosure,
\(\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{aperture}}\le\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{volume}}\)
with equality when (\ref{prop:ch4.aperture-realizability-reduction}) applies
The harmonic continuity and charge linkage is expressed by the requirement that under \(\exp(-\jj\omega t)\), the excitation tuple Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple} requires
\(\divg\Jimp=-\jj\omega\rho\) on \(\Omega\) . Realizable synthesis therefore presupposes a
consistent charge model: aperture or surface currents must be accompanied by the \(\rho\) or \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) jumps
implied by Maxwell continuity . A feed tuple with \(\mathcal{D}_{J}=0\) excludes
\(\mathbf{c}\) before \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) is formed .
A waveguide port supplies \(\mathfrak{g}_\Gamma\) while \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) carries radiation
. Synthesis rank is the minimum of port mode count and aperture MoM rank per
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.channel-count} .
Every admissible feed on a compact range decomposes into volume, surface, and port contributions before harmonic indices attach . Volume excitation $\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{vol}}$ supplies impressed current $\Jimp$ in $\mathcal{J}_\Omega$; surface excitation $\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{surf}}$ supplies equivalent sheet currents $\mathbf{J}_s$ on horn mouths and slots; port excitation $\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{port}}$ supplies guided-mode boundary data $\mathfrak{g}_\Gamma$ at waveguide and coax interfaces . Each channel carries its own synthesizable screen; the joint export $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathfrak{E})]$ is what laboratories must bound jointly, not any single summand in isolation .
Every admissible \(\mathfrak{E}\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple} admits a decomposition
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.excitation-decomposition}
\mathfrak{E}
=
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{vol}}
\oplus
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{surf}}
\oplus
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{port}},
\end{equation}
with each summand carrying its own range membership condition before composition .
Volume terms supply \(\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_\Omega\); surface terms supply \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) through
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding}; port terms supply \(\mathfrak{g}_\Gamma\) from guided-mode catalogs
. The decomposition is not unique, but the \emph{joint} image
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathfrak{E})]\) is unique up to \(\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}\)
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.excitation-class-bound}
For excitation classes Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-volume}--\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-port},
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.excitation-class-rank}
\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C};\mathfrak{E})
\le
\min\big\{
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon),\,
\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}(\mathfrak{E}),\,
|I_{\ell_{\max}}|
\big\},
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{T}(\mathfrak{E})\) the MoM chain Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.discrete-chain-matrix} restricted to the declared class
. Hybrid tuples achieve the bound only when every channel satisfies
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=1\) jointly .
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.channel-count}, (\ref{def:ch4.radiative-rank}), and linearity of
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) .
On a coax-fed horn or patch, the excitation-class bound is the statement that only current distributions in the declared admissible tuple can be driven without violating charge continuity, passivity, or compact support. A denser method-of-moments mesh does not enlarge that class; it only discretizes it . The bound eliminates acausal or unbounded-source claims before harmonic coefficients are discussed .
\subsection{Finite energy, passivity, causality, and support}
\label{sec:ch412}
Every compact-range synthesis claim presupposes four physical screens before harmonic coefficients enter the discussion \cite{stratton1941}. Finite energy on outgoing shells ensures that watt integrals on $\Gamma_R$ converge; passivity excludes net power extraction from passive enclosures; causality ties phasors to retarded time-domain excitation; compact support fixes electrical size $k_0 D_s$ and therefore the rank ceiling developed in Section~\ref{sec:ch402} \cite{maxwell1873}. Passive outlines cannot synthesize patterns requiring forbidden power injection or acausal excitation, no matter how many rows a method-of-moments matrix retains \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch412
\begin{physicalvignette}[Near-field probe on a hexapod alignment stage]
Consider Near-field probe on a hexapod alignment stage. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Passivity and causality exclude unphysical sources that would require net power injection from the exterior . Causality under $\exp(-\jj\omega t)$ selects retarded radiation \cite{jackson1999}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch412} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
The finite-energy condition requires that radiative realizability requires finite energy in the sense of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.energy-class} on
bounded observation shells:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.finite-energy-screen}
\mathcal{D}_{E}[\mathfrak{F}]
=
1
\iff
\sup_{R>R_{0}}
\int_{\Gamma_{R}}
\big(
|\E_{\mathrm{rad}}|^{2}+|\H_{\mathrm{rad}}|^{2}
\big)\,dS
<
\infty,
\end{equation}
with \(\mathfrak{F}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega[\Jimp]\) and \(\Gamma_{R}\) a sphere enclosing
\(\Omega_{s}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.finite-energy-screen}
uses the finite-energy floor of Chapter~1 to synthesis: \(\Jimp\) that excites
non-finite-energy outgoing states cannot produce measurable far patterns even if
\(\mathcal{D}_{J}[\Jimp]=1\) on a bounded domain truncation \cite{stratton1941}. Reactive storage
near \(\Omega_{s}\) may be large while Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.finite-energy-screen} still holds on
\(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) \cite{harrington2001}.
The passivity condition requires that when impressed currents interact with passive boundaries through
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.passive-impedance-class}, $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ requires that no synthesis tuple extract
net power from a passive enclosure:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.passivity-screen}
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}[\mathfrak{E}]
=
1
\iff
\operatorname{Re}\!\int_{\Gamma}
\mathbf{S}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{n}}\,dS
\ge 0
\end{equation}
for all admissible test states generated by \(\mathfrak{E}\) under passive \(\mathcal{B}_\Gamma\)
\cite{harrington2001}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.passivity-screen} excludes active
gain media and negative-index embeddings from the default realizable radiative class; such structures may
be treated as extended tuples with explicit power injection channels \cite{harrington2001}. Passive
conditions do not restrict \(\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega\): non-radiating passive currents remain in
\(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\) but map to \(\mathbf{0}\) under \(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
The causality condition requires that harmonic phasors \(\Jimp(\mathbf{r})\) are realizable only if they are the \(\exp(-\jj\omega t)\)
specialization of a retarded time-domain current \(j(\mathbf{r},t)\) supported on \(t'\le t\) in
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.causality-retarded} \cite{stratton1941}. Define
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.causality-screen}
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{caus}}[\Jimp]
=
1
\iff
\operatorname{supp}_{t}\,j(\cdot,t)\subseteq(-\infty,t],
\end{equation}
equivalently, no advanced Green convolution contributes to \(\E(\mathbf{r},t)\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.causality-screen} is invisible in pure frequency-domain
pattern formulas yet decisive for pulsed and wideband synthesizable: spectra of \(\Jimp\) must be
consistent with a causal time support \cite{jackson1999}.
The compact-support condition requires that spectral compression of Section~\ref{sec:ch402} presupposes compact \(\Omega_{s}\):
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.support-screen}
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}[\Jimp]
=
1
\iff
\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\subseteq\Omega_{s},\
\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})=D_{s}<\infty.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.support-screen} ties range membership to electrical size \(k_{0}D_{s}/2\): the
set of patterns a source can radiate \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) acquires tail decay laws from
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch402} only when Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.support-screen} holds
\cite{stratton1941}. Extended apertures with \(\Omega_{s}\) growing with
frequency require explicit re-declaration of \(D_{s}(\omega)\) \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{def:ch4.realizability-screen}
The synthesizable condition is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.range membership-screen}
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}[\Jimp]
=
1
\iff
\left(
\mathcal{D}_{J}[\Jimp]=1\
\wedge\
\mathcal{D}_{E}[\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega[\Jimp]]=1\
\wedge\
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}[\mathfrak{E}]=1\
\wedge\
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{caus}}[\Jimp]=1\
\wedge\
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}[\Jimp]=1
\right)
\end{equation}
Each indicator in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizability-screen} corresponds to a familiar laboratory error
\cite{harrington2001}.
Infinite-energy violations correspond to nonphysical idealized sources requiring unbounded radiated power on
finite observation shells \cite{stratton1941}.
Passivity violations correspond to hidden gain mechanisms or active enclosures masquerading as passive radiators
\cite{harrington2001}.
Causality violations correspond to responses that precede excitation in the time domain \cite{stratton1941}.
Support violations correspond to radiators whose assumed spatial extent grows without bound while a fixed
frequency is quoted \cite{hansen1988}.
These conditions do not create new electromagnetics; they state the physical assumptions already implicit in
every antenna range measurement on a finite scan sphere \cite{harrington2001}.
Definition~\ref{def:ch4.realizability-screen} is the source-side parallel to
Definition~\ref{def:ch3.R-adm} on the representation side: \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=1\) is necessary
for \(\mathbf{c}(\Jimp)\) to enter \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) with physical meaning
\cite{stratton1941}. Violating any requirement leaves a formal Maxwell solution that cannot be
implemented by finite hardware in the declared model class \cite{harrington2001}.

\centering

 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of de] (dp) {$\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of dp] (dc) {$\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{caus}}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of dc] (ds) {$\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}$};

 \draw[ch1flow] (dj.east) -- (dj.east |- dr.west);

\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Subsection~\ref{sec:ch412} $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ condition: five physical requirements compose
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizability-screen}.}
\label{fig:ch4.range membership-screen}
\end{figure}
\(\mathcal{D}_{J}\) is inherited from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.source-admissibility}; the remaining four
clauses are the new $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ content of Section~\ref{sec:ch41} \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.condition-independence}
The requirements in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizability-screen} are logically independent: for each single
requirement \(X\in\{E,\mathrm{pass},\mathrm{caus},\mathrm{supp}\}\), there exist tuples satisfying all
other requirements with \(\mathcal{D}_{X}=0\) while \(\mathcal{D}_{J}=1\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\(\mathcal{D}_{E}=0\) on formal outgoing solutions with divergent shell energy;
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}=0\) with active embedding;
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{caus}}=0\) with advanced-time spectra;
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}=0\) with spatially extended non-compact models---each while local
Maxwell residuals may still hold on truncated domains \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{prop:ch4.condition-independence}) justifies treating range membership as a conjunction rather
than a restatement of \(\mathcal{D}_{J}\): laboratories fail on distinct requirements, and synthesis checks must
test each condition relevant to the claimed \(\mathbf{c}\) \cite{devaney2004}.
The radiated-power linkage is expressed by the requirement that finite-energy export is equivalent, on \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\), to finite radiated power through the
far-sphere power condition Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.radiated-power-screen} \cite{stratton1941}. For
\(\mathfrak{F}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega[\Jimp]\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.power-energy-equivalence}
\mathcal{D}_{E}[\mathfrak{F}]=1
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
P_{\mathrm{rad}}
=
\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{Re}\!\oint_{\Gamma_{R}}
\mathbf{S}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{n}}\,dS
<
\infty,
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{S}=\tfrac{1}{2}\E\times\H^{*}\) the harmonic Poynting vector \cite{poynting1884}.
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.power-energy-equivalence} connects \(\mathcal{D}_{E}\) to watt meters used in
laboratory recovery: a synthesis tuple with divergent shell energy cannot yield a finite \(P_{\mathrm{rad}}\)
report compatible with Section~\ref{sec:ch35} normalization \cite{hansen1988}.
Let \(\widetilde{\Jimp}(\mathbf{r},\omega)\) denote the temporal Fourier transform of \(j(\mathbf{r},t)\). Causality
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.causality-screen} requires \(\widetilde{\Jimp}\) to be the boundary value of a function analytic
in the lower half-plane \(\operatorname{Im}\omega<0\) under the \(\exp(-\jj\omega t)\) convention
\cite{stratton1941}. Narrowband
antennas at fixed \(\omega\) satisfy Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.causality-screen} automatically when fed by causal
time-domain voltages \cite{collin2001}.
Define the dimensionless electrical size
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.electrical-size}
\Xi
=
\frac{k_{0}D_{s}}{2\pi},
\end{equation}
Recall that electrical size $\Xi=k_0 D_s/(2\pi)$ is the dimensionless parameter that controls on how many angular orders a compact source can excite with appreciable far-zone power. Electrically small radiators cannot realize fine angular spectra regardless of feed complexity displayed in a method-of-moments mesh \cite{chu1948}.
with \(D_{s}\) from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.support-screen} \cite{stratton1941}. Realizable synthesis at
fixed \(\Xi\ll1\) concentrates \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}]\) in low-\(\ell\) sectors;
raising \(\Xi\) enlarges the accessible export pattern cone before truncation and measurement further compress it
\cite{hansen1988}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.electrical-size} is the single parameter that links
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch412} support conditions to Subsection~\ref{sec:ch402} spectral compression.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.condition-failures}
Each failure \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=0\) produces a distinct laboratory signature:
\(\mathcal{D}_{E}=0\) yields divergent far-sphere integrals;
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}=0\) yields negative net power through passive \(\Gamma\);
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{caus}}=0\) yields advanced-time response in transient replay;
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}=0\) removes tail decay laws of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def}
\cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{lem:ch4.condition-failures}) is diagnostic, not constructive: identifying which clause failed prevents
misattributing a $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ obstruction to measurement noise or basis choice \cite{devaney2004}.
A source inside a passive cavity with \(\mathcal{B}_\Gamma\) satisfying
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.passive-impedance-class} cannot sustain \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}=0\) unless external
power is injected through a port counted in \(\mathfrak{E}\) \cite{harrington2001}. Synthesis claims
for \(\mathbf{c}\) that require net outward power exceeding feed capability fail
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.passivity-screen} before \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) is inverted \cite{stratton1941}.
The joint condition Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizability-screen} factors as a conjunction of physical conditions:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.condition-factorization}
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}
=
\mathcal{D}_{E}\cdot\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}\cdot\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{caus}}\cdot\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}
\cdot\mathcal{D}_{J}\cdot\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}},
\end{equation}
with \(\mathcal{D}_{J}\) from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.source-admissibility} and \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}\) from
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-admissibility} when surface channels are present \cite{stratton1941}.
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.condition-factorization} is the bound relations for \(\mathfrak{E}\): a single failed factor
excludes \(\mathbf{c}\) from \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) regardless of pattern fit quality
\cite{devaney2004}. (\ref{lem:ch4.condition-failures}) assigns laboratory signatures to each zero factor
\cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.condition-composition}
\(\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) if and only if \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=1\) on the excitation tuple
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple} \cite{stratton1941}. Moreover,
\(\mathbf{c}=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) only if
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=1\) and \(\mathbf{r}_{\min}=\mathbf{0}\) in (\ref{prin:ch4.operator-composition})
\cite{devaney2004}.
Forward: definitions of \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.range membership-screen}
\cite{stratton1941}. Converse for \(\mathbf{c}\): operator-composition range condition
\cite{harrington2001}.
A non-causal \(\widetilde{\Jimp}(\omega)\) may produce a attractive far-zone template at one frequency yet fail
wideband pulse synthesis when replayed in time \cite{jackson1999}. \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{caus}}=0\)
therefore excludes the tuple before \(\ell_{\max}\) or MoM rank is discussed \cite{harrington2001}.
Conditions multiply in the $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ tuple:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.condition-composition-algebra}
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}
=
\mathcal{D}_{E}\,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}\,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{caus}}\,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}\,
\mathcal{D}_{J}\,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}},
\end{equation}
with each factor binary on the declared domain \cite{stratton1941}. Failure of any factor excludes
\(\mathbf{c}\) before \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) is evaluated \cite{devaney2004}. The algebra is not commutative in
diagnostics: a causality failure may mask a support failure unless conditions are tested in the order
energy \(\rightarrow\) passivity \(\rightarrow\) causality \(\rightarrow\) support \(\rightarrow\) continuity \(\rightarrow\) coupling
\cite{harrington2001}.
Composition of charge-continuous conditions mirrors how a range engineer checks a campaign: continuity and finite power first, outgoing selection second, mode chart last \cite{stratton1941}. Violating the order---for example, reading $\ell$ labels on reactive near-field data---produces coefficients that fail watt consistency on $\Gamma_R$ \cite{jackson1999}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.passivity-finite-energy}
On harmonic tuples with \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}=1\), \(\mathcal{D}_{E}=1\) follows for bounded port power
\(P_{\mathrm{feed}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Conversely, \(\mathcal{D}_{E}=1\) does not imply
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}=1\) when active injection is undeclared \cite{harrington2001}.
\subsection{Distributional sources}
\label{sec:ch413}
Sheet and filamentary extensions cover apertures and wires without changing radiative rank on fixed support \cite{stratton1941}. Distributional sources are bookkeeping on $\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$ \cite{devaney2004}.
\medskip
Surface excitation pairs with Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-aperture} through
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch413
\begin{physicalvignette}[Compact range feed with quad-ridged horn]
Consider Compact range feed with quad-ridged horn. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Thin slots and rim currents are idealizations of finite width . Distributional sources are admissible when power and support remain physical .
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch413} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding}
\langle\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\rangle
=
\int_{\Omega_{s}}\Jimp\cdot\boldsymbol{\phi}\,d^{3}r
\sum_{a}\int_{\Sigma_{a}}\mathbf{J}_{s,a}\cdot\boldsymbol{\phi}\,dS,
\end{equation}
extending Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.distributional-source-extension} to the realizability tuple
\(\mathfrak{E}\) of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple} \cite{stratton1941}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding} is how aperture feeds enter \(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega\): the
dyadic convolution of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch231} is evaluated on \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\) with
finite-part prescription at coincidence \cite{harrington2001}. Tangential \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\)
replaces volume divergence constraints on \(\Sigma\) by surface continuity of normal \(\D\) and
tangential \(\H\) jumps \cite{stratton1941}.
Coupling admissibility. Not every square-integrable \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) produces stable synthesis: near coincidence,
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.dyadic-singular-expansion} demands regularized self-terms. Define
\emph{coupling $\mathcal{D}_J=1$} by
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.coupling-admissibility}
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}[\mathbf{J}_{s}]
=
1
\iff
\int_{\Sigma}\!\int_{\Sigma}
\big|\overline{\mathbf{G}}_{E,0}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{r}')\cdot\mathbf{J}_{s}(\mathbf{r}')\big|^{2}\,dS\,dS'
<
\infty
\end{equation}
after static--dynamic split and finite-part extraction as in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch232}
\cite{harrington2001}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-charge-continuous} is the surface
analog of \(\HCurl\) control for volume sources: it establishes that \(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega\) is
Fredholm-stable on the declared mesh or patch basis \cite{harrington2001}. Filamentary currents
\(\mathbf{J}_{f}\) on curves \(\mathcal{C}_{f}\) replace \(dS\) by \(dl\) with line finite parts
\cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.distributional-realizability}
Let \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\) be defined by Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding} with
\(\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_\Omega\), \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}[\mathbf{J}_{s}]=1\), and
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=1\) on the combined tuple. Then \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\) generates
\(\mathfrak{F}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) and
\(\mathbf{c}=\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{F})\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Conversely, every \(\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) arising from aperture-class
excitation Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-aperture} admits a distributional representation
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding} with \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) after equivalence
reduction of Section~\ref{sec:ch42} \cite{collin2001}.
Forward direction: Subsection~\ref{sec:ch232} makes \(\mathcal{S}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}]\) well-defined;
\(\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\) yields outgoing \(\mathfrak{F}\); \(\mathcal{C}\) lists \(\mathbf{c}\) by
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coefficient-synthesis-map} \cite{stratton1941}. Converse: aperture fields
admit equivalent surface currents by Section~\ref{sec:ch42}; \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) is the
regularity hypothesis under which equivalence preserves \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\)
\cite{collin2001}.
(\ref{prop:ch4.distributional-realizability}) coins \emph{coupling $\mathcal{D}_J=1$} as the surface
range membership bound: without \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\), MoM or Huygens chains return
formal coefficients that are not synthesis-stable \cite{harrington2001}. Volume and surface contributions
superpose linearly in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding} and in
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-superposition} \cite{stratton1941}.
Discretize \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) on patches \(\{S_{p}\}_{p=1}^{P}\) with Rao--Wilton--Glisson or pulse bases
\(\{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{p}\}\) \cite{harrington2001}. The discrete synthesis map is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.mom-synthesis-matrix}
\mathbf{c}
=
\mathbf{T}\,\mathbf{I}_{s},
\qquad
T_{\alpha p}
=
\mathcal{C}\Big[\mathcal{R}_\omega\big[\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{p}\big]\Big]_{\alpha},
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{I}_{s}\in\mathbb{C}^{P}\) patch currents and \(\mathbf{T}\in\mathbb{C}^{|I|\times P}\)
\cite{harrington2001}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.mom-synthesis-matrix} is the matrix form of
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on aperture channels; \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) requires
\(\mathbf{T}\) to be Fredholm-stable after self-term regularization of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch232}
\cite{stratton1941}. Ill-conditioned \(\mathbf{T}\) signals coupling inadmissibility, not merely
numerical inconvenience \cite{harrington2001}.
Static--dynamic split at coincidence. Following Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.dyadic-singular-expansion}, write the patch self-term as
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.static-dynamic-split}
\overline{\mathbf{G}}_{E,0}
=
\overline{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathrm{sing}}+\overline{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathrm{cpt}},
\qquad
\overline{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathrm{sing}}\ \text{extracted at}\ k_{0}=0,
\end{equation}
with \(\overline{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathrm{cpt}}\) integrable on each patch \cite{harrington2001}.
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.static-dynamic-split} is the $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$-preserving regularization: \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\)
holds when \(\overline{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathrm{cpt}}\) dominates the MoM impedance matrix after static subtraction
\cite{harrington2001}. Omitting \(\overline{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathrm{sing}}\) produces formal \(\mathbf{c}\) that are not
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\)-stable under mesh refinement \cite{devaney2004}.
A rectangular aperture \(\Sigma_{a}\) carrying tangential \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) in the TE\(_{10}\) port mode of a
waveguide excites a definable \(\mathfrak{F}\) once \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) on the patch
\cite{collin2001}. 
\emph{Problem.} A MoM mesh on \(\Sigma_{a}\) uses \(P=50\) patches; a design targets \(|I|=200\) modal
coefficients above \(\ell_{\max}=8\). Can \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\) be realizable?
\emph{Step 1.} Form \(\mathbf{T}\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mom-synthesis-matrix} after self-term regularization
\emph{Step 2.} Compute \(\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}\le\min(P,|I|)=50\): at most fifty independent
synthesis directions are available on the mesh.
\emph{Step 3.} If \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\) requires more than fifty independent components, enlarge
\(\Sigma_{a}\), refine patches, or lower \(\ell_{\max}\).
Filamentary and magnetic distributional channels. Line currents \(\mathbf{J}_{f}\) on curves \(\mathcal{C}_{f}\subset\Omega\) embed through
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.filament-embedding}
\langle\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\rangle
\supseteq
\int_{\mathcal{C}_{f}}\mathbf{J}_{f}\cdot\boldsymbol{\phi}\,dl,
\end{equation}
with line finite parts as in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch232} . Magnetic sheets
\(\mathbf{M}_{s}\) on \(\Sigma_{m}\) enter the same tuple \(\mathfrak{E}\) when \(\E_{t}\) is discontinuous across
\(\Sigma_{m}\) . ~\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding} .
