Under Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-superposition}, each channel contributes to \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) only when
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) on its support class . A formally invertible MoM
matrix on surface patches alone does not establish volume--filament combinations unless the combined embedding satisfies
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-charge-continuous} .
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.distributional-rank}
Let \(P_{v},P_{s},P_{f}\) be stable degrees of freedom on volume, surface, and filament discretizations. Then
\(\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}\le P_{v}+P_{s}+P_{f}\) and
\(\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\le r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) regardless of patch count
A center-fed dipole filament through a patch array contributes primarily \(\ell=1\) content when
\(k_{0}L_{\mathrm{fil}}\ll1\) . Adding high-\(\ell\) targets without enlarging
\(\mathcal{C}_{f}\) or \(\Sigma_{a}\) violates coupling $\mathcal{D}_J=1$ before \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) is tested
Multi-scale distributional embedding. Laboratory feeds often combine volume, surface, and filament channels at disparate scales. Define the
\emph{multi-scale tuple}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.multiscale-embedding}
\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}
=
\Jimp\,\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{s}}
\sum_{a}\mathbf{J}_{s,a}\,\delta_{\Sigma_{a}}
\sum_{f}\mathbf{J}_{f,f}\,\delta_{\mathcal{C}_{f}},
\end{equation}
with each channel tested by Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-admissibility} on its support class
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.multiscale-realizability}
Let \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\) be Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.multiscale-embedding} with each channel satisfying
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=1\) individually. Then \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) if and
only if the combined Green self-energy integral in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-charge-continuous} is finite on the union
support and \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}=1\) . Moreover,
\(\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}\le P_{v}+P_{s}+P_{f}\) with equality only when cross-channel couplings are linearly
independent in \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) .
Linearity of \(\mathcal{S}_\omega\) on distributional test classes ; rank bound from
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.distributional-rank} .
\subsection{Realizable source spaces}
\label{sec:ch414}
Realizable source spaces describe what feeds can explore, not what a complete chart lists \cite{harrington2001}.
A method-of-moments mesh on a compact planar radiator at $2.4\,\mathrm{GHz}$ may list thousands of unknowns, yet the spherical near-field report can stabilize on only a handful of export directions above the range noise floor \cite{hansen1988}.
$\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$ maps to $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ through $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ \cite{devaney2004}. MoM refinement discretizes the same operator; SVD cliffs diagnose rank \cite{hansen1988}.
\medskip
The set of patterns a source can radiate \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) is the image of a source manifold, not an
abstract convex body in \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) \cite{devaney2004}. Subsection~\ref{sec:ch414} defines that
manifold---the \emph{realizable source space}---and proves that \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) maps it onto
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) with explicit domain qualifiers, Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizability-screen}, and distributional extensions
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding} \cite{stratton1941}. Excitation classes
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-volume}--\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-port} are the admissible
channels; equivalence reductions of Section~\ref{sec:ch42} may replace port data by aperture equivalents
\cite{collin2001}.
\phantomsection\label{def:ch4.realizable-source-space}
The \emph{realizable source space} is
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch414
\begin{physicalvignette}[Aperture-coupled patch behind a superstrate]
Consider Aperture-coupled patch behind a superstrate. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Realizable source spaces describe what feeds can explore, not what a complete chart lists . Mesh size is not rank .
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch414} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.realizable-source-space}
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}
=
\left\{
\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}:
\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\ \text{satisfies Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding}--\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-admissibility}},\
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}]=1
\right\}.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizable-source-space} is the source-domain preimage of
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\): every realizable coefficient vector factors through
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. The space is not a Hilbert space in
full generality---it is a union of volume \(\HCurl\) classes, distributional sheets, and port-reduced
equivalents---yet \(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega\) acts linearly on each channel
\cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.synthesis-surjectivity}
With \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega=\mathcal{C}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega\) from
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coefficient-synthesis-map},
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.synthesis-surjectivity}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
=
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\big[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\big],
\qquad
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\subseteq\mathcal{J}_\Omega\ \text{(distributional union)}.
\end{equation}
Surjectivity onto the realizable set means every physically reachable coefficient vector admits at least one admissible impressed current on the declared class---not that every chart index is reachable. The distinction is the difference between ``can be synthesized in principle on this hardware'' and ``appears in a complete mode chart'' \cite{devaney2004}.
Moreover, for \(\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\), there exists
\(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) with
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}]=\mathbf{c}\), unique only up to
\(\ker\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{def:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set}) gives
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})=\{\mathbf{c}:\exists\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_\Omega,\
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp])=\mathbf{c}\}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Definitions
\ref{def:ch4.realizability-screen} and~\ref{def:ch4.realizable-source-space} restrict \(\Jimp\) to
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) without shrinking \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on
physical channels: every satisfying tuple in the restricted class maps to the same \(\mathbf{c}\)
\cite{harrington2001}. Surjectivity is definitional; non-uniqueness follows from
\(\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega\) and representation non-injectivity on finite cuts
\cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.synthesis-surjectivity}) is the source-side completion of Section~\ref{sec:ch41}: synthesizable
is the geometry of \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) and its image under \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\), not a
property of \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) alone \cite{hansen1988}. Inverse synthesis seeks
\(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) with
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}]=\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\); non-uniqueness is
budgeted by \(\ker\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) and by spectral compression of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch402}
\cite{devaney2004}.
Operator-domain form. Restricting \(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega\) to \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.restricted-synthesis-operator}
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega^{(\mathrm{real})}:
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\to\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}},
\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega|_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}}
\le
\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(\ell_{\max})}(\mathcal{C})
\end{equation}
for any finite angular cut \(\ell_{\max}\) from (\ref{prop:ch4.compressed-synthesis})
\cite{hansen1988}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.restricted-synthesis-operator} introduces
\emph{radiative dimension} of Section~\ref{sec:ch43}: \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) is infinite-dimensional as
a set, yet \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) compresses it to a finite radiative rank at fixed electrical size at fixed geometry and
\(\omega\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Null-space quotient. Define the synthesis null space
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.synthesis-null-space}
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}
=
\ker\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega
=
\left\{
\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}:
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}]=\mathbf{0}
\right\}.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-null-space} contains \(\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega\cap\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) and
any representation-blind currents that produce vanishing coefficients in \(\mathcal{C}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Realizable synthesis is uniquely defined on the quotient \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}/\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}\)
\cite{devaney2004}. Inverse problems regularize on this quotient, not on raw patch currents
\cite{harrington2001}.
Surjectivity onto the realizable set means every physically reachable coefficient vector admits at least one admissible impressed current on the declared class---not that every chart index is reachable \cite{devaney2004}. In antenna terms: synthesis may target a pattern in coefficient space, but membership in $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ is decided by bounded metal and dielectric, not by listing width \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.channel-count}
Let \(P\) be the number of \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}\)-stable MoM degrees of freedom on \(\Sigma_{a}\) and
\(N\) the measurement rank of \(\mathcal{M}\). Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.channel-count-bound}
\dim\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}]
\le
\min\big(P,\,N,\,|I_{\ell_{\max}}|\big),
\end{equation}
with \(|I_{\ell_{\max}}|\) the number of indices retained by \(\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\)
\cite{hansen1988}.
MoM rank bounds synthesis degrees of freedom by \(P\); (\ref{prop:ch4.compressed-synthesis}) bounds by
\(|I_{\ell_{\max}}|\); accessible sector (\ref{def:ch4.accessibility-envelope}) bounds recoverable
coordinates by \(N\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.channel-count} is the first joint bound on synthesis and recovery: range membership and
$\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}$ compress \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) from opposite sides of Figure~\ref{fig:ch4.synthesis-accessibility-sets}
\cite{devaney2004}. Section~\ref{sec:ch46} replaces the min bound by effective electromagnetic dimension.
For \(k_{0}D_{s}\ll1\), \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) on a single compact patch is well approximated by
three independent dipole components in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-volume}
\cite{jackson1999}. \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) then has dimension at most
three in any basis that resolves only \(\ell=1\) content; targeting \(\ell>1\) coefficients without enlarging
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) fails (\ref{thm:ch4.synthesis-surjectivity})
\cite{hansen1988}.
Topology of \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\). \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) is not a linear subspace of \(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\): passive constraints and
coupling $\mathcal{D}_J=1$ are nonlinear in the sense that \({\Jimp}_{1}+{\Jimp}_{2}\) may fail
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) even when each summand passes \cite{harrington2001}. Synthesis remains
linear on \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) through Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.eigenfield-superposition}, but the admissible
\emph{set} \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) is a constrained manifold whose tangent directions are the exportable
degrees of freedom counted by \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) \cite{devaney2004}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.synthesis-manifold-chart}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
=
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\big(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\big)
\subseteq
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\big(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\big)
\subseteq
\mathbb{C}^{I},
\end{equation}
with strict inclusions on compact \(\Omega_{s}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-manifold-chart} is the coordinate picture for pattern synthesis: optimization must occur on
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\), not on \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) \cite{devaney2004}.

when \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pass}}=0\) by design, \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) must include explicit port power
channels in \(\mathfrak{E}\) \cite{harrington2001}. Loss modifies \(\sigma_{n}\) tails without removing
the manifold chart Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-manifold-chart}: $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ remains
\(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on the declared class \cite{stratton1941}.
At \({\Jimp}_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\), define the tangent export cone
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.tangent-export-cone}
\mathcal{T}_{{\Jimp}_{0}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}
=
\overline{\big\{
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\delta\Jimp]:
\delta\Jimp\ \text{admissible first variation at}\ {\Jimp}_{0}
\big\}},
\end{equation}
with membership in \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Linearization of pattern synthesis is
well-posed only on \(\mathcal{T}_{{\Jimp}_{0}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\), not on full \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\)
\cite{hansen1988}. The cone dimension is bounded by \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) at tolerance \(\varepsilon\)
\cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.tangent-dimension}
\(\dim\mathcal{T}_{{\Jimp}_{0}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\le r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) for every
\({\Jimp}_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) and aligned gauge \cite{stratton1941}. Strict
inequality holds on rank-deficient feeds where \(\sigma_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega)<\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\) for
\(n>r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) \cite{hansen1988}.
\subsection{Small electric dipole: source to finite rank}
\label{sec:ch41.signature-dipole}
The electrically small dipole is the lowest-rank radiator and the elementary limit for every truncation claim \cite{jackson1999}.
Horn mouths, slot arrays, and reflector-equivalent surfaces are standard equivalence laboratories \cite{collin2001}.
The electrically small dipole is the lowest-rank radiator and the elementary limit for every truncation claim \cite{jackson1999}.
The Hertzian dipole is the canonical physical chain for Section~\ref{sec:ch41}:
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch41.signature-dipole
\begin{physicalvignette}[Monopole on a vehicle roof ground plane]
Consider Monopole on a vehicle roof ground plane. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
The electrically small dipole is the lowest-rank radiator and the sanity check for every truncation claim . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch41.signature-dipole} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.signature-dipole-chain}
\Jimp(\mathbf{r})=\mathbf{p}\,\delta(\mathbf{r})
\ \xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{R}_\omega\ }\
\mathfrak{F}
\ \xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{C}\ }\
\{\widehat{c}_{\ell m}^{(\sigma)}\}
\ \xrightarrow{\ \text{rank}\ }\
r_{\mathrm{rad}}\approx 3.
\end{equation}
For $k_0 a\ll 1$ on support ball $B_a$, only $\ell=1$ multipoles carry appreciable exported power; the coefficient vector is effectively three-dimensional in the $\ell=1$ block \cite{jackson1999}.
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.signature-dipole-chain} anchors rank-one sanity: moment, export, VSH chart, $r_{\mathrm{rad}}\approx 3$ \cite{jackson1999}. Multipole tables on sub-wavelength wire list expansion sets, not export above tolerance \cite{devaney2004}.
\medskip
 Remember this geometry: a point dipole does not populate a complete harmonic chart---it occupies a low-rank slice of $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}]$ \cite{harrington2001}.

\section{Equivalence Principles and Apertures}
\label{sec:ch42}
Closing Section~\ref{sec:ch41}, carry three laboratory facts forward: impressed currents are what amplifiers drive; admissibility shrinks $\mathcal{J}_\Omega$ to hardware-realizable classes; and the electrically small dipole remains the rank-one elementary limit before any multipole table is trusted \cite{stratton1941}. Equivalence is a bookkeeping change---engineers replace volume currents by surface distributions on horns, slots, and apertures while preserving the same exterior radiation \cite{stratton1941}. Because $\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]$ is unchanged, $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ is unchanged; equivalence shrinks the inversion domain without enlarging radiative rank \cite{devaney2004}. Stratton--Chu and Love reductions preserve $\mathbf{c}=\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]$ when Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.exterior-equivalence-condition} holds \cite{collin2001}.
Subsections~\ref{sec:ch421}--\ref{sec:ch424} develop volume--surface equivalence, aperture sources, tangential continuity, and the limits under which equivalence fails to preserve $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ \cite{stratton1941}. Horn mouths, slot arrays, and reflector-equivalent surfaces are standard equivalence laboratories where the same far-zone pattern is recovered from different surface descriptions \cite{collin2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch42
\begin{physicalvignette}[Inverted-F antenna on a PCB edge]
Consider Inverted-F antenna on a PCB edge. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch42} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\subsection{Canonical apertures: rectangular waveguide and circular disk}
\label{sec:ch42.signature-apertures}
Waveguide and disk apertures are the canonical laboratory geometries for mode counting \cite{stratton1941}. Compare them to open-region disks before importing closed-mode indices from metal walls: a $TE_{10}$ mouth and a circular disk in a ground plane already display how electrical diameter sets $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ before azimuthal labels are assigned \cite{jackson1999}.
A $TE_{10}$ mouth of width $a$ and height $b$ in a conducting guide radiates through an equivalent magnetic surface current on the open face $\Sigma_a$ \cite{collin2001}. The dominant export is a single lobed pattern; higher guide modes enter only when $k_0 b$ and aspect ratio permit additional propagating orders \cite{harrington2001}. Equivalence replaces the interior feed by $\mathbf{J}_s,\mathbf{M}_s$ on $\Sigma_a$ without changing $\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]$ in the exterior \cite{stratton1941}.
A circular disk of radius $a$ in an infinite ground plane radiates with axisymmetric $\ell=1$ content at $k_0 a\ll 1$ and develops higher $\ell$ only as $k_0 a$ increases \cite{stratton1941}. Electrical diameter sets $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ before any azimuthal index is specified \cite{harrington2001}.
Guide mouths and ground-plane disks are the standard aperture pair for mode-count comparisons \cite{stratton1941}. Closed-guide indices must not enter open-range $\ell$ tables without a chart-transformation record \cite{hansen1988}.
\medskip
 These two geometries---rectangular guide mouth and circular disk---are the standard Stratton--Harrington aperture pair every reader already knows \cite{stratton1941}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch42.signature-apertures
\begin{physicalvignette}[Vivaldi notch array on a printed circuit board]
Consider Vivaldi notch array on a printed circuit board. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Waveguide and disk apertures are the canonical laboratory geometries for mode counting . Compare them to open-region disks before importing closed-mode indices .
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch42.signature-apertures} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\subsection{Volume-surface equivalence}
\label{sec:ch421}
Equivalence replaces interior fields by sources on a surface without altering the exterior \cite{stratton1941}. The far zone stays the same while the inversion domain shrinks---MoM analysts see a smaller unknown vector, not a larger export cone \cite{harrington2001}. Rank is unchanged when $\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]$ is preserved; equivalence does not enlarge electrical size or manufacture extra propagating modes \cite{maxwell1873}.
Stratton--Chu and Love reductions preserve $\mathbf{c}$ when exterior equivalence holds \cite{stratton1941}. MoM gains smaller unknown sets, not larger export cones \cite{devaney2004}.
\medskip
Finite scatterers are frequently analyzed by replacing interior excitation with equivalent currents on a
surface \(S\) that encloses \(\Omega_{s}\) \cite{stratton1941}. The replacement is physically
meaningful only if exterior fields---and therefore \(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega[\Jimp]\) and
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]\)---remain unchanged in the outgoing region
\cite{collin2001}. Subsection~\ref{sec:ch421} states that invariance as a equivalence theorem.
Let \(\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{3}\) be a bounded interior region with Lipschitz boundary
\(S=\partial\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\), exterior \(\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}=\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\overline{\Omega}_{\mathrm{int}}\),
and impressed volume current \(\Jimp\) supported in \(\Omega_{s}\subset\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Fields satisfy harmonic Maxwell equations in \(\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}\) with outgoing
Sommerfeld outgoing condition as in Section~\ref{sec:ch24} \cite{stratton1941}. Equivalence currents
\(\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s}\) on \(S\) are tangential surface densities in the sense of
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.surface-source-embedding} \cite{stratton1941}.
denote by \(\mathcal{S}_\omega[\Jimp]\) the full Maxwell solution pair and by
\(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{(ext)}}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) the restriction of \(\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\mathcal{S}_\omega[\Jimp]\) to
\(\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Volume--surface equivalence requires
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch421
\begin{physicalvignette}[Horn-lens combination on a millimetre-wave bench]
Consider Horn-lens combination on a millimetre-wave bench. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Equivalence replaces interior fields by sources on a surface without altering the exterior . It simplifies computation; it does not enlarge aperture area .
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch421} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.exterior-equivalence-condition}
\big(\E^{\mathrm{(eq)}},\H^{\mathrm{(eq)}}\big)\big|_{\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}}
=
\big(\E^{\mathrm{(vol)}},\H^{\mathrm{(vol)}}\big)\big|_{\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}},
\end{equation}
where \((\E^{\mathrm{(vol)}},\H^{\mathrm{(vol)}})=\mathcal{S}_\omega[\Jimp]\) and
\((\E^{\mathrm{(eq)}},\H^{\mathrm{(eq)}})\) is generated by \((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\) on \(S\) alone
\cite{stratton1941}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.exterior-equivalence-condition} is the field statement of
equivalence: interior mechanisms may change, but \(\mathfrak{F}=\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]\) on
\(\operatorname{ran}\mathcal{R}_\omega\) is fixed \cite{harrington2001}. Coefficient listings satisfy
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.coefficient-equivalence-invariance}
\mathcal{C}\big(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]\big)
=
\mathcal{C}\big(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}]\big),
\qquad
\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}\ \text{the equivalent surface source},
\end{equation}
whenever Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.exterior-equivalence-condition} holds and \(\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{adm}}\)
\cite{hansen1988}. Realizability of \(\mathbf{c}\) is unchanged by equivalence reduction.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}
Let \(\Jimp\) be admissible with \(\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\subset\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\), and let
\((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\) be equivalent surface sources on \(S=\partial\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\) in the
sense of Stratton--Chu / Love equivalence \cite{stratton1941}. Then
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.exterior-equivalence-condition} holds in \(\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}\), and
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.gamma-equivalence-invariance}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]
=
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}]
\end{equation}
for every \(\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{adm}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Equivalence theorems construct \((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\) from tangential traces of
\((\E^{\mathrm{(vol)}},\H^{\mathrm{(vol)}})\) on \(S\) such that the exterior boundary-value problem with
outgoing radiation condition reproduces the same \(\E,\H\) in \(\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Uniqueness of outgoing exterior solutions
(\ref{thm:ch2.exterior-radiative-uniqueness}) fixes the radiative state
\(\mathfrak{F}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\); \(\mathcal{C}\) is a coordinate map on that state, hence
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.gamma-equivalence-invariance} \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) is the first structural simplification of $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$:
synthesis may be computed on \(S\) without modeling feed geometry inside \(\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\), provided
equivalence hypotheses hold \cite{collin2001}. The theorem does not assert uniqueness of
\((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\): distinct surface pairs can yield the same exterior
\(\mathfrak{F}\), enlarging \(\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}\) of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-null-space}
\cite{harrington2001}.
The following statement records the stratton--chu construction statement: on \(S\), define equivalent electric and magnetic surface currents from tangential traces
\cite{stratton1941}:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents}
\mathbf{J}_{s}
=
\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H^{\mathrm{(vol)}},
\qquad
\mathbf{M}_{s}
=
-\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\E^{\mathrm{(vol)}},
\end{equation}
with outward \(\hat{\mathbf{n}}\) on \(S\) \cite{stratton1941}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents} is the
standard Huygens--Stratton prescription: \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) replaces tangential \(\H\) discontinuity and
\(\mathbf{M}_{s}\) replaces tangential \(\E\) discontinuity across a fictitious surface supporting the same
exterior radiation \cite{harrington2001}. Coupling admissibility
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-admissibility} must be verified on the chosen mesh of \(S\), not assumed from
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents} alone \cite{harrington2001}.
Love and Huygens equivalence let an engineer replace volume currents by equivalent surface distributions on an aperture or enclosure while preserving the same exterior radiation \cite{stratton1941}. The theorem establishes that far-zone patterns are unchanged; it does not enlarge aperture area or electrical size \cite{harrington2001}.

Horn mouths, slot arrays, and reflector-equivalent surfaces are standard equivalence laboratories \cite{collin2001}.
The export manifold \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}]\) is invariant under passage from \(\Jimp\) to
\((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\) when (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) applies
\cite{devaney2004}. MoM implementations discretize \(S\) and form \(\mathbf{T}\) of
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mom-synthesis-matrix} on surface patches rather than volume voxels
\cite{harrington2001}.
A dipole feed inside a PEC cavity induces tangential \(\H\) on an aperture \(S_{a}\subset S\).
Equivalence replaces the interior \(\Jimp\) by \(\mathbf{J}_{s}=\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H\) on \(S_{a}\) with
\(\mathbf{M}_{s}=\mathbf{0}\) on PEC portions of \(S\) \cite{stratton1941}.
On \(S=\partial\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\), equivalent electric and magnetic surface densities are fixed by exterior
tangential traces through
\begin{equation}
\mathbf{J}_{s}
=
\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H^{\mathrm{(ext)}},
\qquad
\mathbf{M}_{s}
=
-\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\E^{\mathrm{(ext)}},
\end{equation}
with \(\hat{\mathbf{n}}\) outward from \(\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Equations
\eqref{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents} are not design freedoms: they are the unique surface tuple that reproduces
\(\mathfrak{F}\) in \(\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}\) when interior sources are annihilated and \(S\) encloses all
\(\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\) \cite{collin2001}. In synthesis language,
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}]\) with \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) assembled from
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents} equals \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]\) whenever
(\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) applies \cite{stratton1941}.
The love reduction on pec proceeds as follows: when \(S\) is PEC except for an aperture \(S_{a}\), \(\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\E=\mathbf{0}\) on the conductor and
\(\mathbf{M}_{s}=\mathbf{0}\) on PEC portions of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents}
\cite{stratton1941}. MoM discretizations that introduce magnetic currents on PEC without aperture justification
inflate \(\ker\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) without enlarging \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\)
\cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.equivalence-rank-invariance}
If (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) holds and \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) on \(S\), then
\(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) of (\ref{def:ch4.radiative-rank}) computed from volume
\(\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) equals \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) computed from equivalent
\((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\) on \(S\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}]\) by
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.gamma-equivalence-invariance}; singular values of \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on the
equivalence-reduced domain coincide when coupling is admissible \cite{harrington2001}.
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.equivalence-rank-invariance} states that equivalence is a \emph{representation change} on
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\), not a rank enlargement: discretizing \(S\) instead of \(\Omega_{s}\) may change MoM
patch count but not continuum radiative dimension when \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) \cite{harrington2001}.
\emph{Problem.} A volume current \(\Jimp\) has support in \(\Omega_{s}\). An engineer places equivalence surface
\(S'\) that fails to enclose a portion of \(\Omega_{s}\). Can \(\mathbf{c}=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]\) be
recovered from equivalent currents on \(S'\)?
\emph{Step 1.} Identify the exterior field region \(\Omega_{\mathrm{ext}}(S')\) where
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.exterior-equivalence-condition} is asserted.
\emph{Step 2.} Unenclosed source segments radiate directly into regions not determined by tangential data on \(S'\);
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}]\) on \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) differs from the true
\(\mathbf{c}\).
\emph{Step 3.} Enlarge \(S'\) to contain \(\Omega_{s}\) or restrict \(\mathcal{C}\) to the partial exterior where
equivalence is valid.
Regarding coefficient invariance under equivalent currents, when (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) holds,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.coefficient-invariance-equivalence}
\mathcal{C}\big(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]\big)
=
\mathcal{C}\big(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}]\big)
\quad\forall\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{adm}},
\end{equation}
extending Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coefficient-equivalence-invariance} to every admissible listing
. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.coefficient-invariance-equivalence} is the
synthesizable bound for equivalence-based design: optimizing \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) on \(S\) is equivalent to
optimizing \(\Jimp\) in volume only when enclosure and \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) hold .
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.mom-equivalence-consistency}
If volume and surface MoM models satisfy (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) and share the same
\(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{C}\), then \(\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{vol}}\mathbf{I}_{v}\) and
\(\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{surf}}\mathbf{I}_{s}\) yield identical \(\mathbf{c}\) up to \(\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}\)
When (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) holds, equivalence is a representation change on the inversion domain, not a mechanism that enlarges radiative rank. A smaller MoM matrix does not enlarge radiative degrees of freedom; fewer unknowns can represent the same export cone, not a larger one .
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.equivalence-rank-equality}
r_{\mathrm{rad}}^{\mathrm{(vol)}}(\varepsilon)
=
r_{\mathrm{rad}}^{\mathrm{(surf)}}(\varepsilon),
\end{equation}
as in Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.equivalence-rank-invariance}, but MoM ranks may differ:
\(\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{vol}}\neq\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{surf}}\) until
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) on both meshes . Surface reduction is a
search-space reduction, not a rank inflation mechanism .
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.love-reduction-domain}
Love equivalence on PEC restricts \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) to electric currents on apertures, shrinking the discrete
search basis on \(S\) without shrinking \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) when full equivalence is valid
. Targets requiring \(\mathbf{M}_{s}\neq0\) on PEC are outside the Love
domain .
\subsection{Apertures as equivalent sources}
\label{sec:ch422}
Antenna engineering speaks of \emph{apertures}---openings through which radiation passes---rather than of
closed equivalence surfaces \(S\) \cite{collin2001}. Disk, horn mouth, and waveguide flange are canonical laboratories for electrical size: equivalent currents model the opening, they do not invent extra propagating modes beyond what the aperture geometry can support \cite{stratton1941}. On a fixed measurement range, enlarging aperture diameter narrows the main beam and reallocates tail watts among sidelobes because $k_0 a$ rises before azimuthal harmonic labels acquire physical meaning \cite{chu1948}.
\medskip
Let \(\Sigma_{a}\subset S\) be a smooth aperture in an otherwise PEC or impedance boundary
\cite{collin2001}. Tangential \(\E_{t}\) and \(\H_{t}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) are traces of an
admissible exterior solution \cite{stratton1941}. Port feeds may reduce to \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) on
\(\Sigma_{a}\) by equivalence of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch421} \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{def:ch4.aperture-equivalent-source}
An \emph{aperture equivalent source} is a tangential surface current \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) such
that
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch422
\begin{physicalvignette}[Reflector with shaped beam for satellite downlink]
Consider Reflector with shaped beam for satellite downlink. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Apertures radiate through openings whose electrical size sets diffraction and directivity . Equivalent currents model the opening, not magic extra modes .
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch422} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.aperture-equivalent-def}
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{s}]
=
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_\omega[\Jimp^{\mathrm{(full)}}]
\quad\text{in}\ \mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}},
\end{equation}
for some full-model impressed current \(\Jimp^{\mathrm{(full)}}\) generating the same exterior
\(\mathfrak{F}\) \cite{stratton1941}. The aperture set of patterns a source can radiate is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.aperture-synthesis-set}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})
=
\left\{
\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C}):
\exists\,\mathbf{J}_{s}\ \text{on}\ \Sigma_{a}\ \text{with}\
\mathcal{C}\big(\mathcal{R}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{s}]\big)=\mathbf{c}
\right\}.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.aperture-synthesis-set} is the restriction of
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) to excitation class
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-class-aperture} \cite{harrington2001}. \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\)
is the set of far-zone listings producible by currents on the aperture alone, without resolving interior feed
detail \cite{collin2001}. \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\);
strict inclusion holds when interior degrees of freedom synthesize patterns not realizable by \(\Sigma_{a}\) alone
\cite{stratton1941}.
a \emph{radiating} aperture carries impressed \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) from a feed; a \emph{scattering} aperture
carries induced \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) from incident \(\E^{\mathrm{(inc)}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. For scattering,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.scattering-aperture-decomposition}
\mathbf{J}_{s}
=
\mathbf{J}_{s}^{\mathrm{(inc)}}+\mathbf{J}_{s}^{\mathrm{(ind)}},
Horn mouths, slot arrays, and reflector-equivalent surfaces are standard equivalence laboratories \cite{collin2001}.
\qquad
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{s}]
=
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{s}^{\mathrm{(ind)}}]
\end{equation}
when \(\mathbf{J}_{s}^{\mathrm{(inc)}}\) is a known calibration term \cite{stratton1941}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.scattering-aperture-decomposition} separates calibration from synthesis targets in inverse
problems \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.aperture-realizability-reduction}
If (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) holds with equivalence surface \(S\) containing
\(\Sigma_{a}\), then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.aperture-containment}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})
=
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
\quad\text{for patterns observed outside }S.
\end{equation}
If \(\Sigma_{a}\subsetneq S\) and PEC shorts part of \(S\), then
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\subsetneq\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) in general
\cite{collin2001}.
Equality in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.aperture-containment} follows from
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.gamma-equivalence-invariance} when \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) is supported only on
\(\Sigma_{a}\) after PEC reduction \cite{stratton1941}. Strict inclusion when \(\Sigma_{a}\subsetneq S\) follows
because interior currents behind PEC may radiate only through \(\Sigma_{a}\), restricting achievable \(\mathbf{c}\)
\cite{collin2001}.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ch4.aperture-realizability-reduction} explains why horn, slot, and patch designs optimize
\(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) rather than full volume \(\Jimp\): when containment holds, the aperture-limited synthesis manifold is the entire set of patterns a source can radiate \cite{harrington2001}. When containment fails, phase-only
aperture engineering optimizes over \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\), not over
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{devaney2004}.
Given measured tangential \(\E_{t},\H_{t}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\), equivalence currents follow
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents} with \(\hat{\mathbf{n}}\) the aperture normal
\cite{stratton1941}. Near-field reactive content on \(\Sigma_{a}\) affects \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) but not
\(\mathfrak{F}\) once \(\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\) has acted \cite{stratton1941}.
A rectangular waveguide radiates through aperture \(\Sigma_{a}\) at the open end. TE\(_{10}\) modal \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\)
on \(\Sigma_{a}\) generates \(\mathbf{c}\) with primarily \(\ell=1\) content at \(k_{0}a\ll1\) for small
\(\Sigma_{a}\) \cite{collin2001}. Targeting \(\ell>3\) coefficients from \(\Sigma_{a}\) alone fails
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.channel-count} when patch rank \(P\) is insufficient \cite{hansen1988}.
Regarding the horn slot and patch classification, laboratory apertures fall into three realizable radiative classes distinguished by how \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\)
sits inside \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{collin2001}. \emph{Primary-radiating}
apertures (open-ended guides, horns) satisfy Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.aperture-containment} with \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}\)
equal to the exterior-observed set of patterns a source can radiate \cite{stratton1941}. \emph{Secondary-radiating} apertures (slots in PEC,
coupled apertures) excite \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\subsetneq\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) when
interior resonances synthesize patterns not reachable by \(\Sigma_{a}\) alone \cite{harrington2001}.
\emph{Scattering} apertures carry induced \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) and require calibration separation
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.scattering-aperture-decomposition} before \(\mathbf{c}\) is interpreted as a synthesis target
\cite{stratton1941}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.aperture-class-inclusion}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}^{\mathrm{(scat)}}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}^{\mathrm{(sec)}}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}^{\mathrm{(pri)}}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C}),
\end{equation}
with inclusions strict in general \cite{collin2001}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.aperture-class-inclusion} orders
design freedom: phase-only optimization on a slot optimizes \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}^{\mathrm{(sec)}}\), not arbitrary
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{devaney2004}.
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering

 \node[ch1box, minimum width=28mm, right=7mm of sec] (scat) {Scattering\\aperture};
