\label{fig:ch4.aperture-taxonomy}
\end{figure}
Define \(D_{\mathrm{eff}}(\Sigma_{a})\) as the diameter of the smallest ball enclosing \(\Sigma_{a}\) with appreciable
\(\|\mathbf{J}_{s}\|\) \cite{collin2001}. Spectral compression uses \(D_{\mathrm{eff}}\) in place of
\(D_{s}\) when equivalence reduction is valid:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.effective-aperture-size}
\Xi_{\mathrm{eff}}
=
\frac{k_{0}D_{\mathrm{eff}}(\Sigma_{a})}{2\pi},
\qquad
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})
\ \text{compressed by}\ 
\Xi_{\mathrm{eff}}
\ \text{as in}\ 
\text{Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.ellmax-from-tail}}.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.effective-aperture-size} links aperture engineering to the same rank laws as volume supports
\cite{hansen1988}.
On \(\Sigma_{a}\) with area \(A_{a}\), patch spacing \(\Delta\) sets a discrete coupling Nyquist wavenumber
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.aperture-nyquist-rate}
k_{t,\mathrm{Nyq}}
=
\frac{\pi}{\Delta},
\qquad
\Delta\lesssim\frac{\lambda}{2}
\ \Longrightarrow\
k_{t,\mathrm{Nyq}}\gtrsim k_{0},
\end{equation}
using standard aperture sampling limits \cite{collin2001}. MoM bases that violate
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.aperture-nyquist-rate} produce aliased \(\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{disc}}\) rows: formally nonzero couplings
at high \(\ell\) that are mesh artifacts, not export modes \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.aperture-mom-rank}
For stable RWG discretization on \(\Sigma_{a}\) with \(P\) patches,
\(\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{VSH}}\le\min(P,|I_{\mathrm{acc}}|)\) and
\(\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{VSH}}\le C\,k_{0}^{2}A_{a}\) asymptotically per
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.aperture-coupling-scaling} \cite{harrington2001}. Excess patches beyond the
Nyquist rate do not increase \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) in the continuum limit
\cite{stratton1941}.
\subsection{Tangential continuity at boundaries}
\label{sec:ch423}
Tangential \(\E\) continuity. Across a dielectric interface without electric sheet charge,
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.interface-conditions} requires
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch423
\begin{physicalvignette}[Open cavity slot fed by a microstrip line]
Consider Open cavity slot fed by a microstrip line. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Tangential $\E$ and $\H$ must match across the aperture plane \cite{stratton1941}. Least-squares patches on sparse points can hide reactive errors \cite{harrington2001}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch423} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity}
\hat{\mathbf{n}}_{ab}\times\big(\E_{b}-\E_{a}\big)=\mathbf{0}
\quad\text{on}\ \Sigma_{ab},
\end{equation}
using the first row of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.interface-conditions} \cite{stratton1941}. For a PEC surface,
\(\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\E=\mathbf{0}\) replaces Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity} on the conductor
\cite{stratton1941}. Realizable aperture fields must satisfy
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity} on dielectric portions and the PEC condition elsewhere; otherwise
\(\mathbf{J}_{s}=\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H\) from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents} is inconsistent with
the declared boundary class \cite{harrington2001}.
the second row of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.interface-conditions} gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.tangential-H-jump}
\hat{\mathbf{n}}_{ab}\times\big(\H_{b}-\H_{a}\big)=\mathbf{J}_{s,ab}
\quad\text{on}\ \Sigma_{ab},
\end{equation}
linking impressed surface current to tangential magnetic jump \cite{stratton1941}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-H-jump} is the realizability completion for \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\): an equivalent aperture
current must equal the magnetic-field jump it claims to represent \cite{harrington2001}. On PEC with
aperture \(\Sigma_{a}\), \(\H_{t}\) is continuous in the sense of exterior traces while \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) carries
the feed \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.tangential-realizability-condition}
Define
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.tangential-screen}
\mathcal{D}_{\tau}[\mathbf{J}_{s},\E,\H]
=
1
\iff
\text{Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity}--\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-H-jump} hold on }\Sigma_{a}
\ \wedge\
\mathbf{J}_{s}=\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H_{t}\ \text{on radiating portions}.
\end{equation}
Then \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) on aperture channels only if
\(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1\) and \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Violation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity} contradicts
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.interface-conditions} and therefore Maxwell admissibility
\cite{stratton1941}. Mismatch between \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) and
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-H-jump} implies the surface source does not generate the declared tangential
\(\H\) \cite{harrington2001}. Coupling $\mathcal{D}_J=1$ is independent and is presupposed from
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-admissibility} \cite{harrington2001}.
(\ref{prop:ch4.tangential-realizability-condition}) prevents a common laboratory error: prescribing a
tangential \(\E\) template on \(\Sigma_{a}\) that is not the trace of any outgoing \(\mathfrak{F}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Pattern optimization over unconstrained \(\E_{t}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) optimizes
outside \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{hansen1988}.
Trace-space form. Tangential traces live in \(H^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{a})^{3}\) as in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.trace-space-definition}
\cite{stratton1941}. Realizable \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) are dual to tangential \(\E_{t}\) under the pairing of
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch232} \cite{harrington2001}. Numerically, \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1\) is enforced by matching
continuity rows in the MoM impedance matrix before \(\mathbf{T}\) of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mom-synthesis-matrix} is
inverted \cite{harrington2001}.
On PEC, \(\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\E=\mathbf{0}\) everywhere; on the slot \(\Sigma_{a}\), \(\E_{t}\neq\mathbf{0}\) and
\(\mathbf{J}_{s}=\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H\) carries radiation \cite{stratton1941}. A slot pattern that
violates \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1\) by discontinuity of \(\E_{t}\) across the slot rim is not realizable by any
\(\mathbf{J}_{s}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{harrington2001}.
on a PEC screen with slot \(\Sigma_{a}\), the tangential field pair satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.pec-slot-template}
\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\E
=
\mathbf{0}
\quad\text{on PEC},
\qquad
\mathbf{J}_{s}
=
\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H
\quad\text{on }\Sigma_{a},
\qquad
\mathbf{J}_{s}=\mathbf{0}
\quad\text{on PEC},
\end{equation}
using the PEC specialization of Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity}--\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-H-jump}
\cite{stratton1941}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.pec-slot-template} is the admissible manifold for
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\) on a flat aperture: any \(\E_{t}\) template on \(\Sigma_{a}\) that cannot be
embedded in an outgoing solution with Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.pec-slot-template} on the surrounding PEC is outside
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{harrington2001}.
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box, minimum width=26mm] (pec) {PEC screen\\$\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\E=\mathbf{0}$};
 \node[ch1box, minimum width=26mm, right=7mm of pec] (slot) {Slot $\Sigma_{a}$\\$\E_{t}\neq\mathbf{0}$};
 \node[ch1box, minimum width=28mm, below=5mm of slot] (js) {$\mathbf{J}_{s}=\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H$};
 \node[ch1box, minimum width=28mm, left=7mm of js] (dtau) {$\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1$};
 \draw[ch1flow] (pec.east) -- (pec.east |- slot.west);
 \draw[ch1flow] (pec.east |- slot.west) -- (slot.west);
 \draw[ch1flow] (slot.south) -- (slot.south |- js.north);
 \draw[ch1flow] (slot.south |- js.north) -- (js.north);
 \draw[ch1flow] (js.west) -- (js.west |- dtau.east);
 \draw[ch1flow] (js.west |- dtau.east) -- (dtau.east);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Subsection~\ref{sec:ch423}: tangential range membership on a PEC screen requires
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.pec-slot-template}; \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1\) is the joint condition on \(\E_{t}\) and
\(\mathbf{J}_{s}\).}
\label{fig:ch4.pec-slot-tangential}
\end{figure}
Figure~\ref{fig:ch4.pec-slot-tangential} connects boundary-class data to synthesis: \(\mathbf{T}\) of
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mom-synthesis-matrix} must be assembled only after \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1\) is enforced on the
declared trace class \cite{harrington2001}. Inverse problems that fit \(\E_{t}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) without
the PEC constraint inject non-physical tangential content and produce formal \(\mathbf{c}\) outside
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{devaney2004}.
When \(\Sigma_{a}\) lies on a dielectric interface, Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity} requires continuous
tangential \(\E\), while Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-H-jump} permits a surface current \(\mathbf{J}_{s,ab}\) only when
magnetic jump is physical \cite{stratton1941}. If \(\E_{t}\) is continuous but the declared \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) does not
equal \(\hat{\mathbf{n}}_{ab}\times(\H_{b}-\H_{a})\), \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=0\) and aperture synthesis is undefined
\cite{harrington2001}. Magnetic currents \(\mathbf{M}_{s}\) enter on interfaces where \(\E_{t}\) is discontinuous in
the equivalent-source picture of Section~\ref{sec:ch42} \cite{collin2001}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.tangential-range}
Let \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{ap}}=\{\mathbf{J}_{s}:\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1,\ \mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\}\). Then
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{ap}}]\subseteq
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\), with equality when every realizable \(\mathbf{c}\) admits an aperture
representative in \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{ap}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
On impedance surfaces \(\mathbf{Z}_{s}=\mathbf{Z}_{s}(\mathbf{r})\), tangential fields satisfy
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.leontovich-condition}
\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\big(\E-\mathbf{Z}_{s}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{n}}\times\H\big)=\mathbf{0}
\quad\text{on }\Sigma_{a},
\end{equation}
using the impedance row of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch1.interface-conditions} \cite{stratton1941}. Realizable
\(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) must be consistent with Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.leontovich-condition}: prescribing
\(\E_{t}\) incompatible with \(\H_{t}\) and \(\mathbf{Z}_{s}\) yields \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=0\)
\cite{collin2001}. PEC is the limit \(\mathbf{Z}_{s}\to\mathbf{0}\) with
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.pec-slot-template} \cite{stratton1941}.
Waveguide ports attach through \(\mathfrak{g}_\Gamma\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.excitation-tuple}: port modes define a
low-dimensional subspace of \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) before equivalence reduction \cite{collin2001}.
Tangential matching at the port aperture is the discrete analog of Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity}--\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-H-jump}
\cite{stratton1941}. Port synthesis rank is bounded by guided-mode count, not by full \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\)
\cite{hansen1988}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.impedance-tangential-condition}
On Leontovich \(\Sigma_{a}\), \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1\) requires simultaneous satisfaction of
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.leontovich-condition}, \eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-H-jump}, and
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) on the MoM mesh \cite{stratton1941}. Violation at any row
excludes \(\mathbf{c}\) from \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{devaney2004}.
On PEC bodies with apertures \(\Sigma_{a}\), tangential \(\E_{t}=0\) on metal and finite \(\E_{t}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) impose
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.pec-aperture-duality}
\mathcal{D}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{(PEC)}}
=
1
\iff
\begin{cases}
\E_{t}=0 & \text{on PEC},\\
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1,\ \mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=1 & \text{on }\Sigma_{a},
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
using the PEC limit of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.leontovich-condition} \cite{stratton1941}. Magnetic
rim currents \(\mathbf{M}_{s}\) on aperture edges are required when \(\E_{t}\) jumps between PEC and aperture sectors
\cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.composite-tangential-realizability}
Let \(S=S_{\mathrm{PEC}}\cup\Sigma_{a}\) be a composite boundary with declared \(\mathbf{Z}_{s}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\). Then
\(\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\) only if \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=1\) on every interface row of
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tangential-E-continuity}--\eqref{eq:ch4.leontovich-condition} simultaneously
\cite{stratton1941}. Failure on any row excludes \(\mathbf{c}\) before
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) rank is tested \cite{devaney2004}.
Well-posedness of equivalent-source reduction requires consistent tangential traces on all boundary components
\cite{stratton1941}; (\ref{prop:ch4.impedance-tangential-condition}) \cite{harrington2001}.
A narrow slot requires \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) and consistent \(\H_{t}\) jumps; prescribing slot voltage without
\(\H_{t}\) violates Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.pec-aperture-duality} \cite{collin2001}.
Tangential $\E$ and $\H$ matching on an aperture plane is what a flange or radome design enforces at the opening \cite{stratton1941}. synthesizable requires both components to be consistent with an impressed-current model on declared support; sparse probe patches that fit only one component can hide reactive error \cite{harrington2001}.
\subsection{Limits of equivalent-source models}
\label{sec:ch424}
Region of validity. Equivalence preserves \(\mathfrak{F}\) only in the exterior observation domain declared in
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.exterior-equivalence-condition} \cite{stratton1941}. Interior fields
\((\E,\H)\big|_{\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}}\) are generally \emph{not} invariant under replacement of \(\Jimp\) by
\((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\) \cite{harrington2001}. Near-field probes inside
\(\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\) therefore test a different realizable radiative class than far-zone \(\mathbf{c}\)
\cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.equivalence-error}
Suppose \((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\) on \(S\) are constructed from approximate tangential traces
\(\widetilde{\E}_{t},\widetilde{\H}_{t}\) rather than exact values. Then
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch424
\begin{physicalvignette}[Dual-offset reflector for compact range quiet zone]
Consider Dual-offset reflector for compact range quiet zone. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Equivalence fails when the equivalent surface is too small to represent reactive near fields that still affect input match . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch424} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.equivalence-coefficient-error}
\big\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}]-\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{true}}\big\|_{\mathcal{C}}
\le
\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{eq}}(S,\omega)\,
\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}-\mathbf{u}_{t}\big\|_{\mathcal{T}_\Gamma},
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{u}_{t}=(\E_{t},\H_{t})\), \(\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) the operator norm of the equivalence map
on tangential traces, and \(\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}}\) the coefficient norm induced by \(\mathcal{C}\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
Linearity of \(\mathcal{S}_\omega\), \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\), and \(\mathcal{C}\) gives
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}-\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}^{\mathrm{(true)}}]\) proportional to
trace perturbations on \(S\) \cite{stratton1941}. Continuity of tangential trace maps
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch2.tangential-trace-maps}--\eqref{eq:ch2.tangential-trace-magnetic} bounds the coefficient
perturbation by \(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}-\mathbf{u}_{t}\|_{\mathcal{T}_\Gamma}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.equivalence-error}) quantifies equivalence breakdown: approximate aperture fields produce
approximate \(\mathbf{c}\) with error controlled by trace mismatch, not by wishful normalization
\cite{devaney2004}. \(\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) grows with electrical size of \(S\) and with
proximity of \(S\) to \(\Omega_{s}\) \cite{harrington2001}.
Non-uniqueness and null contributions. Equivalent sources are not unique: any \(\delta\mathbf{J}_{s}\in\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega\) on \(S\) leaves
\(\mathbf{c}\) unchanged while altering near fields \cite{stratton1941}. Combined with
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-null-space},
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.equivalence-null-ambiguity}
\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(1)}-\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(2)}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(1)}]=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(2)}],
\end{equation}
even when \(\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(1)}\neq\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(2)}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\) \cite{devaney2004}. Equivalence
reduction therefore enlarges the synthesis null space without shrinking \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\)
\cite{harrington2001}.
Regarding the failure classification, equivalence fails to preserve \(\mathbf{c}\) when \(S\) does not enclose all
\(\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\) \cite{stratton1941}; when interior loss or dispersion is mis-modeled in the
equivalent exterior problem \cite{harrington2001}; when \(\Sigma_{a}\subsetneq S\) and containment
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.aperture-containment} fails \cite{collin2001}; when \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}=0\) or
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=0\) on the discretized aperture \cite{harrington2001}; or when reactive
near-zone content is mistaken for radiative \(\mathbf{c}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Each failure
produces a distinct signature in (\ref{lem:ch4.condition-failures}) or in
(\ref{thm:ch4.equivalence-error}) \cite{harrington2001}.
Equivalence error bounds tell the laboratory when an equivalent surface is too small to represent reactive near fields that still affect input match \cite{stratton1941}. A compact equivalent surface may reproduce far-zone gain yet fail on near-field probes---the bound quantifies that gap \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.interior-insensitivity}
If (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) holds, modifications of \(\Jimp\) inside
\(\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\) that leave tangential traces on \(S\) unchanged do not alter
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]\) \cite{stratton1941}. Conversely, interior changes that
\emph{do} alter tangential traces on \(S\) change \(\mathbf{c}\) even when exterior equivalence was previously
valid \cite{harrington2001}.
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.interior-insensitivity} is the engineering reading of equivalence limits: feed redesign
inside a cavity is invisible to far-zone \(\mathbf{c}\) only until it changes aperture tangential fields
\cite{collin2001}. Phase-only metasurface design on \(\Sigma_{a}\) acts on
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{ap}}(\mathcal{C})\), not on arbitrary \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\)
\cite{devaney2004}.
A radome with loss \(\varepsilon''>0\) inside \(\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\) alters tangential traces on \(S\) relative
to a lossless model \cite{stratton1941}. Equivalent \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) fit from exterior data
alone omit the loss mechanism; \(\mathbf{c}\) errors scale with \(\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) in
(\ref{thm:ch4.equivalence-error}) \cite{devaney2004}.
\emph{Problem.} MoM on \(\Sigma_{a}\) yields \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{MoM}}\); full-volume FEM yields
\(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{FEM}}\). When must they agree?
\emph{Step 1.} Verify enclosure and outgoing radiation condition for both models.
\emph{Step 2.} Test \(\mathcal{D}_{\tau}\) and \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}\) on \(\Sigma_{a}\)
\emph{Step 3.} Bound \(\|\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{MoM}}-\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{FEM}}\|\) by
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.equivalence-coefficient-error} using measured trace mismatch on \(S\)
Stratton--Chu equivalence Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.stratton-chu-currents} requires both \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) and
\(\mathbf{M}_{s}\) on \(S\) . Love equivalence eliminates \(\mathbf{M}_{s}\) on PEC portions but
restricts \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) to electric currents on apertures . The
realizability consequence is rank: Love-reduced models shrink the discrete multipole basis on \(S\) without shrinking
\(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) when (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) holds
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.equivalence-proximity}
\(\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{eq}}(S,\omega)\) in (\ref{thm:ch4.equivalence-error}) increases as \(S\) approaches
\(\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\) .
Equivalence preserves \(\mathfrak{F}\) on \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) but not reactive fields in
\(\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\) . Near-field probes that reconstruct \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}\) from
interior samples therefore test a different realizable radiative class than far-zone \(\mathbf{c}\)
. The split mirrors Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.near-far-coupling-split} at the equivalence reduction.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.far-insensitivity-equivalence}
Distinct equivalent pairs \((\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(A)},\mathbf{M}_{s}^{(A)})\) and
\((\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(B)},\mathbf{M}_{s}^{(B)})\) on \(S\) that satisfy
(\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) yield identical \(\mathbf{c}\) while differing in near-zone
\(\H,\E\) on \(\Omega_{\mathrm{int}}\) .
Equivalence at \(\omega_{0}\) does not establish \(\mathbf{c}(\omega)\) on a band \([\omega_{1},\omega_{2}]\) unless
\((\mathbf{J}_{s},\mathbf{M}_{s})\) are reconstructed frequency-by-frequency with causal time-domain reduction
. Define the broadband equivalence residual
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.broadband-equivalence-residual}
\delta_{\mathrm{bb}}
=
\sup_{\omega\in[\omega_{1},\omega_{2}]}
\big\|
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}(\omega)]
-
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp(\omega)]
\big\|_{\mathcal{C}},
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}(\omega)\) the Stratton--Chu reduction at each \(\omega\)
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.single-freq-equivalence-trap}
If \(\delta_{\mathrm{bb}}>0\) on a declared band, then \(\mathbf{c}(\omega)\) fitted only at \(\omega_{0}\) is not a
wideband synthesis test even when (\ref{thm:ch4.volume-surface-equivalence}) holds at \(\omega_{0}\)
. Dispersive interiors amplify \(\delta_{\mathrm{bb}}\) through
frequency-dependent tangential traces on \(S\) .
Null-space enlargement under equivalence. Equivalent-source non-uniqueness enlarges \(\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}\) through
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.equivalence-null-ambiguity} without enlarging \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\)
. The synthesis quotient
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}/\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}\) is therefore smaller in cardinality of representatives but
unchanged in \(\mathbf{c}\) image . Near-field discrimination of equivalent pairs is generally
impossible from \(\mathbf{c}\) alone .
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.equivalence-fiber}
For \(\mathbf{c}_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\), the fiber
\(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{c}_{0}}=\{\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}:\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{eq}}]=\mathbf{c}_{0}\}\)
has dimension at least \(\dim\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega|_{S}\) on the equivalence surface .
Linearity and non-uniqueness of equivalent sources on \(S\) ; kernel inclusion
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.rank-null-composition} .
\emph{Problem.} MoM returns \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) claiming uniqueness. Is \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) unique?
\emph{Step 1.} Test \(\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(1)}-\mathbf{J}_{s}^{(2)}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{syn}}\).
\emph{Step 2.} If difference lies in equivalence null space, \(\mathbf{c}\) is still unique.
\emph{Step 3.} Near-field validation cannot resolve \(\mathbf{J}_{s}\) without additional constraints outside
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\).
Non-uniqueness of equivalent sources is the mathematical expression of gauge-like freedom on surfaces: many surface currents produce the same exterior field.
\section{Finite Support and Radiation Operators}
Geometry legislates; operators record the legislation---support size, aperture extent, and electrical size determine $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ before any synthesis chart enters \cite{chu1948}.
\label{sec:ch43}
Section~\ref{sec:ch42} answered \emph{which} surface description reproduces a given far-zone pattern; Section~\ref{sec:ch43} asks \emph{how many} independent far-zone patterns that description permits once support is compact \cite{stratton1941}. compact $\operatorname{supp}\Jimp$ makes the export map compact on $\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$---a Hertzian dipole, horn mouth, or compact enclosure radiator of fixed diameter cannot independently excite infinitely many far-zone directions at fixed $k_{0}$ \cite{stratton1941}. MoM mesh refinement discretizes the same operator without enlarging $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$; publish singular-value cliffs alongside any $\ell_{\max}$ setting \cite{harrington2001}.
Compact support makes export compact on $\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$ \cite{stratton1941}; Subsections~\ref{sec:ch431}--\ref{sec:ch434} factor compression through domains, spectra, excitation-to-flux sequences, and singular values \cite{devaney2004}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch43
\begin{physicalvignette}[Spherical NF range with theta-phi robot arm]
Consider Spherical NF range with theta-phi robot arm. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch43} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\subsection{Compact source domains}
\label{sec:ch431}
Compact support bandlimits what lateral spectrum can be supplied to the far zone \cite{jackson1999}.
once $\operatorname{supp}\Jimp$ is bounded, high angular orders are suppressed before any harmonic listing is interpreted---electrical size $k_0 D_s$ sets the sidelobe floor the range engineer already measures \cite{jackson1999}.
Bounded support suppresses high angular orders before harmonic listing \cite{jackson1999}. Enclosing ball radius $a$ enters every rank inequality \cite{chu1948}.
\medskip
Spectral compression is not an post hoc choice imposed after the fact: it is the physically imposed
consequence of compact \(\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\) \cite{stratton1941}. Once
support is bounded, \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) acting on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) is compact, and
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) cannot have infinite radiative rank at fixed electrical size at fixed \(\omega\) \cite{hansen1988}.
Let \(\Omega_{s}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{3}\) be compact with \(\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})=D_{s}<\infty\) as in
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.support-screen} \cite{stratton1941}. Realizability requires Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizability-screen} \cite{harrington2001}. Exterior
region carries outgoing Sommerfeld outgoing condition Section~\ref{sec:ch24} \cite{stratton1941}. Electrical size
\(\Xi=k_{0}D_{s}/(2\pi)\) from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.electrical-size} parametrizes decay of far coefficients
\cite{jackson1999}.
\phantomsection\label{def:ch4.compact-source-domain}
A \emph{compact source domain} is a pair \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) with \(\Omega_{s}\) compact and
\(\operatorname{supp}\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\subseteq\Omega_{s}\) for every
\(\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{tot}}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. ~\eqref{eq:ch4.support-screen}.
when \(\Omega_{s}=B_{a}=\{\mathbf{r}:|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}_{c}|\le a\}\), the dimensionless parameter
\(k_{0}a\) controls how many angular orders carry appreciable power in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-tail-energy}
\cite{stratton1941}. For \(k_{0}a\ll1\), \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}]\) is
concentrated in low-\(\ell\) sectors; for \(k_{0}a\gg1\), higher \(\ell\) become accessible before aperture and
measurement compress further \cite{hansen1988}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.electrical-size} unifies
volume and aperture supports: \(\Sigma_{a}\subset\partial\Omega_{s}\) inherits the same \(D_{s}\) when the
aperture diameter equals the enclosing scale \cite{collin2001}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.compact-radiation-map}
Let \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}(\Omega_{s})\) denote realizable sources on compact \(\Omega_{s}\). Then
\(\mathcal{R}_\omega:\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}(\Omega_{s})\to\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) is a compact linear
operator in the energy topology of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.field-energy-norm} \cite{stratton1941}. For
every countable \(\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{adm}}\),
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega:\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}(\Omega_{s})\to\mathbb{C}^{I}\) is compact into any
\(\ell^{2}(I)\) radiative dimension with flux-normalized basis \cite{hansen1988}.
Bounded \(\Omega_{s}\) and retarded Green representation
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.electric-dyadic-convolution} yield integral operators with square-integrable kernels on
exterior observation shells \cite{stratton1941}. Composition with outgoing projection
\(\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\) and finite-energy norm Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.field-energy-norm} gives compactness on
\(\mathcal{J}_\Omega\) as in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch213} \cite{harrington2001}. Flux-normalized
\(\mathcal{C}\) from Section~\ref{sec:ch35} is bounded on \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\), hence
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) is compact \cite{hansen1988}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.compact-radiation-map}) is the Maxwell backbone of synthesizable: compactness forces
singular values of \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) to decay, hence \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) is
intrinsically finite-dimensional at any fixed truncation tolerance \cite{harrington2001}.
Physically, singular-value cliffs on dish and slot patterns appear as sidelobe decay; enclosing-sphere analysis is routine in RCS and antenna facilities \cite{hansen1988}.
Corollary
\ref{cor:ch4.synthesis-not-open} is the coefficient-space restatement of the same fact \cite{hansen1988}.
A dish antenna with diameter $D_{s}$ and a uniformly spaced $16\times16$ planar array at the same electrical
size excite different current distributions on $\Omega_{s}$, yet both yield compact $\mathcal{R}_\omega$ on
$\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$ \cite{stratton1941}.
Singular values of $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ decay after index $\sim (k_{0}D_{s}/2)^{3}$ in volume-like scaling:
the radiative dimension is set by geometry and frequency, not by how many harmonics the listing prints
\cite{hansen1988}.
Compact support is why a compact radiator, dish, or horn cannot populate an infinite harmonic ladder at fixed frequency \cite{stratton1941}. The retarded radiation operator maps bounded sources to a decaying singular-value ladder; high chart indices may appear in a complete listing but carry no export above tolerance \cite{hansen1988}.

\(\ell=4\) content without enlarging \(a\) targets outside \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}]\)
\cite{hansen1988}.
for \(\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}(\Omega_{s})\) with \(\Omega_{s}\) compact, the radiation kernel induced by
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.electric-dyadic-convolution} is square-integrable on exterior observation shells
\cite{stratton1941}. Hence \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) is not merely compact but Hilbert--Schmidt on bounded
energy norms:

On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Compact support bandlimits what lateral spectrum can be supplied to the far zone . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.

\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.HS-bound}
\|\mathcal{R}_\omega\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}
=
\int_{\Omega_{s}}\!\int_{\Omega_{s}}
\big|\mathbf{G}_{E}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{r}',\omega)\big|^{2}\,d^{3}r\,d^{3}r'
<
\infty,
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{G}_{E}\) the retarded electric Green dyadic \cite{stratton1941}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.HS-bound} quantifies how support volume enters export capacity: enlarging \(\Omega_{s}\) increases
\(\|\mathcal{R}_\omega\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\) and typically increases \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) at fixed
\(\varepsilon\) \cite{devaney2004}. Compactness is the continuum reason MoM matrices exhibit clustered
singular spectra on electrically small structures \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.noncompact-no-decay}
If \(\operatorname{supp}\Jimp\) is non-compact (infinite line, half-space feed), \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) need not be
compact on the declared source class and \(\sigma_{n}\) may not decay to zero \cite{stratton1941}. Then
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) is not intrinsically finite-dimensional at any fixed truncation tolerance
\cite{harrington2001}.
Non-compact supports violate the integral condition underlying Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.HS-bound}; unbounded travel along the
feed direction sustains non-decaying lateral spectral content in \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) \cite{jackson1999}.
(\ref{lem:ch4.noncompact-no-decay}) explains why \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}=1\) is a default range membership
screen: without compact support, Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.synthesis-not-open} and radiative dimension laws of
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch434} need not apply \cite{stratton1941}.
the support condition Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.support-screen} is the first condition in the $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ tuple: without
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}=1\), \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) need not be compact and \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) may
be infinite \cite{stratton1941}. Define the \emph{support diameter functional}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.support-diameter-functional}
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{diam}}[\Omega_{s}]
=
\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s}),
\qquad
\Xi[\Omega_{s}]
=
\frac{k_{0}\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{diam}}[\Omega_{s}]}{2\pi},
\end{equation}
linking geometry directly to electrical size used in Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.ellmax-from-tail} and
\eqref{eq:ch4.geometry-coupling-scaling} \cite{hansen1988}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.support-diameter-functional}
makes support a synthesis parameter: two feeds with identical \(\mathbf{c}\) targets but different
\(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{diam}}\) belong to different \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) classes
\cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.nested-support-synthesis}
If \(\Omega_{s}^{(1)}\subseteq\Omega_{s}^{(2)}\), then
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C};\Omega_{s}^{(1)})\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C};\Omega_{s}^{(2)})\)
for identical \(\mathcal{C}\) and \(\omega\) \cite{stratton1941}. Strict inclusion holds when
\(\Xi[\Omega_{s}^{(2)}]>\Xi[\Omega_{s}^{(1)}]\) activates new eigenfield couplings
\cite{jackson1999}.
Compact \(\Omega_{s}\) makes the retarded convolution \(\mathcal{S}_\omega\) a compact operator on bounded source
norms \cite{stratton1941}. The export capacity functional
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.export-capacity-functional}
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{exp}}[\Omega_{s}]
=
\sup_{\|\Jimp\|=1}
\|\mathcal{R}_\omega[\Jimp]\|_{\mathrm{rad}},
\end{equation}
is finite if and only if \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{supp}}=1\) and \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}}=1\)
\cite{stratton1941}. For ball support \(B_{a}\),
\(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{exp}}\) grows monotonically with \(k_{0}a\) and \(\operatorname{vol}(B_{a})\) through
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.HS-bound} \cite{jackson1999}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.support-volume-scaling}
On \(B_{a}\) with \(k_{0}a\ge1\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.support-volume-scaling}
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)
\ \le\
C_{\mathrm{vol}}(\varepsilon)\,(k_{0}a)^{3},
\end{equation}
with \(C_{\mathrm{vol}}\) depending only on \(\varepsilon\) and flux gauge \cite{stratton1941}.
Consequently \(\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C};B_{a})\) cannot grow faster than volume scaling at fixed
\(\varepsilon\) \cite{harrington2001}.
Combine (\ref{thm:ch4.compact-radiation-map}), Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.weyl-mode-count}, and
(\ref{thm:ch4.radiative-rank}) \cite{stratton1941}.
Enlarging the enclosing sphere or support volume raises the electrical size parameter and therefore the number of angular directions that can carry appreciable power \cite{jackson1999}. A prime-focus reflector at $k_0 D=20$ permits far more radiative rank than a Hertzian dipole at $k_0 a=0.3$ \cite{stratton1941}.

\subsection{Spectral compression from finite geometry}
\label{sec:ch432}
On a compact-range circular aperture, sidelobe shoulders---not fit tolerance---reveal when high-$\ell$ sectors have already lost exported watts \cite{hansen1988}.
Spectral compression means high-order directions carry less exported power on finite apertures \cite{hansen1988}.
Tail energy above $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ is the watt-facing truncation diagnostic inherited from Chapter~3 \cite{stratton1941}. Method-of-moments refinement samples the same compact operator more finely without steepening the singular-value ladder unless support or frequency changes \cite{harrington2001}.
\medskip
(\ref{def:ch4.spectral-compression}) introduced tail budgets on radiative dimension; Subsection
~\ref{sec:ch432} identifies the same phenomenon as operator compression on \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) when
\(\Omega_{s}\) is compact \cite{stratton1941}. The abstract template is
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.spectral-compression-operator} from Subsection~\ref{sec:ch213}; the display below
applies to synthesis on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{hansen1988}.

\(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) from (\ref{thm:ch4.compact-radiation-map}), and VSH flux normalization from
Section~\ref{sec:ch35} \cite{stratton1941}. Truncation level \(N\) may denote modal index cutoff or
singular-value cutoff interchangeably until Subsection~\ref{sec:ch434} fixes the SVD gauge \cite{harrington2001}.
Let \(\{\mathbf{u}_{n}\}_{n\ge1}\) be radiation eigenpairs Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.radiation-eigenpair-def} ordered by
decreasing export gain \(|\lambda_{n}|\) \cite{stratton1941}. Define the rank-\(N\) compressed map

On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. \begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.compressed-radiation-operator}
\mathcal{R}_\omega^{(N)}
=
\sum_{n=1}^{N}\lambda_{n}\,P_{n}\circ\mathcal{S}_\omega,
\end{equation}
Physically, the relation connects hardware limits to the harmonic coordinates established in \ref{ch:03} without re-opening Maxwell foundations. \cite{hansen1988}
from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.spectral-compression-operator} \cite{stratton1941}. On compact
\(\Omega_{s}\), the compression error satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.compression-error-import}
\|\mathcal{R}_\omega-\mathcal{R}_\omega^{(N)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
C\,\sigma_{N+1}\!\big(\mathcal{R}_\omega|_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}}\big),
\end{equation}
from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.compression-error-bound} with \(C\) independent of \(\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\)
\cite{harrington2001}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.compression-error-import} bounds watt residuals from truncating
\(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) before \(\mathcal{C}\) is applied: geometry compresses fields, not merely coefficients
\cite{stratton1941}.
Coefficient-space compression. Composing Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.compressed-radiation-operator} with \(\mathcal{C}\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.compressed-gamma-operator}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega^{(N)}
=
\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega^{(N)}:
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\to\mathbb{C}^{I},
\qquad
\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega^{(N)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
C'\,\sigma_{N+1}\!\big(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\big),
\end{equation}
with \(C'\) depending only on normalization of \(\mathcal{C}\) \cite{hansen1988}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.compressed-gamma-operator} links (\ref{prop:ch4.compressed-synthesis}) to singular-value
tail: \(\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{\max}}\) in radiative dimension and \(\mathcal{R}_\omega^{(N)}\) in field space are
equivalent compressions when \(\{\mathbf{u}_{n}\}\) align with VSH modes Section~\ref{sec:ch34}
\cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.geometry-spectral-equivalence}
For ball support \(B_{a}\) and VSH listing \(\mathcal{C}\), there exists \(N_{\mathrm{geo}}(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}},k_{0}a)\)
such that Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def} holds with \(\ell_{\max}=N_{\mathrm{geo}}\) if and only if
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.compression-error-import} holds with \(N=N_{\mathrm{geo}}\) at tolerance \(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}}\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
Tail energy Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-tail-energy} equals \(\sum_{n>N}|\lambda_{n}|^{2}\|\widehat{c}_{n}\|^{2}\) in
an aligned eigenbasis \cite{hansen1988}. Compactness (\ref{thm:ch4.compact-radiation-map}) gives
\(\sigma_{n}\sim|\lambda_{n}|\) ordering for export modes \cite{harrington2001}. Equivalence of cutoffs follows
from Parseval on \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.mode-power-from-coefficient} \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{prop:ch4.geometry-spectral-equivalence}) is the inevitability statement of Subsection
~\ref{sec:ch432}: electrical size and singular-value tails are two readings of the same compression, not separate
engineering knobs \cite{devaney2004}. Section~\ref{sec:ch46} develops scaling exponents for
\(N_{\mathrm{geo}}\); Section~\ref{sec:ch48} replaces \(N\) by concentration bounds \cite{harrington2001}.
At \(k_{0}a=2\), suppose \(\sigma_{N+1}/\sigma_{1}=10^{-3}\). Then rank-\(N\) synthesis captures \(99.9\%\) of
export gain in operator norm \cite{harrington2001}. Demanding \(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}}<10^{-3}\) in
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def} requires \(N\ge N_{\mathrm{geo}}\) from (\ref{prop:ch4.geometry-spectral-equivalence}) \cite{hansen1988}.
on ball support \(B_{a}\), the number of export modes above threshold \(\varepsilon\) scales as
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.weyl-mode-count}
N_{\mathrm{geo}}(\eta_{\mathrm{tail}},k_{0}a)
\ \sim\
\mathcal{W}(k_{0}a)^{3}
\ \sim\
(k_{0}a)^{3},
\end{equation}
in the high-frequency limit within the compact-source regime \cite{stratton1941}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.weyl-mode-count} is the volume scaling foreseen by Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.support-monotone-enlargement}:
doubling \(a\) at fixed \(k_{0}\) increases available synthesis directions faster than doubling frequency alone on fixed
\(a\) \cite{devaney2004}. Section~\ref{sec:ch46} sharpens exponents; here the scaling explains why
electrically large volumes support high \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}\) while electrically small volumes do not
\cite{hansen1988}.
Define the compression residual operator
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.compression-residual-operator}
\mathbf{R}_{N}
=
\mathcal{R}_\omega-\mathcal{R}_\omega^{(N)},
\qquad
\|\mathbf{R}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
C\,\sigma_{N+1},
\end{equation}
Physically, radiative rank $r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)$ counts watt-distinguishable far-zone patterns, not rows in a mode coefficient listing. Singular-value cliffs on $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ are the operator face of the same integer as $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ in the aligned gauge. \cite{hansen1988}
from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.compression-error-import} \cite{stratton1941}. On radiative dimension,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.coefficient-compression-residual}
\|\mathbf{c}-\mathbf{c}^{(N)}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}
\le
C'\,\sigma_{N+1}\big(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\big)\,\|\Jimp\|_{\mathcal{J}},
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{c}^{(N)}=\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega^{(N)}[\Jimp]\) \cite{hansen1988}. Equations
\eqref{eq:ch4.compression-residual-operator}--\eqref{eq:ch4.coefficient-compression-residual} quantify truncation error from
premature truncation: choosing \(N\) too small leaves \(\mathbf{c}^{(N)}\notin\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{fs}}\); choosing \(N\)
too large does not enlarge \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) beyond geometry rank \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.spectral-compression-optimal}
Among all rank-\(N\) operators \(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\) on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\),
\(\mathcal{R}_\omega^{(N)}\) minimizes \(\|\mathcal{R}_\omega-\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\), and
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega^{(N)}\) minimizes \(\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\)
at the same \(N\) \cite{harrington2001}.
Eckart--Young theorem applied to \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) and \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) with export eigenpairs
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.radiation-eigenpair-def} \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.spectral-compression-optimal}) elevates singular-value truncation from engineering habit to
realizability optimality: no other rank-\(N\) synthesis operator approximates \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) better in
operator norm \cite{hansen1988}. Listing \(\ell_{\max}\) without matching \(N_{\mathrm{geo}}\) from
(\ref{prop:ch4.geometry-spectral-equivalence}) wastes degrees of freedom or omits export modes
\cite{devaney2004}.
Spectral truncation order on \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\). Finite geometry induces a nested family of truncated set of patterns a source can radiate
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.compression-hierarchy}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(1)}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(2)}(\mathcal{C})
\subseteq
\cdots
\subseteq
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C}),
\qquad
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})
=
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega^{(N)}[\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}],
\end{equation}
with strict inclusions until \(N\ge r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) \cite{stratton1941}. The
nesting is monotone in export gain and optimal at each \(N\) by (\ref{thm:ch4.spectral-compression-optimal})
\cite{hansen1988}.
Spectral compression is visible on finite apertures as sidelobe floors: high-order multipoles carry less power than low-order ones \cite{hansen1988}. The optimal compression law states that no reparameterization of feed weights can beat the decay imposed by geometry---only enlarging support or raising frequency widens the ladder \cite{chu1948}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.compression-saturation}
Let \(N_{\mathrm{sat}}(\varepsilon)\) be the smallest \(N\) such that
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})=\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}^{(N+1)}(\mathcal{C})\). Then
\(N_{\mathrm{sat}}(\varepsilon)=r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) in the aligned eigenbasis of
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.gamma-SVD} \cite{harrington2001}. Listing \(\ell_{\max}>N_{\mathrm{sat}}\)
without power above \(\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\) is gauge padding \cite{hansen1988}.
\subsection{Source-to-field radiation operators}
\label{sec:ch433}
The radiation map sends currents to outgoing fields before any harmonic label is attached \cite{stratton1941}.
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.master-operator-composition} is the laboratory map from feed currents to pattern coefficients \cite{stratton1941}. $\mathcal{S}_\omega$ solves Maxwell; $\mathcal{R}_\omega$ selects outgoing radiation; $\mathcal{N}$ fixes flux meters; $\mathcal{C}$ chooses angular coordinates on $\Gamma_R$ \cite{poynting1884}. realizability checks apply to the composed map---impressed data pass through solution, radiation, and listing before coefficients enter $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$; reactive content stripped before $\mathcal{C}$ never appears in $\mathbf{c}$ \cite{devaney2004}.
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.master-operator-composition} maps feed currents to coefficients through solution, outgoing selection, flux normalization, and listing \cite{stratton1941}. $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ applies to composed $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ \cite{devaney2004}.
\medskip
 Subsection~\ref{sec:ch433} writes that composition as a single chain and prepares its singular-value factorization \cite{hansen1988}.
Fix \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\), \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\), \(\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{adm}}\), and
flux normalization \(\mathcal{N}\) from Section~\ref{sec:ch35} \cite{stratton1941}. All maps are linear
on declared domains \cite{stratton1941}.
the source--radiation--field--coefficient chain is
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch433
\begin{physicalvignette}[Open-ended WR-90 waveguide on a gantry]
Consider Open-ended WR-90 waveguide on a gantry. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
The radiation map sends currents to outgoing fields before any harmonic label is attached . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch433} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.master-operator-composition}
\mathbf{c}
=
\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega\circ\mathcal{S}_\omega[\Jimp]
=
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp],
\qquad
\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}},
\end{equation}
extending Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coefficient-synthesis-map} with explicit \(\mathcal{S}_\omega\) and \(\mathcal{N}\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.master-operator-composition} is the synthesizable backbone:
each factor is fixed by prior chapters; Chapter~4 analyzes rank and inversion of the composition
\cite{devaney2004}. Reactive content removed by \(\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\subset\mathcal{R}_\omega\) never reaches
\(\mathbf{c}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Define the field-space radiation operator
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.field-radiation-operator}
\mathfrak{R}_\omega
=
\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega\circ\mathcal{S}_\omega:
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\to\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}},
\end{equation}
so \(\mathbf{c}=\mathcal{C}\circ\mathfrak{R}_\omega[\Jimp]\) \cite{stratton1941}. On compact
\(\Omega_{s}\), \(\mathfrak{R}_\omega\) is compact; \(\mathcal{C}\) is bounded on
\(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\cap\mathcal{V}\) \cite{harrington2001}. Singular values of
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) satisfy \(\sigma_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega)\le
\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\sigma_{n}(\mathfrak{R}_\omega)\) \cite{stratton1941}.

Physically, the display fixes a step that every compact-range campaign presupposes once outgoing export has been declared on the measurement sphere. \cite{stratton1941}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.discrete-chain-matrix}
\mathbf{c}
=
\mathbf{T}\,\mathbf{I}_{s},

=

extending Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mom-synthesis-matrix} to the full chain \cite{harrington2001}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.discrete-chain-matrix} is the matrix verified in design codes: \(\mathbf{T}\) encodes Green
convolution, outgoing projection, normalization, and listing in one pass \cite{harrington2001}. Rank of
\(\mathbf{T}\) upper-bounds \(\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) by Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.channel-count}
\cite{devaney2004}.
When reciprocity holds Section~\ref{sec:ch28}, the adjoint chain
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.adjoint-chain}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega^{\dagger}
=
\mathcal{S}_\omega^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}
\end{equation}
maps coefficient functionals to source duals on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{prin:ch4.operator-composition}
\emph{Truncation bound.} For declared $(\Omega_s,k_0,\varepsilon,\mathcal{C})$, the integer $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ counted from the singular-value ladder of $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$, together with tail energy below $\varepsilon$ on $\Gamma_R$, establishes that harmonic orders above $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ carry no watt-measurable export \cite{hansen1988}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-certificate} records the same bound in operational form.
A coefficient target \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\) is realizable if and only if it lies in
\(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\); equivalently, if and only if
the minimum-distance residual \(\mathbf{r}_{\min}=\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{ran}}\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\)
vanishes, with \(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{ran}}\) the orthogonal projector onto
\(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) in \(\ell^{2}(I)\) \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{prin:ch4.operator-composition}) replaces pattern-matching language with a range condition: the chain
\emph{defines} \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) as \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\)
\cite{hansen1988}. 
Write the master chain as explicit composition
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.chain-factorization}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega
=
\underbrace{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{list}}
\circ
\underbrace{\mathcal{N}}_{\text{gauge}}
\circ
\underbrace{\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}}_{\text{export}}
\circ
\underbrace{\mathcal{S}_\omega}_{\text{solve}}
\quad\text{on}\ \mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}},
\end{equation}
from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.master-operator-composition} \cite{stratton1941}. Each factor is verified
separately in synthesis failure diagnosis: \(\mathcal{S}_\omega\) failures are source or boundary class errors;
\(\Pi_{\mathrm{rad}}\) failures are reactive contamination; \(\mathcal{N}\) failures are gauge mismatches;
\(\mathcal{C}\) failures are overcomplete listings \cite{devaney2004}.

Residual norm as synthesis test. Define
$\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ is a property of sources before it is a property of coefficient vectors. Frequency, support outline, and flux gauge must be fixed before any index in $\mathbb{C}^{I}$ is interpreted. \cite{stratton1941}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.synthesis-residual-norm}
\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}
=
\|\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{ran}}\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}},
\end{equation}
with \(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{ran}}\) from (\ref{prin:ch4.operator-composition}) \cite{stratton1941}.
Then \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\iff\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}=0\)
\cite{harrington2001}. Nonzero \(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}\) lower-bounds irreducible pattern error at fixed
\((\Omega_{s},\omega,\mathcal{C})\) \cite{hansen1988}.
The chain conditioning and inversion stability condition requires that the condition number of the discrete chain satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.chain-condition-number}
\kappa(\mathbf{T})
\le
\kappa(\mathcal{C})\,\kappa(\mathcal{N})\,\kappa(\mathcal{R}_\omega|_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}})\,
\kappa(\mathcal{S}_\omega|_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}}),
\end{equation}
with each factor the operator condition number on the declared domain \cite{harrington2001}.
Ill-conditioned \(\mathbf{T}\) signals near rank deficiency in \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\), not merely mesh noise:
inversion amplifies components along \(\ker\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega^{\dagger}\) that lie outside
\(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{hansen1988}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.moore-penrose-synthesis}
Let \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\in\mathbb{C}^{I}\) and \(\mathbf{T}^{+}\) the Moore--Penrose pseudoinverse of
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.discrete-chain-matrix}. The minimum-norm least-squares feed
\(\mathbf{I}_{s}^{\star}=\mathbf{T}^{+}\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\) is realizable only if
\(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\in\operatorname{ran}\mathbf{T}\); otherwise
\(\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{I}_{s}^{\star}-\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\|=\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}>0\) with
\(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}\) from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.synthesis-residual-norm} \cite{harrington2001}.
Pseudoinverse optimality on \(\ell^{2}\) \cite{harrington2001}; (\ref{prin:ch4.operator-composition}) for range
membership \cite{devaney2004}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.moore-penrose-synthesis}) is the numerical reading of inevitability: least-squares MoM fits always
return a current, but \(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}=0\) is the synthesis test \cite{hansen1988}. Tikhonov
regularization lowers \(\kappa(\mathbf{T})\) while enlarging \(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}\)---a trade controlled by
\(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) \cite{devaney2004}.
Each chain factor in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.master-operator-composition} carries a local rank budget:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.chain-factor-ranks}
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega)
\le
\min\big\{
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\mathcal{C}),\,
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\mathcal{N}),\,
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\mathcal{R}_\omega),\,
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\mathcal{S}_\omega)
\big\},
\end{equation}
with each term the radiative dimension of the restricted map on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
If \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\mathcal{R}_\omega)\ll r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\mathcal{S}_\omega)\), reactive contamination
dominates before listing \cite{harrington2001}; if \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\mathcal{C})\ll r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\mathcal{N}\mathcal{R}_\omega)\),
the listing is the bottleneck \cite{hansen1988}.
Moore--Penrose synthesis is the least-squares feed solution on declared hardware: it finds the best admissible current for a target pattern without claiming the target is exactly reachable \cite{harrington2001}. Residual norm separates ``closest realizable pattern'' from ``exact match''---a distinction every synthesis report must include \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.chain-bottleneck}
The factor achieving the minimum in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.chain-factor-ranks} is the dominant synthesis bottleneck at
tolerance \(\varepsilon\) \cite{harrington2001}. Refining MoM meshes on \(\mathcal{S}_\omega\) alone
does not raise \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega)\) when \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) or \(\mathcal{C}\) is the minimizer
\subsection{Spherical source enclosure and radiative rank at fixed electrical size}
\label{sec:ch43.signature-sphere}
% HARRINGTON-OPEN:sec:ch43.signature-sphere
A pyramidal horn over a square ground plane at $ka\approx1$ is routinely enclosed in an analysis sphere before multipole coefficients are reported on a compact range \cite{hansen1988}. Enclosing spheres convert irregular feeds into a standard multipole analysis; radius $a$ sets the export bandwidth before aperture or probe limits enter \cite{stratton1941}. Maxwell's retarded export on the declared ball remains the governing constraint \cite{maxwell1873}.
Enclosing spheres convert irregular sources into a standard multipole analysis \cite{stratton1941}. Radius sets the analysis bandwidth \cite{hansen1988}.
Enclose all impressed currents in a sphere $B_a$ of radius $a$. Compact support forces $\mathcal{R}_\omega$ to be compact on $\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$; singular values $\sigma_n$ of $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ decay beyond index $n\sim (k_0 a)^2$ in aligned gauge \cite{stratton1941}. Radius $a$ therefore sets radiative rank at fixed electrical size before aperture or probe limits act:
Enclosing spheres standardize multipole analysis; $r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\lesssim C(k_0 a)^2$ \cite{stratton1941}. Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.signature-sphere-rank} is the spherical rank bound \cite{devaney2004}.
\medskip
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch43.signature-sphere

On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Enclosing spheres convert irregular sources into a standard multipole analysis . Radius sets the analysis bandwidth .

\subsection{Singular values and radiative dimension}
\label{sec:ch434}
Singular values rank radiative channels by watts aligned with each pattern \cite{harrington2001}. They diagnose hardware; they do not create channels \cite{stratton1941}.
Singular values of $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ form the decaying ladder that $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ counts \cite{hansen1988}. MoM mesh refinement cannot lift a bottleneck set by export or listing operators \cite{harrington2001}.
\medskip
Compactness forces singular values to decay; decay defines how many synthesis directions matter at tolerance
\(\varepsilon\) \cite{stratton1941}. Subsection~\ref{sec:ch434} names that count
\emph{radiative dimension} and ties it to \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) \cite{hansen1988}.
Let \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega:\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\to\ell^{2}(I)\) be compact with singular values
\(\{\sigma_{n}\}_{n\ge1}\) ordered \(\sigma_{1}\ge\sigma_{2}\ge\cdots\to0\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Tolerance \(\varepsilon>0\) is declared for synthesis or inversion \cite{devaney2004}.
Regarding the singular value decomposition, there exist orthonormal source modes \(\{\mathbf{j}_{n}\}\subset\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) and coefficient modes
\(\{\mathbf{e}_{n}\}\subset\ell^{2}(I)\) such that

The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Singular values rank export directions by watts aligned with each pattern . They diagnose hardware; they do not create channels .
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.

\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.gamma-SVD}
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega
=
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{n}\,
|\mathbf{j}_{n}\rangle\langle\mathbf{e}_{n}|,
\qquad
\sigma_{n}\to0.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.gamma-SVD} uses Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.singular-value-decay} for
\(K=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) \cite{stratton1941}. \(\mathbf{j}_{n}\) are
\emph{left} synthesis modes: unit feed along \(\mathbf{j}_{n}\) produces coefficient \(\sigma_{n}\mathbf{e}_{n}\)
\cite{harrington2001}. Truncating at \(\sigma_{N}\) discards synthesis directions below export gain
\(\sigma_{N}\) \cite{hansen1988}.
\phantomsection\label{def:ch4.radiative-rank}
The radiative rank $r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)$ counts independent far-zone patterns the source--radiation map can populate above tolerance $\varepsilon$---the operator-theoretic name for the synthesis ceiling introduced in Â§\ref{sec:ch401}. 
For tolerance \(\varepsilon>0\), the \emph{radiative dimension} of \((\Omega_{s},\omega,\mathcal{C})\) is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.radiative-rank-def}
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)
=
\max\left\{
N:\ \sigma_{N}\big(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\big)\ge\varepsilon\,\sigma_{1}\big(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\big)
\right\}.
\end{equation}
Physically, singular values rank radiative channels by aligned watt content on $\Gamma_R$, not by matrix row count in a mode coefficient listing \cite{harrington2001}. rank cliffs appear as how many independent beam shapes can be synthesized before residual mismatch floors on a compact range \cite{devaney2004}. compare $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ to probe count $N$ in every campaign \cite{harrington2001}. integrate tail watts above declared $\varepsilon$ \cite{poynting1884}.
Radiative rank is the effective dimension of \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) at relative tolerance
\(\varepsilon\) \cite{harrington2001}. It is not the same as \(|I_{\ell_{\max}}|\): many listed modes may
have \(\sigma_{n}<\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\) and therefore lie outside the set of patterns a source can radiate at the declared tolerance
\cite{hansen1988}. \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}\) is the Chapter~4 name for the singular-value threshold
established in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch213} \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.radiative-rank}
For every \(\varepsilon\in(0,1)\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.rank-synthesis-bound}
\dim\Big\{\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C}):
\|\mathbf{c}\|\ge\varepsilon\|\mathbf{c}_{\max}\|\Big\}
\le
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon),
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{c}_{\max}\) a maximizer of \(\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp]\|\) over \(\|\Jimp\|=1\) in
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Moreover,
Spectral truncation is not chosen for numerical convenience on passive hardware at fixed $\omega$: high angular orders are suppressed when $k_0 D_s$ is small. The subsection makes that suppression quantitative. \cite{stratton1941}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.rank-error-bound}
\inf_{\mathrm{rank}\,R\le r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)}\|
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega-R\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
\varepsilon\,\sigma_{1}\big(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\big),
\end{equation}
linking radiative dimension to best low-rank synthesis approximation \cite{devaney2004}.
Inequality Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.rank-synthesis-bound} follows because significant \(\mathbf{c}\) lie in the span of
\(\{\mathbf{e}_{n}\}_{n\le r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)}\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.gamma-SVD}
\cite{stratton1941}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.rank-error-bound} is the Eckart--Young specialization of
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch2.singular-value-decay} with \(K=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) and \(N=r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\)
\cite{harrington2001}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.radiative-rank}) elevates singular values from diagnostic to definitional: \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}\) is
the count of independent realizability directions at tolerance \(\varepsilon\) \cite{hansen1988}. It tightens
Corollary~\ref{cor:ch4.channel-count} by replacing MoM patch count \(P\) with export-weighted rank of the continuum
operator \cite{harrington2001}. Section~\ref{sec:ch46} identifies \(r_{\mathrm{rad}}\) with effective
electromagnetic dimension.
Regarding relation to null space, modes with \(\sigma_{n}=0\) span \(\ker\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega^{\dagger}\): targets with component along those
modes are not realizable \cite{stratton1941}. Combined with \(\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.rank-null-composition}
\ker\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega
\supseteq
\ker\mathcal{R}_\omega\cap\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}},
\end{equation}
so synthesis nullity is at least non-radiating nullity \cite{harrington2001}. Radiative rank counts
\emph{nonzero} singular values above threshold, not kernel dimension alone \cite{devaney2004}.
Effective dimension. Radiative rank is the precursor of \emph{effective electromagnetic dimension} developed in
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch464} \cite{harrington2001}:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.effective-dimension}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)
=
r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)
=
\#\big\{n:\ \sigma_{n}\ge\varepsilon\,\sigma_{1}\big\},
\end{equation}
Physically, $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ counts singular directions whose aligned watt fraction exceeds $\varepsilon$ of the leading mode---a tolerance tied to noise floors and calibration, not an abstract cut \cite{harrington2001}. how many independent beam shapes can be synthesized before residual mismatch floors \cite{hansen1988}. declare $\varepsilon$ alongside probe count $N$; it is not counting chart indices or array elements \cite{harrington2001}. Measurement: tail watt integration on $\Gamma_R$ \cite{poynting1884}.
counting independent synthesis directions at tolerance \(\varepsilon\) \cite{stratton1941}. Equation
\eqref{eq:ch4.effective-dimension} replaces informal mode-count claims in design reports \cite{devaney2004}.
Discrete--continuum rank gap. Mesh refinement increases \(\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}\) monotonically but converges to
\(r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)\) only when \(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{cpl}}=1\) and patch spacing satisfies sampling limits
\cite{harrington2001}. Define the rank gap
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.rank-gap}
\Delta r_{\mathrm{gap}}
=
\operatorname{rank}\mathbf{T}-r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon),
\end{equation}
with \(\Delta r_{\mathrm{gap}}\ge0\) and \(\Delta r_{\mathrm{gap}}\to0\) under standard RWG refinement on compact
\(\Omega_{s}\) \cite{harrington2001}.
Radiative rank $r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)$ counts independent far-zone directions above tolerance on the export map $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ \cite{harrington2001}. It is not element count, not mesh nodes, and not the length of a complete mode chart \cite{stratton1941}. On a circular aperture at $k_0 a=3$, rank $\sim (k_0 a)^2$ is the watt-distinguishable export budget \cite{jackson1999}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.rank-gap-aliasing}
If \(\Delta r_{\mathrm{gap}}>0\) at fixed \((\Omega_{s},\omega,\varepsilon)\), then at least
\(\Delta r_{\mathrm{gap}}\) singular directions of \(\mathbf{T}\) have \(\sigma_{n}<\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\) in the
continuum \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) gauge \cite{stratton1941}. Those directions are
mesh-aliased synthesis artifacts, not export modes \cite{devaney2004}.
As a worked example, fix a circular disk $\Omega_{s}=\{|\mathbf{r}_{\perp}|\le a\}$ with electrical size $k_{0}a$
\cite{stratton1941}.
The radiation map $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega:\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\to\ell^{2}(I)$ is compact on
$\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$ \cite{harrington2001}.
Singular values $\{\sigma_{n}\}_{n\ge1}$ ordered $\sigma_{1}\ge\sigma_{2}\ge\cdots$ decay rapidly once
$n\gtrsim(k_{0}a)^{3}$ in volume-like scaling \cite{hansen1988}.
The supported angular ceiling satisfies $\ell_{\max}\sim k_{0}a$ on $B_{a}$ per
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} \cite{hansen1988}.
For $k_{0}a=2.5$ and $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$, one finds $r_{\mathrm{rad}}\approx9$--$11$ while the VSH chart still
lists hundreds of labels \cite{harrington2001}.
This is the operator picture behind Slepian concentration, Harrington source degrees of freedom, and Landau mode
counting on finite domains \cite{slepian1961}.
