\(\|\mathbf{r}_{\perp}\|/\|\mathbf{c}\|=0.15\) at \(\varepsilon=10^{-2}\) fails synthesis despite MoM residual \(10^{-4}\) and
\(\kappa(\mathbf{T}_{11})=120\) \cite{harrington2001}.
Inversion is a variational problem on \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\); synthesis is a membership problem on \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Every pattern-synthesis chain must terminate on Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.range-certificate}, not on variational
cost \cite{hansen1988}. \(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}=0\) is the synthesis test; MoM residual, adjoint score, and Tikhonov norm are diagnostics only
\cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.tikhonov-unreachable}
If \(\mathbf{r}_{\perp}\neq0\), no \(\alpha>0\) yields \(\delta_{\mathrm{reg}}=0\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tikhonov-synthesis-gap}
\cite{harrington2001}.
A square array target specifies $\widehat{c}_{8,2}^{(\mathbf{N})}=0.9$ on a centered dipole feed at $k_{0}a=0.8$. A method-of-moments inversion achieves $\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{I}_{s}-\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\|=3\times10^{-5}$ yet $\|\mathbf{r}_{\perp}\|=0.85\|\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\|$ in the decomposition Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.inversion-residual-decomposition} \cite{harrington2001}, with centered feed, open region, and aligned gauge. The residual is small in chart norm because the variational fit achieves a listed direction, yet $\mathbf{r}_{\perp}\neq0$ confirms $\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}>0$ \cite{devaney2004}. Numerical closeness is not synthesizable; synthesis reports must decompose range and null components before claiming success \cite{stratton1941}.
\subsection{Localization versus angular resolution}
\label{sec:ch474}
Localization and angular resolution pull the same finite support in opposite directions on $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$ \cite{stratton1941}. A electrically small outline cannot simultaneously concentrate $\mathcal{L}_{2}$ energy in a sub-wavelength footprint and populate high-$\ell$ sectors above laboratory tolerance---Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.localization-resolution-tradeoff} states the joint inequality that marketing copy often ignores \cite{harrington2001}. Representation dependence of angular labels in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch384} and localization bounds in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch364} constrain simultaneous spatial concentration and angular resolution; Subsection~\ref{sec:ch474} states the tradeoff as realizability inequalities on compact $\Omega_{s}$ \cite{devaney2004}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch474
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch474-b2
\begin{physicalvignette}[Slotted waveguide array in an aircraft radome]
A second bench observation matters on Dielectric lens fed by a scalar horn. When technicians swap only the receive probe while holding the transmit chain fixed, reciprocity checks whether the reported coupling is property of the structure or artifact of a mismatched flux gauge \cite{stratton1941}. Failed reciprocity usually traces to a near-field tap, a radome standing-wave, or a probe polarization basis that was never aligned with the transmit standard \cite{hansen1988}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
The measurement logic above is made precise in Subsection~\ref{sec:ch474} \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{physicalvignette}[Slotted waveguide array in an aircraft radome]
Consider Slotted waveguide array in an aircraft radome. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Localization and angular resolution cannot both be extreme on fixed support . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch474} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
When a product brief promises both extreme directivity and dominant orbital order on fixed metal, the $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ check begins with $\mathcal{L}_{2}$ and $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}$ on the declared support, not with synthesis settings \cite{collin2001}. Tight beams and high $\ell$ on the same support violate export tail laws unless both functionals are jointly verified \cite{hansen1988}. Phase retuning relabels harmonics within the same export cone; simultaneous localization and resolution are geometry-imposed, not post hoc \cite{stratton1941}. Fix compact $\Omega_{s}$, flux gauge on $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$, and tolerance $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$ \cite{jackson1999}; paraxial LG labels require Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.paraxial-vs-spherical-OAM} before VSH verification \cite{harrington2001}.
Define block angular resolution
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.angular-resolution-functional}
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}(\mathbf{c})=\sup\left\{\ell:\ \sum_{m,\sigma}|\widehat{c}_{\ell m}^{(\sigma)}|^{2}\ge\eta_{\mathrm{blk}}\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}^{2}\right\},
\end{equation}
the highest \(\ell\) carrying fraction \(\eta_{\mathrm{blk}}\) of radiative energy \cite{hansen1988}. On ball \(B_{a}\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.localization-resolution-tradeoff}
\mathcal{L}_{2}[\Jimp]\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}(\mathbf{c})\ \gtrsim\ \mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{lr}}(k_{0}a,\eta_{\mathrm{blk}}),
\end{equation}
using Subsection~\ref{sec:ch364} \cite{jackson1999}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.localization-resolution-tradeoff} forbids
arbitrarily tight \(\mathcal{L}_{2}\) with arbitrarily high \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\) on the same \(\Jimp\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.resolution-ceiling}
\(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}(\mathbf{c})\le\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)\) on \(B_{a}\) \cite{hansen1988}.
\(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) is the synthesis-side angular cutoff from singular-value ordering \cite{harrington2001}; \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\) counts
energy above \(\eta_{\mathrm{blk}}\) in \(\ell\) blocks \cite{hansen1988}. Tail laws cap nonzero \(\widehat{c}_{\ell m}\) for
\(\ell>\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) \cite{devaney2004}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.resolution-ceiling}) converts tail-law decay into an angular-order verification: \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\) cannot exceed the
synthesis-supported cutoff \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)\) regardless of mode chart or synthesis success \cite{hansen1988}.
Vendor orbital-mode claims must therefore be tested against \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) before localization inequalities are invoked \cite{devaney2004}.
on \(B_{a}\), \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)=\mathcal{O}(k_{0}a)\) per Section~\ref{sec:ch46} finite observable structures \cite{hansen1988}.
Electrically small \(k_{0}a<1\) supports only dipole-class \(\ell\) at practical \(\varepsilon\); super-hemispherical \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\) is excluded
\cite{jackson1999}.
Resolution ceiling is the finest angular detail a scan of radius $R$ at spacing $\Delta$ can resolve on $\Gamma_R$ \cite{harrington2001}. Sub-wavelength probe spacing alone cannot exceed radiative rank set by the source enclosure \cite{hansen1988}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.simultaneous-loc-ell}
\mathcal{L}_{2}[\Jimp]\cdot\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}(\mathbf{c})\ge\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{sl}}(k_{0}a,\eta_{\mathrm{blk}}),
\end{equation}
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.super-resolution-obstruction}
Simultaneous \(\mathcal{L}_{2}\ll a^{2}\) and \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\gg k_{0}a\) implies \(\mathbf{c}\notin\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) on \(B_{a}\)
\cite{hansen1988}.
Uncertainty product form. Combining Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.localization-resolution-tradeoff} and (\ref{thm:ch4.resolution-ceiling}),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.loc-res-uncertainty}
\mathcal{L}_{2}[\Jimp]\cdot\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)
\ge
\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{unc}}(k_{0}a,\varepsilon),
\end{equation}
an export-side uncertainty relation on compact supports \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.joint-loc-supp}
On \(B_{a}\), any \(\mathbf{c}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\) satisfies
\(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}(\mathbf{c})\le\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)\) and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.loc-res-uncertainty} simultaneously
\cite{hansen1988}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.resolution-ceiling}) and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.localization-resolution-tradeoff} \cite{hansen1988}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.joint-loc-supp}) is the synthesizable verification for simultaneous focusing and orbital-mode claims: both \(\mathcal{L}_{2}\) and
\(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\) must pass, not merely one \cite{harrington2001}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.beamwidth-ell-consistency}
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}(\mathbf{c})\le\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)\le\mathcal{O}(k_{0}a).
\end{equation}
Narrow beamwidth claims require Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.ell-max-scaling} before orbital order is established \cite{hansen1988}.
Representation map consistency check. Paraxial LG labels require Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.paraxial-vs-spherical-OAM} before \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\) tests \cite{harrington2001}.
Full-vector decomposition may spread one LG index across multiple \(\ell\) sectors \cite{hansen1988}. 
Joint localization and support bounds tie narrow beams to large apertures \cite{jackson1999}. 
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.lg-vsh-spread}
Paraxial mode \(m_{\mathrm{LG}}\) maps to VSH sectors \(\ell\in\{m_{\mathrm{LG}}-1,m_{\mathrm{LG}},m_{\mathrm{LG}}+1,\ldots\}\) after full-vector
decomposition; \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\) must be computed in VSH gauge \cite{harrington2001}.
Full-vector LG decomposition spreads azimuthal order across adjacent \(\ell\) sectors \cite{harrington2001}. \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\) is
defined on VSH coefficients after gauge conversion, not on paraxial labels alone \cite{hansen1988}.
on ball supports \(B_{a}\), supported order scales as \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)=\mathcal{O}(k_{0}a)\) per
(\ref{thm:ch4.resolution-ceiling}) \cite{hansen1988}. Claims with \(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ang}}\gg k_{0}a\) fail before
localization tests are applied \cite{stratton1941}.

\cite{stratton1941}.
\section{Scaling Laws and Spectral Concentration}
\label{sec:ch48}
Landau--Pollak--Slepian theory enters a Maxwell chapter as a description of finite information capacity on compact domains, not as a substitute for source radiation \cite{landau1962}. A dish of fixed diameter cannot simultaneously localize on its rim and on an arbitrarily narrow cone; a line array of fixed length cannot sustain unlimited angular sharpness without paying aperture or bandwidth \cite{harrington2001}. Singular-value decay and prolate mode counts measure how rapidly exportable information falls once geometry fixes support \cite{devaney2004}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch48
\begin{physicalvignette}[Reflector with serrated edge treatment]
Consider Reflector with serrated edge treatment. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch48} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
Scaling laws and concentration inequalities enter here because truncation is inevitable on real hardware: mode count grows with electrical area and volume; high-frequency asymptotics cross from dipole-class to volume-growth rank; concentration ratios bound how sharply geometry compresses what can be observed \cite{stratton1941}. The mathematics counts geometry-limited export; the physics is finite Maxwell sources on finite supports \cite{chu1948}.
Effective dimension $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ counts radiative channels; scaling exponents and concentration inequalities describe how that count grows with aperture, frequency, and spectral bandwidth \cite{stratton1941}. Section~\ref{sec:ch48} supplies asymptotic laws and concentration bounds that make truncation inevitable on finite hardware \cite{devaney2004}.
\subsection{Mode count versus aperture and frequency}
\label{sec:ch481}
Phased arrays and large reflectors illustrate mode-count scaling versus element sampling \cite{collin2001}.
Three integers often collide in array marketing: element count $N_{\mathrm{el}}$, analytic mode budget $N_{\mathrm{mode}}$, and measured radiative rank $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$. Elements sample the aperture; modes bound export cardinality; $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ counts directions above $\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$ on the declared gauge \cite{collin2001}.
Mode-count laws connect aperture area, wavelength, and integer budgets in array and MIMO design \cite{collin2001}.
Mode-count, asymptotic, concentration, and n-width accompany measurement campaigns alongside $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ \cite{landau1962}.
\medskip
 Elements sample the aperture; modes bound export cardinality; \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) counts directions above \(\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\) \cite{collin2001}. Confusing the three produces hardware budgets that exceed radiative information capacity \cite{devaney2004}.
$N_{\mathrm{el}}$, $N_{\mathrm{mode}}$, and $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ answer different questions \cite{collin2001}. Publish all with $(k_0 D_s,\varepsilon)$ \cite{devaney2004}.
\medskip
Volume and area laws ceiling listing cardinality:
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch481

Consider Compact antenna test range with quiet zone probe. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Mode-count laws cap how many independent channels geometry can host . Element count samples; area and wavelength set the budget .
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch481} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.mode-count-volume}
N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(vol)}}(\varepsilon)\ \le\ C_{V}(\varepsilon)\,(k_{0}a)^{3},
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}
N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}}(\varepsilon)\ \le\ C_{A}(\varepsilon)\,k_{0}^{2}A_{a},
\end{equation}
with \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) as analytic ceiling in aligned gauge \cite{collin2001}.
Equations~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-volume}--\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area} are Weyl-type counting bounds. In antenna terms, they limit how many
independent propagating channels the geometry can support at tolerance \(\varepsilon\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.mode-count-monotone}
At fixed \(\varepsilon\), \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(vol)}}\) and \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}}\) are non-decreasing in \(k_{0}\) and aperture size
until dispersion alters \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Phase-only tuning does not raise \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}\)
\cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.mode-ceiling-rank}
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\le N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(vol)}}(\varepsilon)\le C_{V}(\varepsilon)(k_{0}a)^{3}\) and
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\le N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}}(\varepsilon)\le C_{A}(\varepsilon)k_{0}^{2}A_{a}\) on aligned gauge
\cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{def:ch4.effective-em-dimension}) and Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-volume-scaling}, \eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-area-scaling}
\cite{stratton1941}. Radiative rank cannot exceed mode-count ceiling \cite{devaney2004}.
Volume and area mode-count laws cap how many independent radiation channels geometry can host at fixed $\omega$ \cite{collin2001}. \emph{Source geometry:} compact resonators obey $(k_{0}a)^{3}$ scaling; planar arrays obey $k_{0}^{2}A_{a}$ \cite{jackson1999}. The physical conclusion is that element count cannot exceed geometry-imposed $N_{\mathrm{mode}}$ \cite{harrington2001}.
A line array of width $W$ at fixed $k_{0}$ obeys mode-count ceilings of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}:
declaring forty independent spatial streams in commercial MIMO claims without reporting $\mathrm{EPR}$ or $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ repeats
a listing cardinality that geometry has not populated \cite{collin2001}.
Concentration ratios and $n$-widths express the same fact in analytic form: enlarging spatial extent trades against
spectral bandwidth on finite hardware \cite{devaney2004}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.mode-ceiling-rank}) is the array honesty law: manufacturer stream counts must be compared to \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}\) and measured
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\), not to element count alone \cite{collin2001}. Volume and area ceilings are not interchangeable:
compact three-dimensional sources are bounded by Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-volume}, while planar apertures obey
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area} \cite{jackson1999}. Synthesis checks must declare which geometry class governs the
declared \(\Omega_{s}\) \cite{harrington2001}.
for thin apertures with thickness \(h\ll a\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.vol-area-dominance}
N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(vol)}}\approx\frac{4\pi}{3}\frac{h}{a}\,N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}},
\end{equation}
so area law typically sets the operative ceiling on panel arrays while volume law governs compact resonators \cite{collin2001}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.mode-rank-gap}
\Delta_{\mathrm{mr}}=N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\varepsilon)-d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\ \ge\ 0
\end{equation}
measures listed modes below synthesis floor \cite{devaney2004}. Large \(\Delta_{\mathrm{mr}}\) with small \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) signals over-listing
\cite{stratton1941}.
Mode-ceiling rank aligns Weyl-type counts with singular-value rank on $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ \cite{hansen1988}. When the two disagree, gauge, support class, or tolerance calibration record is inconsistent \cite{devaney2004}.
Mode count grows with electrical area and volume---the same counting that limits MIMO diversity on finite panels \cite{stratton1941}. Monotone laws prevent citing element count as radiative rank \cite{jackson1999}.
Engineering margin applies to mode budget, not to rank:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.mode-count-margin}
N_{\mathrm{design}}=\lfloor\eta_{\mathrm{margin}}\,N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\varepsilon)\rfloor,\qquad 0<\eta_{\mathrm{margin}}<1.
\end{equation}
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.mode-margin-monotone}
\(N_{\mathrm{design}}\) is non-decreasing in \(k_{0}\) and aperture at fixed \(\eta_{\mathrm{margin}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.element-mode-inequality}
N_{\mathrm{el}}\ge N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\varepsilon)\ \not\Rightarrow\ d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)=N_{\mathrm{el}},
\end{equation}
because \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) is radiative rank fixed by geometry, not element count \cite{collin2001}. Oversampled MoM bases inflate \(N_{\mathrm{el}}\)
without raising \(\sigma_{d_{\mathrm{eff}}+1}/\sigma_{1}\) above \(\varepsilon\) \cite{hansen1988}.
Frequency scaling. At fixed aperture area \(A_{a}\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.mode-freq-scaling}
\frac{N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\omega_{2})}{N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\omega_{1})}=\left(\frac{\omega_{2}}{\omega_{1}}\right)^{2}.
\end{equation}
Doubling frequency quadruples area-law mode budget at fixed footprint \cite{jackson1999}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.stream-bound}
Deployable MIMO streams satisfy \(N_{\mathrm{stream}}\le\min(N_{\mathrm{design}},d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon))\) on declared \((\Omega_{s},k_{0},\varepsilon)\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
\(N_{\mathrm{design}}\) is an engineering truncation of \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}\); deployable streams cannot exceed measured \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\)
on \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) \cite{harrington2001}. Margin lowers listing; rank sets export \cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{lem:ch4.stream-bound}) is the MIMO realizability bound: manufacturer stream counts above \(\min(N_{\mathrm{design}},d_{\mathrm{eff}})\) are
listing claims, not physics guarantees \cite{collin2001}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.rank-ka-monotone}
At fixed \(\varepsilon\) and aperture class, \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) is non-decreasing in \(k_{0}a\) until material dispersion alters
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Singular values \(\sigma_{n}\) of \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) are non-decreasing in \(k_{0}a\) on fixed aperture class until dispersion alters
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{hansen1988}. \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) counts modes above \(\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.rank-ka-monotone}) pairs with (\ref{thm:ch4.mode-count-monotone}): electrical enlargement raises listing ceilings and measured
rank together, but \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\le N_{\mathrm{mode}}\) always \cite{collin2001}.
Rank increases monotonically with $k_0 a$ on enclosed supports \cite{jackson1999}. Doubling frequency at fixed outline doubles electrical size and typically raises $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$---the explanation when a band edge admits high-$\ell$ marketing claims \cite{stratton1941}.

\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.phase-no-mode-gain}
At fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0},\varepsilon)\), leaves \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\varepsilon)\) and \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) unchanged
\cite{devaney2004}.
A rectangular panel of area $A_{a}$ at $k_{0}\sqrt{A_{a}/\pi}=2.5$ carries $48$ patch elements. With planar aperture class and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}, $k_{0}^{2}A_{a}\approx6.25$ gives $N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}}\lesssim20$ at $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; measured $d_{\mathrm{eff}}\approx12<48$ \cite{collin2001}. Elements sample the aperture; geometry sets the mode budget \cite{stratton1941}. Forty-eight feeds do not imply forty-eight independent far-zone channels \cite{harrington2001}.
\subsection{High- and low-frequency asymptotics}
\label{sec:ch482}
At low electrical size, the Hertzian dipole is the reference rank-one limit: multipole moments with $\ell\ge 2$ are suppressed by $(k_0 D_s)^{2\ell+1}$ until support or frequency enlarges the body \cite{jackson1999}. a compact planar radiator at $k_0 a\ll 1$ cannot honestly claim high-$\ell$ diversity---radiative rank saturates near dipole-class cardinality before any synthesis attempt \cite{harrington2001}.
Rayleigh suppression leaves $\ell=1$ dominance at low $k_0 D_s$ \cite{jackson1999}. High frequency opens more sectors on fixed support \cite{hansen1988}.
\medskip
As \(k_{0}D_{s}\to0\), multipole moments with \(\ell\ge1\) are suppressed by \((k_{0}D_{s})^{2\ell+1}\) \cite{jackson1999}. Radiative rank
saturates at dipole-class cardinality:
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch482
\begin{physicalvignette}[Outdoor elevated range with boresight tower]
Consider Outdoor elevated range with boresight tower. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Low frequency is dipole-class; large electrical size accesses volume-growth diversity . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch482} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.rayleigh-rank-limit}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\ \longrightarrow\ d_{0}\le3
\end{equation}
in the electric-dipole gauge \cite{rayleigh1871}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.rayleigh-rank-limit} is the small-aperture synthesis law: no
passive \(\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) can excite more than three independent far-zone directions when the body is electrically tiny
\cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.rayleigh-dipole-cap}
For \(k_{0}D_{s}<0.5\) on compact \(\Omega_{s}\), \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\le3\) for any \(\varepsilon\in(0,1)\) in the electric-dipole
gauge \cite{jackson1999}.
Leading multipole weights scale as \((k_{0}D_{s})^{2\ell+1}\) \cite{jackson1999}. For \(k_{0}D_{s}<0.5\), only \(\ell=0,1\) sectors can exceed
\(\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\) in (\ref{def:ch4.effective-em-dimension}) \cite{stratton1941}.
Multipole rank table (Rayleigh band). Expanding the far field in vector spherical harmonics,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.multipole-rank-record}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\le\sum_{\ell=0}^{\ell_{\max}}\sum_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}\!\left[\sigma_{(\ell,\cdot,\sigma)}\ge\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\right],
\end{equation}
with \(\ell\in\{0,1\}\) dominant when \(k_{0}D_{s}<0.5\) \cite{jackson1999}. Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.multipole-rank-record}
makes the dipole cap constructive: higher multipoles are below threshold before any synthesis attempt \cite{harrington2001}.
Large-body limit. As \(k_{0}D_{s}\to\infty\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.large-body-rank-growth}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\ \sim\ C_{\infty}(\varepsilon)\,(k_{0}D_{s})^{3},
\end{equation}
matching Weyl volume law Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-volume-scaling} up to prefactor \(C_{\infty}(\varepsilon)\) \cite{stratton1941}.
when \(k_{0}D_{s}>2\), rank increments track volume mode density on \(B_{D_{s}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Rank is not tunable by
feed phasing on fixed \(\Omega_{s}\) \cite{devaney2004}. Truncation follows from
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\sim(k_{0}D_{s})^{3}\) growth \cite{hansen1988}.
Rayleigh dipole cap is the elementary limit: at $k_0 a\ll 1$, rank $\le 3$ regardless of harmonic listing cardinality \cite{jackson1999}. Every truncation claim in the chapter must reduce to this limit on electrically small hardware \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.asymptotic-regimes}
Small hardware is dipole-limited; large hardware is volume-growth limited with mandatory truncation \cite{stratton1941}.
Electrical crossover occurs when the first multipole beyond dipole class exceeds \(\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\):
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.crossover-frequency}
k_{0}^{(\mathrm{cross})}D_{s}\sim\mathcal{O}(1),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.regime-table}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\in\begin{cases}\{1,2,3\}, & k_{0}D_{s}<0.5,\\ \mathcal{O}((k_{0}D_{s})^{3}), & k_{0}D_{s}>2,\end{cases}
\end{equation}
The laboratory situation is a bounded source at fixed $\omega$ observed on $\Gamma_R$ with declared flux gauge. The relation below connects that situation to the coefficient description inherited from Chapter~3. \cite{stratton1941}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.crossover-knee}
\kappa_{\mathrm{cross}}=\inf\left\{k_{0}D_{s}:\ d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)>3\right\}.
\end{equation}
Transition band \(0.5<k_{0}D_{s}<2\) requires direct SVD verification; neither asymptote alone is reliable \cite{hansen1988}.

At fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0},\varepsilon)\), leaves the active asymptotic branch in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.regime-table} unchanged

For \(k_{0}D_{s}>2\), replacing Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.large-body-rank-growth} with Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.rayleigh-rank-limit} underestimates

\emph{Problem 4.8.2.} Compact loop antenna; enclosing diameter $D_{s}=0.12\,\mathrm{m}$; narrowband phasors; passive linear exterior; $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; aligned flux gauge. Use Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.rayleigh-rank-limit}, \eqref{eq:ch4.regime-table}, and \eqref{eq:ch4.crossover-knee}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] Estimate $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})$ at $1\,\mathrm{GHz}$ and classify the active asymptotic branch.
\item[(b)] Repeat at $20\,\mathrm{GHz}$ on the same mechanical outline.
\item[(c)] Locate $\kappa_{\mathrm{cross}}$ and show why neither Rayleigh nor volume law alone is reliable in the transition band.
\end{enumerate}
For part~(a), At $1\,\mathrm{GHz}$, $k_{0}D_{s}\approx0.4<0.5$; (\ref{thm:ch4.rayleigh-dipole-cap}) gives $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})\le3$ in the electric-dipole gauge. The active branch is dipole-class per Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.regime-table}.
For part~(b), At $20\,\mathrm{GHz}$, $k_{0}D_{s}\approx8>2$; Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.large-body-rank-growth} predicts $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})\sim\mathcal{O}((k_{0}D_{s})^{3})$---order $10^{2}$ radiative channels before scan compression. Mechanical CAD is unchanged; electrical size is not.
For part~(c), Subsection data give $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})=\{2,2,2,3,8,22\}$ at $\{1,3,6,10,20,40\}\,\mathrm{GHz}$ with $\kappa_{\mathrm{cross}}$ near $10\,\mathrm{GHz}$. For $0.5<k_{0}D_{s}<2$, direct SVD verification is mandatory.

\label{sec:ch483}

On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. \begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.spectral-concentration-ratio}
\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}(\varepsilon)=\frac{\sum_{n=1}^{d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)}\sigma_{n}^{2}}{\sum_{n\ge1}\sigma_{n}^{2}}\ \ge\ 1-\varepsilon^{2},
\end{equation}
\cite{harrington2001}. \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}\) is the fraction of \(\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\)
Reflectarrays and metasurfaces demonstrate phase-only steering within fixed rank cones \cite{harrington2001}.
carried by the leading \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) singular values. In physical terms, it is the fraction of exportable radiative information retained after rank-\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\)
truncation \cite{devaney2004}.
the discarded export fraction is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.tail-discard}
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{tail}}(\varepsilon)=1-\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}(\varepsilon)=\frac{\sum_{n>d_{\mathrm{eff}}}\sigma_{n}^{2}}{\sum_{n\ge1}\sigma_{n}^{2}}.
\end{equation}
Slow singular-value tails (\(\sigma_{n}\sim n^{-\alpha}\), \(\alpha\le2\)) keep \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{tail}}\) large even when \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) is modest
\cite{devaney2004}. Flat tails are the concentration analogue of coupling-tail mass
\(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{cpl}}\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.coupling-tail-mass} \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.truncation-concentration}
Rank-\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) truncation maximizes \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}(\varepsilon)\) among all rank-\(N\) approximations of
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) \cite{harrington2001}.
Eckart--Young theorem: best rank-\(N\) approximation minimizes discarded tail energy \cite{harrington2001}. (\ref{def:ch4.effective-em-dimension})
selects \(N=d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) at tolerance \(\varepsilon\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.eckart-young-conc}
Among all rank-\(N\) approximations of \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\), truncation at \(N=d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) minimizes
\(\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{tail}}(\varepsilon)\) and maximizes \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}(\varepsilon)\) \cite{harrington2001}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.export-participation}
\mathrm{EPR}(\varepsilon)=\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}(\varepsilon),\qquad 1-\mathrm{EPR}(\varepsilon)=\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{tail}}(\varepsilon).
\end{equation}
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.optimal-participation}
Rank-\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) truncation maximizes \(\mathrm{EPR}(\varepsilon)\) among rank-\(N\) maps on \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\)
\cite{harrington2001}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.optimal-participation}) is the watt-closure test: a synthesis report that reports \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) without
\(\mathrm{EPR}\) omits how much radiative information was sacrificed \cite{hansen1988}.
Tolerance \(\varepsilon\) locates the knee
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.sv-knee}
\sigma_{d_{\mathrm{eff}}}/\sigma_{1}\ge\varepsilon,\qquad \sigma_{d_{\mathrm{eff}}+1}/\sigma_{1}<\varepsilon.
\end{equation}
Knee location sets both \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) and \(\mathrm{EPR}\) simultaneously \cite{hansen1988}.
Per-mode participation. Define \(\pi_{n}=\sigma_{n}^{2}/\sum_{k}\sigma_{k}^{2}\). Flat \(\{\pi_{n}\}\) implies large \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{tail}}\) at small rank increment
\cite{devaney2004}. Steep knees permit high \(\mathrm{EPR}\) at modest \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) \cite{harrington2001}.
if \(\sigma_{n}\le C n^{-\alpha}\) for \(n\ge n_{0}\), then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.sv-decay-bound}
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{tail}}(\varepsilon)\le C'\,d_{\mathrm{eff}}^{1-2\alpha}\quad(\alpha>1/2),
\end{equation}
linking tail flatness to slow rank convergence \cite{hansen1988}. Algebraic tails require higher \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) for
\(\mathrm{EPR}\ge0.99\) than exponential tails at the same \(\varepsilon\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.conc-monotone}
\(\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}(\varepsilon)\) is non-decreasing as \(\varepsilon\) decreases (rank \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) increases)
\cite{harrington2001}.

Compression--concentration alignment. Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-compression-def} caps angular listing; Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-concentration-ratio} caps energy participation
\cite{hansen1988}. A pattern can satisfy \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) yet fail \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}\ge1-\varepsilon^{2}\) when
tail modes carry non-negligible power \cite{devaney2004}. Both must appear in synthesis reports \cite{stratton1941}.
Reflectarrays and metasurfaces demonstrate phase-only steering within fixed rank cones \cite{harrington2001}.
\(\varepsilon\in\{10^{-1},10^{-2},10^{-3}\}\Rightarrow(d_{\mathrm{eff}},\mathrm{EPR})=(2,0.91),(5,0.97),(12,0.995)\) on \(k_{0}a=2.5\) aperture
\cite{hansen1988}.
Finite support forces a singular-value table whether or not the designer computes it \cite{stratton1941}. Truncation is
not a post hoc modeling choice; it is the unique rank-\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) approximation minimizing discarded export energy per
(\ref{thm:ch4.eckart-young-conc}) \cite{devaney2004}. Claims of full harmonic synthesis without \(\mathrm{EPR}\) verification confuse coordinate
cardinality with energy concentration \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.tail-dominated}
If \(\sigma_{d_{\mathrm{eff}}+1}/\sigma_{d_{\mathrm{eff}}}>1-\varepsilon\), then \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{tail}}(\varepsilon)>\varepsilon^{2}\) despite minimal rank increment
\cite{hansen1988}.
\emph{Problem 4.8.3.} Circular aperture on $B_{a}$; $k_{0}a=2$; tolerance $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; singular-value table of $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ in aligned gauge. Use Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-concentration-ratio}, \eqref{eq:ch4.export-participation}, and (\ref{thm:ch4.eckart-young-conc}).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] From the knee Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.sv-knee}, estimate $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})$.
\item[(b)] Compute $\mathrm{EPR}(\varepsilon)=\Lambda_{\mathrm{conc}}(\varepsilon)$ when the first eight modes carry $95\%$ of $\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}$.
\item[(c)] Explain why listing $\ell\le15$ while $\mathrm{EPR}\approx0.95$ at rank eight is not a contradiction.
\end{enumerate}
For part~(a), Tail decay on $k_{0}a=2$ places the $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$ knee near $d_{\mathrm{eff}}\approx5$--$8$. Subsection sweeps give $(d_{\mathrm{eff}},\mathrm{EPR})=(5,0.97)$ at $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$ on a comparable aperture.
For part~(b), $\mathrm{EPR}=0.95$ means $5\%$ of exportable energy sits in discarded tail modes per Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.tail-discard}. Rank-$8$ truncation is watt-optimal among rank-$8$ approximations by (\ref{thm:ch4.eckart-young-conc}).
For part~(c), Chart cardinality counts coordinate rows; $\mathrm{EPR}$ counts energy participation. Seven additional listed indices may sit below $\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$---listing width and supported order diverge once $\ell_{\max}\gg k_{0}a$.
\subsection{Landau-Pollak-Slepian limits and n-widths}
\label{sec:ch484}
% HARRINGTON-OPEN:sec:ch484
A biconical calibrator in a wideband chamber shows why aperture area and measured bandwidth jointly cap how many independent far-zone channels can be concentrated on finite support \cite{hansen1988}. Slepian and Landau bounds describe that geometry-limited concentration before any method-of-moments matrix is formed \cite{slepian1961}. Maxwell's retarded export on the declared domain remains the governing constraint \cite{maxwell1873}.
Slepian and Landau bounds fix simultaneous spatial and spectral limits on finite domains \cite{landau1962}. They describe geometry; Maxwell still radiates \cite{maxwell1873}.
Slepian, Pollak, and Landau \cite{slepian1961} asked how many functions can be simultaneously concentrated in a spatial interval and a band-limited frequency domain---the mathematical origin of finite effective dimension. Communication theory later imported the same concentration question; prolate spheroidal wave functions became the standard language for aperture-limited electromagnetic channels \cite{hansen1988}. In Chapter~\ref{ch:04}, $N_{\mathrm{LPS}}(\varepsilon,D,\Omega_k)$ is not a post hoc fit to MoM matrices: it is the classical concentration bound that Kolmogorov $n$-widths and radiative dimension inherit \cite{stratton1941}.
Landau--Pollak--Slepian (LPS) theory and Kolmogorov \(n\)-widths bound the number of functions simultaneously concentrated in a spatial domain
\(D\) and a spectral domain \(\Omega_{k}\) \cite{hansen1988}. Subsection~\ref{sec:ch484} uses those bounds as concentration ceilings
on \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\), unifying LPS, mode-count, and SVD bounds through Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.lps-export-ceiling}
\cite{harrington2001}. Concentration is the primitive fact; radiative rank fixed by geometry is the derived rank bound
\cite{stratton1941}.
Declare spatial domain \(D\supset\Omega_{s}\) and spectral domain \(\Omega_{k}\) for propagating content \cite{stratton1941}.
Use Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.duality-bandlimit-scaling} and linear time-harmonic maps \cite{harrington2001}. Tolerance \(\varepsilon\in(0,1)\)
fixed \cite{hansen1988}.
the export dimension is ceilinged by the concentration width
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch484
\begin{physicalvignette}[Biconical antenna for wideband chamber calibration]
Consider Biconical antenna for wideband chamber calibration. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Slepian and Landau bounds state simultaneous spatial and spectral limits on finite domains. They describe geometry; Maxwell still radiates.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch484} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.lps-nwidth-bound}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\ \le\ N_{\mathrm{LPS}}(\varepsilon,D,\Omega_{k}),
\end{equation}
where \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}\) counts approximate simultaneous eigenfunctions of spatial and spectral concentration operators on \((D,\Omega_{k})\)
\cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.lps-synthesis-bound}
\(\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})\le N_{\mathrm{LPS}}(\varepsilon,D,\Omega_{k})\) for any coefficient chart \(\mathcal{C}\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
The concentration operator on \((D,\Omega_{k})\) dominates the radiation map \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on compact supports: no passive
\(\Jimp\) can excite more than \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}\) approximately orthogonal radiative channels above noise floor \cite{hansen1988}. Definition
\ref{def:ch4.effective-em-dimension} counts those directions at \(\varepsilon\) \cite{harrington2001}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.lps-synthesis-bound}) explains why rank is finite before hardware enters: spatial--spectral concentration is a geometry-imposed
barrier independent of feed count \cite{hansen1988}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.lps-export-ceiling}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\ \le\ \min\big(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}(\varepsilon,D,\Omega_{k}),\,N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\varepsilon)\big),
\end{equation}
unifying LPS, mode-count laws Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-volume}--\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}, and SVD rank \cite{hansen1988}.
Eckart--Young concentration identifies which truncated subspace captures maximum export energy \cite{hansen1988}. Truncation at $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ is optimal in watt sense, not an arbitrary synthesis truncation \cite{stratton1941}.
Landau--Pollak--Slepian bounds cap time-bandwidth and spatial-bandwidth products on finite supports \cite{landau1962}. They show why super-directive arrays pay sensitivity and bandwidth penalties \cite{stratton1941}.
Optimal participation ratios rank which sectors deserve feed effort at fixed power budget \cite{harrington2001}. Feeding a suppressed tail sector wastes watts that geometry cannot convert to far-zone gain \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.triple-ceiling}
On declared \((D,\Omega_{k},\mathcal{C})\), if any two of \(\{d_{\mathrm{eff}},N_{\mathrm{LPS}},N_{\mathrm{mode}}\}\) exceed the third, then
\((D,\Omega_{k})\) were mis-declared, \(\mathcal{C}\) is over-listed, or gauge alignment failed \cite{hansen1988}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.lps-synthesis-bound}), (\ref{thm:ch4.mode-ceiling-rank}), and (\ref{def:ch4.effective-em-dimension}) form a
consistent triple on aligned domains \cite{stratton1941}.
$d_{\mathrm{eff}}$, $N_{\mathrm{LPS}}$, and $N_{\mathrm{mode}}$ must agree on declared geometry: finite support $\to$ finite information $\to$ finite angular dimension \cite{landau1962}. Misalignment signals over-listed charts or mis-declared spatial/spectral domains, not solver failure \cite{harrington2001}.
On anisotropic \(\Omega_{s}\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.lps-anisotropy}
N_{\mathrm{LPS}}(\varepsilon,D,\Omega_{k})\le\sum_{i=1}^{3}N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(i)}(\varepsilon,D_{i},\Omega_{k}^{(i)}),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.prolate-nwidth}
N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(\mathrm{major})}\sim(k_{0}a)^{3},\qquad N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(\mathrm{minor})}\sim(k_{0}b)^{3}.
\end{equation}
Major-axis modes activate before minor-axis modes on prolate and slot apertures \cite{hansen1988}.
on prolate \((a,b,c)\) with \(a\ge b\ge c\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.lps-directional-record}
N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(x)}\gtrsim(k_{0}a)^{3},\quad
N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(y)}\gtrsim(k_{0}b)^{3},\quad
N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(z)}\gtrsim(k_{0}c)^{3}.
\end{equation}
Isotropic \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}\) on anisotropic \(\Omega_{s}\) overstates minor-axis export \cite{harrington2001}.
One-dimensional PSWF concentration on \([-1,1]\) with bandwidth parameter \(c=k_{0}a\) yields \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(1\mathrm{D})}\sim c/\log c\)
\cite{hansen1988}. Embedding into three-dimensional prolate geometry recovers Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.prolate-nwidth} scaling
\cite{stratton1941}. EM radiation maps inherit the same barrier \cite{hansen1988}.
Triple ceiling packages synthesis, finite-support, and instrument limits into one reportable tuple \cite{stratton1941}. Omitting any leg produces headlines that contradict watt consistency on $\Gamma_R$ \cite{hansen1988}.

The measurement procedure runs: (i) declare \((D,\Omega_{k})\); (ii) compute directional \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(i)}\); (iii) compute \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}\);
(iv) measure \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) from SVD; (v) apply Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.lps-export-ceiling} and (\ref{thm:ch4.triple-ceiling})
\cite{hansen1988}.
Prolate spheroid \(k_{0}a=3\), \(k_{0}b=0.5\): \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(x)}\approx27\), \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(y)}\approx1\); major axis dominates
\cite{hansen1988}.
Slot \(10{:}1\), \(k_{0}a=2.5\): \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(x)}\approx18\), \(N_{\mathrm{LPS}}^{(y)}\approx3\), \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}}\approx22\),
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})=14\) \cite{hansen1988}.
Reflectarrays and metasurfaces demonstrate phase-only steering within fixed rank cones \cite{harrington2001}.
\(n\)-width bounds are not parallel to radiative rank fixed by geometry; they show why rank is finite on compact supports \cite{hansen1988}.
Spatial concentration and spectral bandlimit are conjucriterion resources: enlarging one shrinks the other in the LPS sense \cite{jackson1999}.
Synthesis dimension is a concentration fact before it is a hardware fact \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.lps-domain}
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}>N_{\mathrm{LPS}}(\varepsilon,D,\Omega_{k})\) on passive \(\Omega_{s}\) implies \((D,\Omega_{k})\) were mis-declared or
\(\mathcal{C}\) includes non-export rows \cite{hansen1988}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.finite-geometry-concentration}
For every compact source support $\Omega_{s}$ of characteristic size $D_{s}$ and every fixed frequency $\omega$,
the number of angular degrees of freedom capable of carrying appreciable radiated power scales asymptotically with
electrical size $k_{0}D_{s}$ \cite{stratton1941}.
Consequently no finite source can populate arbitrarily large angular orders with finite efficiency on passive
compact support \cite{devaney2004}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.lps-synthesis-bound}), (\ref{thm:ch4.mode-ceiling-rank}), and
(\ref{def:ch4.effective-em-dimension}) bound radiative dimension by concentration on $(D_{s},\Omega_{k})$
\cite{hansen1988}.
Tail laws Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} and~\eqref{eq:ch4.spectral-tail-energy} suppress high-$\ell$ weight at
fixed $k_{0}D_{s}$ \cite{hansen1988}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.finite-geometry-concentration}) is the statement a referee remembers: finite geometry fixes how
many angular directions can carry watts, independent of how many harmonics a complete chart displays
\cite{harrington2001}.
For a $\lambda/4$ circular aperture at $k_{0}a=\pi/2$, only low-$\ell$ sectors dominate exported power; claiming
forty independent angular channels without enlarging $a$ or $k_{0}$ contradicts the same concentration inequality
\cite{collin2001}.
Section~\ref{sec:ch48} has linked mode count, asymptotic regimes, spectral concentration, and LPS \(n\)-widths to \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\)
\cite{stratton1941}. Truncation is inevitable because every bound is finite at fixed tolerance.
Section~\ref{sec:ch49} records physical consequences for engineering and measurement on the same realizability theory \cite{stratton1941}.
Concentration inequalities show how sharply geometry compresses observable power into leading singular directions \cite{hansen1988}.
n-widths on $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ connect operator theory to range rank bounds \cite{landau1962}. Aligned gauge: $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ equals singular-value count above $\varepsilon\sigma_1$ \cite{stratton1941}.
\medskip
\section{Physical Consequences of $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ Limits}
\label{sec:ch49}
Truncation and rank are engineering facts before they are operator facts: they bound what finite apertures can radiate, what phase-only surfaces can retune, and what laboratories can report in watts on a declared sphere \cite{stratton1941}. Section~\ref{sec:ch49} collects the physical consequences of the synthesizable theory proved in Sections~\ref{sec:ch41}--\ref{sec:ch48} \cite{hansen1988}. Every design report that equates harmonic listing cardinality with radiative rank inherits inequalities already established on the geometry-limited export map \cite{stratton1941}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch49
\begin{physicalvignette}[Standard-gain horn trio for gain transfer]
Consider Standard-gain horn trio for gain transfer. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch49} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
Geometry buys electrical size, radiative rank, and multipole diversity as support and frequency increase \cite{stratton1941}. Geometry cannot buy unlimited angular complexity on fixed passive compact hardware at fixed $\omega$: phase retuning explores the same rank-limited export subspace; feed optimization cannot manufacture propagating channels the support never coupled \cite{devaney2004}.
Finite geometry implies finite radiative dimensionality; finite radiative dimensionality implies angular truncation; angular truncation implies inaccessible eigenstructures beyond the radiative rank of $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ \cite{chu1948}.
\subsection{Geometry as origin of dimensionality limits}
\label{sec:ch491}
Electrical size $k_0 D_s$ sets radiative rank on passive hardware; CAD outline alone does not \cite{stratton1941}. Phase rotations on fixed metal preserve singular-value ladders \cite{harrington2001}.
\medskip

On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Rank tracks electrical size, not mesh nodes or iteration count. 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.

\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.hardware-rank-map}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)=F\big(\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s}),k_{0},\varepsilon;\mathcal{C}\big),
\end{equation}
with \(F\) monotone in \(\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})\) and \(k_{0}\) per (\ref{thm:ch4.rank-ka-monotone}) \cite{stratton1941}.
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.hardware-rank-map} is the counting functional induced by \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\). On the range, this is the number
of independent far-zone directions a laboratory can excite above the noise floor at declared tolerance \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.geometry-rank-origin}
At fixed \(\varepsilon\) and listing \(\mathcal{C}\), \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) is invariant under phase-only
reparameterizations of \(\Jimp\) on fixed \(\Omega_{s}\) and changes only when \(\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})\), \(k_{0}\), or
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) class changes \cite{stratton1941}.
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) depends on support and material class through \(\mathcal{R}_\omega\) and \(\mathcal{C}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Phase rotations on fixed support are unitary gauge transformations on \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) that preserve singular values of the radiation map
\cite{hansen1988}. Enlarging \(\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})\) or raising \(k_{0}\) enlarges the set of propagating channels coupled by
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) until dispersion alters \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{jackson1999}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.geometry-rank-origin}) is the origin bound for hardware verifications: diversity saturation on unchanged outline is a geometry bound,
not a solver defect \cite{stratton1941}. Metasurfaces that retune phases without enlarging \(\mathcal{L}_{2}\) cannot raise \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\)
\cite{hansen1988}. Rank growth requires electrical enlargement---frequency, aperture, or material class---not mesh refinement alone
\cite{jackson1999}.
On fixed \(\Omega_{s}\) class,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.outline-trap}
\frac{\partial d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{\partial k_{0}}\Big|_{\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})=\text{const}}>0\quad\text{for }k_{0}D_{s}\gtrsim1.
\end{equation}
Mechanical CAD outline is invariant under band change, but \(k_{0}D_{s}\) is not \cite{jackson1999}. Rank reports must quote electrical
size, not enclosure volume alone \cite{hansen1988}.
Finite geometry is the origin of rank limits, not operator notation chosen for convenience \cite{maxwell1873}. Every inequality in Section~\ref{sec:ch49} expresses that Maxwell fact for laboratories \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.mesh-dof-trap}
FEM or MoM degrees of freedom \(N_{\mathrm{dof}}\) satisfy \(N_{\mathrm{dof}}\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) but \(N_{\mathrm{dof}}\gg d_{\mathrm{eff}}\)
does not imply rank growth on fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) \cite{harrington2001}.
Discretization enlarges the trial space for \(\Jimp\) without enlarging \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) on fixed compact support
\cite{harrington2001}. Singular values \(\sigma_{n}\) of \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) are properties of the continuum radiation map;
mesh refinement approximates them but cannot create new propagating channels on fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.cad-rank-decouple}
Identical mechanical outlines at different \(k_{0}\) generally yield different \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) via Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.joint-scaling-law}
\cite{stratton1941}.
Compact three-dimensional \(\Omega_{s}\) obey Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-volume}; planar apertures obey Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}
\cite{collin2001}. Declaring the wrong class over-estimates or under-estimates \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}\) and mis-attributes rank
saturation \cite{stratton1941}.

\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.diam-rank-monotone}
At fixed \(k_{0}\), \(\varepsilon\), and \(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) class, \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) is non-decreasing in \(\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
Enlarging \(\operatorname{diam}(\Omega_{s})\) enlarges coupled propagating channels in \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) until material dispersion alters
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) \cite{harrington2001}. \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) counts singular values above \(\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\), which cannot
decrease under support enlargement on fixed class \cite{hansen1988}.
MoM or FEM bases of size \(N_{\mathrm{basis}}\) approximate \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) with error \(\tau_{\mathrm{mom}}\) on \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\)
but cannot raise continuum rank on fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) \cite{harrington2001}. 
\(D_{s}=0.5\lambda,1.0\lambda,1.5\lambda\) at fixed \(k_{0}\): \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})\) increases monotonically per (\ref{thm:ch4.diam-rank-monotone})
\cite{stratton1941}.
Joint scaling Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.joint-scaling-law} combines volume and area ceilings; the operative bound is
\(\min\{C_{V}(k_{0}a)^{3},C_{A}k_{0}^{2}A_{a}\}\) on declared class \cite{stratton1941}.
Rank verifications that cite only mechanical area without \(k_{0}\) mis-state electrical mode budget \cite{stratton1941}.
\(k_{0}D_{s}=0.6\) at \(28\,\mathrm{GHz}\) on \(150\,\mathrm{mm}\) outline: \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\le4\) despite \(|\mathbb{C}^{I}|=120\) mode chart
\cite{stratton1941}.
Dimensionality is imposed by \((\Omega_{s},k_{0},\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}})\) through \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\), not by how many indices \(\mathcal{C}\)
prints or how fine a mesh resolves \(\Jimp\) \cite{stratton1941}. Every synthesis report that attributes
rank saturation to synthesis failure without verifying electrical size mis-identifies the origin of truncation \cite{collin2001}.
 Reports that
tabulate \(\sigma_{n}\) without \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) context are incomplete rank bounds \cite{stratton1941}.
\(\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) class---metal, dielectric, or active---enters Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.hardware-rank-map} through constitutive parameters that alter
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) without changing the monotonicity conclusions on fixed class \cite{stratton1941}.
Rank reports must declare material class alongside \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) \cite{harrington2001}.
Enlarging mechanical aperture or enclosure diameter raises radiative rank when frequency is fixed \cite{stratton1941}. Phase retuning on fixed diameter explores a subspace; it does not recreate dish-scale rank on a patch \cite{hansen1988}.
\emph{Problem 4.9.1.} Identical mechanical outline at $1\,\mathrm{GHz}$ and $3\,\mathrm{GHz}$; FEM model with $10^{4}$ tetrahedra; $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; passive linear exterior. Use Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.hardware-rank-map}, (\ref{thm:ch4.geometry-rank-origin}), and (\ref{lem:ch4.mesh-dof-trap}).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] Explain why $N_{\mathrm{dof}}=10^{4}$ does not set $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ on fixed $(\Omega_{s},k_{0})$.
\item[(b)] Predict rank ordering at $1\,\mathrm{GHz}$ versus $3\,\mathrm{GHz}$ on the same outline.
\item[(c)] State the quadruple a laboratory rank report must publish.
\end{enumerate}
For part~(a), (\ref{lem:ch4.mesh-dof-trap}): mesh refinement approximates $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ but cannot enlarge $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ on fixed $(\Omega_{s},k_{0})$. Singular values are continuum properties of the radiation map.
For part~(b), (\ref{thm:ch4.geometry-rank-origin}): tripling $k_{0}$ at fixed outline raises $k_{0}D_{s}$ and therefore $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ via Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.outline-trap}. Mechanical CAD alone is an insufficient rank bound.
For part~(c), Report $(k_{0}D_{s},\varepsilon,d_{\mathrm{eff}},N_{\mathrm{mode}})$ with geometry class declared. Mesh $N_{\mathrm{dof}}$ and harmonic listing cardinality $|\mathbb{C}^{I}|$ are diagnostics, not substitutes for $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$.
\subsection{Limits of phase-only engineering}
\label{sec:ch492}
Reflectarrays and metasurfaces are the everyday laboratory face of phase-only engineering: hundreds of phase states rotate $\mathbf{c}$ within a fixed export cone on unchanged outline \cite{harrington2001}. steering is not synthesis of new channels---$\Delta d_{\mathrm{eff}}=0$ under phase-only retuning on fixed \((\Omega_s,k_0)\) per Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.phase-tangent-bound}; amplitude support or electrical enlargement is required to raise $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ or $N_{\mathrm{mode}}$ \cite{devaney2004}.
\medskip
Phase-only surfaces---reflectarrays, metasurfaces, and programmable skins---rotate $\mathbf{c}$ within a fixed export cone on unchanged outline \cite{harrington2001}. Beam steering, orbital labeling by phase plates, and sidelobe shaping by retuning are legitimate within $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$; they are not rank enlargement \cite{devaney2004}.
Campaigns report $\Delta d_{\mathrm{eff}}=0$ explicitly when only $\phi_n$ varies on fixed $|\widehat{J}_n|$ portraits, and should cite Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.phase-tangent-bound} when comparing phase states \cite{stratton1941}. Amplitude support or electrical enlargement remains the path to new channels \cite{chu1948}.
On fixed \((\Omega_s,k_0)\), phase rotations retune $\mathbf{c}$ within $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ without enlarging
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\), \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)\), or \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\varepsilon)\) \cite{hansen1988}.
Use (\ref{prop:ch4.tangent-dimension}), (\ref{thm:ch4.geometry-rank-origin}), and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay}
\cite{stratton1941}. Passive linear exterior; fixed \(\mathcal{L}_{2}\) envelope on \(\Omega_{s}\); tolerance
\(\varepsilon\in(0,1)\) in aligned flux gauge \cite{jackson1999}. Phase-only designs vary \(\phi_{n}\) on fixed \(|\widehat{J}_{n}|\) portraits
\cite{collin2001}.
\phantomsection\label{prop:ch4.phase-only-ceiling}
Phase-only optimization on fixed $\Omega_s$ cannot increase $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$, $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$, or $N_{\mathrm{mode}}$ \cite{stratton1941}. It rotates $\mathbf{c}$ within the same export
subspace of dimension \(\le d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) \cite{devaney2004}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.geometry-rank-origin}) and singular-value invariance under unitary phase gauge on fixed support \cite{stratton1941}.
\(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) and \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}\) are properties of \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\), not of phase arguments on fixed amplitude
\cite{hansen1988}.
(\ref{prop:ch4.phase-only-ceiling}) closes the phase-only specification loop: beam steering and orbital-mode labeling by phase plates do not manufacture
high-\(\ell\) tails forbidden by Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} on fixed \(a\) \cite{hansen1988}. Amplitude support must
enlarge \(\mathcal{L}_{2}\) or \(k_{0}a\) must rise to raise \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Phase-only designs explore a tangent space of dimension

\begin{physicalvignette}[Reflector with subreflector and strut scattering]
Consider Reflector with subreflector and strut scattering. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Phase-only surfaces steer within a fixed rank cone . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch492} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.phase-tangent-bound}
\dim\mathcal{T}_{\phi}\le r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon),\qquad
\Delta d_{\mathrm{eff}}=0\ \text{under phase-only retuning},
\end{equation}
with \(\dim\mathcal{T}_{\phi}\ll d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) typical on fixed amplitude support per Proposition~\ref{prop:ch4.tangent-dimension}
\cite{stratton1941}. The bound counts independent phase knobs that retune far-zone direction without creating new export channels \cite{collin2001}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.phase-manifold-rank}
For \(\Jimp(\phi)\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}\) with fixed \(|\Jimp|\) support on \(\Omega_{s}\),
\(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega[\Jimp(\phi)]\) lies in a fixed \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\)-dimensional subspace of \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) for all admissible \(\phi\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
Phase variations are unitary rotations on coefficient charts restricted to fixed amplitude envelopes \cite{harrington2001}. Export singular
values of \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) depend on support and coupling, not on phase arguments alone \cite{hansen1988}.
Phase-only retuning admits
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.phase-amplitude-factor}
\mathbf{c}(\phi)=\mathbf{D}(\phi)\mathbf{c}_{0},\qquad
\mathbf{D}(\phi)\ \text{unitary on }\operatorname{span}\{\mathbf{c}_{0}\},\quad
\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi)=\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(0),
\end{equation}
fixing \(\|\widehat{c}_{\ell m}^{(\sigma)}\|\) sector envelopes while rotating phases within \(\dim\mathcal{T}_{\phi}\)
\cite{stratton1941}. High-\(\ell\) tails cannot be created without amplitude support on enlarged \(\mathcal{L}_{2}\)
\cite{devaney2004}.
Phase-only surfaces explore a low-dimensional manifold inside the full export space at fixed amplitude support \cite{stratton1941}. Orbital-mode claims on fixed area without amplitude support confuse manifold dimension with radiative rank \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.phase-retune-invariant}
\(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)\) and \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\varepsilon)\) are invariant under phase-only optimization on fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\)
\cite{stratton1941}.

\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}=15\) reflectarray with \(\dim\mathcal{T}_{\phi}=2\) steers beam in \(\theta\)--\(\phi\) only \cite{hansen1988}.
Claimed \(\Delta\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}=+3\) after phase-only synthesis on \(k_{0}a=1.5\): measured \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) unchanged at \(4\)
\cite{hansen1988}.
Holographic metasurfaces impose \(\mathbf{c}(\phi)=\mathbf{D}(\phi)\mathbf{c}_{0}\) with \(\mathbf{D}(\phi)\) unitary on a fixed amplitude portrait
\cite{stratton1941}. Steering and focusing are retunings within \(\dim\mathcal{T}_{\phi}\); they do not alter tail exponents or
\(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) on fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) per Lemma~\ref{lem:ch4.phase-retune-invariant} \cite{hansen1988}.
Raising \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) requires amplitude degrees of freedom that enlarge \(\mathcal{L}_{2}\) or couple new \(\ell\) sectors through feed topology
\cite{harrington2001}. Phase-only paths satisfy \(\partial\|\widehat{c}_{\ell m}^{(\sigma)}\|/\partial\phi_{n}=0\) on fixed support
\cite{hansen1988}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.phase-mimo-invariant}
Deployable stream count \(N_{\mathrm{stream}}\) is invariant under phase-only retuning on fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0},\varepsilon)\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
Rank-\(20\) aperture, \(\dim\mathcal{T}_{\phi}=3\): \(97\%\) of radiative channels are unreachable by phase-only gradients per
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.phase-tangent-bound} \cite{stratton1941}.
Phase-only optimization traces a curve on the complex export sphere of dimension \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}-1\) embedded in \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\)
\cite{stratton1941}. The trajectory cannot exit \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) without amplitude or
support enlargement \cite{jackson1999}.
Fraction of \(\mathbb{C}^{I}\) reachable by phase-only retuning is at most \(\dim\mathcal{T}_{\phi}/d_{\mathrm{eff}}\), typically \(\ll1\) on fixed amplitude
support \cite{stratton1941}.
Phase-only engineering is powerful within \(\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\) but powerless against geometry-imposed ceilings on
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\), \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\), and \(N_{\mathrm{mode}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Claims that phase alone eliminates spectral truncation confuse coordinate retuning on \(\mathcal{T}_{\phi}\) with rank creation on \(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\)
\cite{collin2001}.
Reconfigurable intelligent surfaces. RIS phase tiles implement \(\mathbf{D}(\phi)\) on fixed element amplitudes; \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) and \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) are set by tile footprint and \(k_{0}\),
not by tile count alone \cite{stratton1941}. Additional tiles sample the aperture; rank rises only when
electrical aperture grows \cite{harrington2001}.
Phase-only synthesis may create near-field hotspots without raising far-zone \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) per (\ref{thm:ch4.reactive-dominance}) in
Section~\ref{sec:ch472} \cite{stratton1941}. 
Near-field enhancement factor \(10\times\) with \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) unchanged on fixed \(\Omega_{s}\): reactive dominance without rank gain
\cite{stratton1941}.
A fixed-area metasurface retunes $256$ phase states on a disk with $k_{0}a=1.5$ while amplitude support is unchanged. Under Proposition~\ref{prop:ch4.phase-only-ceiling} at fixed $(\Omega_{s},k_{0})$, measured $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})=6$ before and after retuning; only the phase of $\mathbf{c}$ rotates within the same six-dimensional export cone \cite{stratton1941}. Beam steering is not rank enlargement \cite{jackson1999}.
\subsection{Observable manifestations of truncation}
\label{sec:ch493}
Observable signatures. Truncation manifests in pattern and channel domains as
Truncation appears in sidelobes, cross-pol floors, and SNF noise before chart truncation warnings \cite{stratton1941}.
\medskip
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch493
\begin{physicalvignette}[Ceramic-backed planar radiator on a compact ground plane]
Consider a ceramic-backed planar radiator on a compact ground plane. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Truncation appears as shoulders and lost directivity when charts exceed support . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch493} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.truncation-observables}
\Delta_{\mathrm{pat}}
\sim
\|\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}},
\qquad
\Delta_{\mathrm{chan}}
\sim
d_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1},
\end{equation}
with \(\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\) the discarded tail from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy} \cite{hansen1988}.
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-observables} links remainder energy to shoulder level and rank to the spacing of observable channels; elevated
far-zone sidelobes and capacity knees at fixed SNR are consistent signatures of \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1\) or \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) saturation
\cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.truncation-signature}
If \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}[\mathbf{c}]>1\) or \(\ell_{\mathrm{listed}}>\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)\), then measured patterns exhibit
shoulder artifacts \(\Delta_{\mathrm{pat}}\) bounded below by Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.shoulder-level} even under perfect calibration \cite{hansen1988}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.finite-support certificate}) and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy} convert discarded tail mass into far-zone power
\cite{hansen1988}. Unreachable high-\(\ell\) content cannot be absorbed by calibration on \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) \cite{harrington2001}.
(\ref{thm:ch4.truncation-signature}) redirects shoulder diagnostics: when \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1\), recalibration cannot remove artifacts;
aperture enlargement or \(\ell_{\max}\) reduction is required \cite{stratton1941}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.shoulder-level}
\Delta_{\mathrm{pat}}/\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}\sim\sqrt{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}-1},\qquad
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1.
\end{equation}
Shoulder level scales with discarded tail mass \(\|\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}\) per Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-observables}
\cite{hansen1988}.
in linear MIMO models,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.capacity-saturation}
C_{\mathrm{MIMO}}\le\sum_{n=1}^{d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)}\log\bigl(1+\mathrm{SNR}\,\sigma_{n}^{2}\bigr),
\end{equation}
so streams beyond \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) cannot raise capacity \cite{harrington2001}. Mathematically,
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.capacity-saturation} is the water-filling bound on export singular values. On the range, this is the capacity knee laboratories observe
when element count exceeds rank \cite{stratton1941}.
Truncation signatures appear as shouldered patterns and cross-polar lobes when discarded sectors carry measurable watt remainder \cite{harrington2001}. Recognizing the signature prevents misattributing truncation error to feed defects \cite{devaney2004}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.capacity-knee}
Capacity curves knee near stream count \(N_{\mathrm{stream}}\approx d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) at fixed SNR and \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\)
\cite{stratton1941}.

}
