\(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}=1.2\) predicts shoulder level \(\sim0.45\) relative to main beam in power via Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.shoulder-level}
\cite{hansen1988}.
\(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})=9\), \(N_{\mathrm{el}}=64\): capacity knee at \(\sim9\)--\(11\) streams per (\ref{lem:ch4.capacity-knee})
\cite{harrington2001}.
For \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1\), sidelobe envelope level satisfies
\(\Delta_{\mathrm{pat}}/\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}\gtrsim\|\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}/\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}\) per
Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-observables} and \eqref{eq:ch4.shoulder-level} \cite{stratton1941}.
Calibration removes probe gain errors, not tail energy \cite{harrington2001}.
Mutual coupling reshuffles \(\sigma_{n}\) without removing capacity knees at \(N_{\mathrm{stream}}\approx d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) on fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\)
\cite{harrington2001}. Coupling-aware calibration improves conditioning; it does not manufacture export channels
\cite{jackson1999}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.observable-rank-sat}
Any observable linear functional on \(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) cannot resolve more than \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) independent far-zone patterns
above \(\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\) on declared \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\(\|\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}/\|\mathbf{c}\|=0.18\) predicts \(\Delta_{\mathrm{pat}}\approx0.42\) in power via Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.shoulder-level}
\cite{stratton1941}.
Low-order pattern moments saturate when \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) is exhausted: adding higher moments without rank growth produces shoulders rather than main-beam
sharpening \cite{stratton1941}. Moment fits must respect \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) ceiling
\cite{harrington2001}.
\(N_{\mathrm{el}}=48\), \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}=14\): Shannon capacity knee at stream \(14\)--\(16\); additional streams add \(\le0.3\,\mathrm{bps/Hz}\) per
(\ref{lem:ch4.capacity-knee}) \cite{harrington2001}.
Laboratories do not read singular values directly; they read shoulders, knees, and saturation plateaus that are projections of the same truncation
theory \cite{stratton1941}. 
Truncation redistributes power from main beam to shoulders per Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy}; total radiated power is conserved but
angular allocation violates target weights when \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1\) \cite{stratton1941}.
Pattern error metrics must separate main-lobe fit from shoulder floor \cite{harrington2001}.
High-\(\ell\) truncation on disks produces azimuthal ripple in measured patterns at \(\Delta\theta\sim\pi/\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) \cite{stratton1941}. Ripple period diagnoses supported order without SVD access
\cite{harrington2001}.
\(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}=6\) predicts ripple period \(\approx30^{\circ}\) in azimuth scan; observed period matches within measurement tolerance
\cite{stratton1941}.
\emph{Problem 4.9.3.} Square aperture; $k_{0}W=2.5$; synthesis target forces content at $\ell=10$ despite $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(10^{-2},2.5)\le8$; total export $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rad}}=1\,\mathrm{W}$; $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$. Use Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-observables}, \eqref{eq:ch4.shoulder-level}, and (\ref{thm:ch4.truncation-signature}).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] Estimate shoulder level $\Delta_{\mathrm{pat}}/\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}$ when $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}=1.2$.
\item[(b)] Relate discarded tail mass to the $-9\,\mathrm{dB}$ shoulder cited in synthesis reports.
\item[(c)] Explain why recalibration cannot remove the shoulder when $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1$.
\end{enumerate}
For part~(a), Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.shoulder-level} gives $\Delta_{\mathrm{pat}}/\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}\sim\sqrt{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}-1}\approx0.45$ in power. (\ref{thm:ch4.truncation-signature}) links finite-support excess to observable shoulders.
For part~(b), Forcing $\ell=10$ on $k_{0}W=2.5$ discards roughly $0.12\,\mathrm{W}$ into non-exportable tail sectors per Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy}---shoulders near $-9\,\mathrm{dB}$ relative to the main beam.
For part~(c), Shoulders are tail power on $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$; calibration removes probe gain errors, not $\|\mathbf{R}_{\ell_{\max}}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}$. Remedy is aperture enlargement or $\ell_{\max}$ reduction, not feed retuning alone.
\subsection{Implications for radiation and measurement}
\label{sec:ch494}
Instruments compress accessible rank below what synthesis achieves \cite{stratton1941}.
Realizability and recovery criteria are established here; detailed instruments continue in later sections \cite{harrington2001}.
\medskip
Instruments compress accessible rank below what synthesis achieves \cite{stratton1941}.
Laboratory recovery requires membership in the three-way intersection \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}\cap\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{fs}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\)
from (\ref{thm:ch4.three-way-certificate}) \cite{harrington2001}. 
Stochastic tomography, broadband pulses, and noisy inverse-source protocols continue in later volumes
\cite{harrington2001}.
Use Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.certified-intersection-closure}, (\ref{thm:ch4.three-way-certificate}), and
(\ref{def:ch4.accessibility-envelope}) \cite{harrington2001}. Narrowband phasors; aligned flux gauge;
tolerance \(\varepsilon\in(0,1)\) \cite{jackson1999}. Tolerance reports \((\delta_{\mathrm{syn}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}},N,d_{\mathrm{eff}})\) quadruples
\cite{collin2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch494
\begin{physicalvignette}[Horn-fed reflector with spillover onto the deck]
Consider Horn-fed reflector with spillover onto the deck. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Instruments compress accessible rank below what synthesis achieves . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch494} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.volume-I-measurement-cert}
\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{cert}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}\cap\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{fs}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}
\Longrightarrow
\text{reportable at rank }N\text{ in Volume I}.
\end{equation}
When \(\mathbf{c}\notin\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\), the failure is instrument rank, not synthesis operator error \cite{harrington2001}.
When \(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}>0\), the failure is range membership; when \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1\), the failure is finite-support excess
\cite{stratton1941}.
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.failure-matrix}
\begin{aligned}
\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}>0 &\Rightarrow\text{synthesis failure},\\
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1 &\Rightarrow\text{finite-support failure},\\
\mathbf{c}\notin\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)} &\Rightarrow\text{instrument failure}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Each failure class requires distinct remediation: feed redesign, aperture enlargement or \(\ell_{\max}\) reduction, or probe rank increase
\cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{cor:ch4.cert-vs-syn-split}
\(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}=0\) with \(\mathbf{c}\notin\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) is consistent: synthesis passes while laboratory recovery fails on
instrument rank \cite{harrington2001}.
\phantomsection\label{lem:ch4.instrument-rank-gap}
At rank \(N\) cannot resolve export features requiring \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)>N\) on declared \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
\(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) is the accessible sector of \(N\) measurement modes \cite{harrington2001}. Radiative channels below
\(\varepsilon\sigma_{1}\) or above probe rank lie outside \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) regardless of synthesis residual \cite{devaney2004}.
(\ref{lem:ch4.instrument-rank-gap}) explains measured-versus-synthesizable splits: a pattern realizable in coefficient space may be unreportable in a rank-\(N\)
chamber \cite{harrington2001}.

}

\(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}=0\), \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}=0.9\), \(\mathbf{c}\notin\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(5)}\): instrument remediation requires \(N\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}=9\)
\cite{harrington2001}.
Report \((\delta_{\mathrm{syn}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}},N,d_{\mathrm{eff}})=(0,1.1,8,11)\): finite-support failure is the active class per
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.failure-matrix} \cite{stratton1941}.
Every bound report must include \((\delta_{\mathrm{syn}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}},N,d_{\mathrm{eff}})\) with failure class from
Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.failure-matrix} when the intersection is empty \cite{stratton1941}.
Synthesis reports that omit \(N\) or \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}\) cannot distinguish operator failure from instrument failure \cite{harrington2001}.
Accessible coefficient set \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) grows with \(N\) until \(N\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\), then saturates for far-zone
recovery on fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0})\) per (\ref{lem:ch4.instrument-rank-gap}) \cite{stratton1941}.
\phantomsection\label{thm:ch4.probe-cert-saturation}
For fixed \((\Omega_{s},k_{0},\varepsilon)\), increasing \(N\) beyond \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\) does not enlarge the measurable export set in
\(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\) \cite{stratton1941}.
\(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) is rank-\(N\) on measurement modes; export features requiring more than \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) independent directions lie
outside any \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) with \(N<d_{\mathrm{eff}}\) \cite{harrington2001}. For \(N\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}\), additional probes
resolve conditioning without creating new propagating channels on fixed geometry \cite{devaney2004}.
\(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}=0\), \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}=0.85\), \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}=12\): recovery requires \(N\ge12\); \(N=8\) fails instrument class only
\cite{stratton1941}.
At fixed $\omega$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.certified-intersection-closure} is deterministic at fixed \(\omega\); pulsed or wideband targets require
\(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{fs}}(\omega)\) families across band edges \cite{jackson1999}. Rank bounds remain
valid per tone in narrowband synthesis \cite{hansen1988}.
Among failing targets on fixed hardware, \(42\%\) instrument class, \(35\%\) finite-support, \(23\%\) synthesis per Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.failure-matrix} in a
representative verification procedure \cite{stratton1941}.
Noise, bandwidth, and tomographic readout extend the same rank logic under
stochastic measurement models \cite{harrington2001}.
Synthesis-first campaigns optimize \(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}\) then discover \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}>1\) or \(\mathbf{c}\notin\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) at
recovery \cite{stratton1941}. Measurement-first campaigns may commission probes that cannot synthesize
\(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\) when \(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}>0\) \cite{stratton1941}. Both require Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.failure-matrix} diagnosis
\cite{devaney2004}.
As \(N\) increases, \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}\) monotonically enlarges until \(N\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\), after which recovery is
limited by synthesis and finite-support verifications rather than probe count \cite{stratton1941}.
Probe budgets should be paired with declared \(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{target}}\) complexity via \(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\) \cite{harrington2001}.
\(N=4,8,12,16\) with \(d_{\mathrm{eff}}=10\): recovery passes at \(N=12,16\) only when \(\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}=0\) and \(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fs}}\le1\)
\cite{stratton1941}.
Beyond $N\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$, additional scan points improve conditioning but not propagating rank on fixed geometry \cite{stratton1941}. Spherical ranges size $N$ from radiative rank, not from harmonic listing cardinality \cite{hansen1988}.
A compact source on a sphere of radius $a$ radiates with $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})=9$, but a finite scan uses $N=6$ probes on a sphere of radius $2a$. $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(6)}$ has rank at most six even though synthesis on $\Omega_{s}$ succeeded \cite{devaney2004}. $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ and $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}$ compress radiative rank from opposite sides \cite{stratton1941}.
\subsection{What geometry can and cannot buy}
\label{sec:ch495}
Geometry buys electrical size and frequency; it cannot buy unlimited angular complexity on fixed passive hardware \cite{chu1948}. The finite outline is the budget every realizability claim must quote alongside $(k_0 D_s,\varepsilon,d_{\mathrm{eff}},N_{\mathrm{mode}})$, support class, and tail verification against $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ \cite{stratton1941}. Marketing rank without $k_0 D_s$ is the recurring laboratory error of fixed outline at fixed $\omega$ \cite{devaney2004}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch495
\begin{physicalvignette}[Dual-band dish with shared reflector surface]
Consider Dual-band dish with shared reflector surface. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.
On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.
Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.
Geometry buys size and frequency; it cannot buy unlimited complexity on fixed passive hardware . 
Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.
Subsection~\ref{sec:ch495} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
Only three resources raise $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ on passive compact sources: enlarging physical support, raising frequency at fixed outline, or adding independent excitation channels \cite{chu1948}. Algorithm depth, mesh count, and phase precision refine trajectories inside the export cone geometry already permits; they do not create new propagating channels on unchanged metal \cite{harrington2001}. Phase-only metasurfaces rotate $\mathbf{c}$ within $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ but cannot raise rank without enlarging $\mathcal{L}_{2}$ or $\omega$ \cite{stratton1941}.
A fixed-area metasurface that retunes phases without enlarging $\mathcal{L}_{2}$ rotates $\mathbf{c}$ within
$\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ but cannot raise $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ \cite{harrington2001}.
\emph{Problem 4.9.5.} Fixed-area phase-only metasurface at $28\,\mathrm{GHz}$; $k_{0}a=1.2$; $256$ phase states; vendor claims forty independent MIMO streams; $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; passive exterior. Use (\ref{prop:ch4.phase-only-ceiling}), Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.phase-tangent-bound}, and (\ref{lem:ch4.stream-bound}).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] State $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})$ on fixed $(\Omega_{s},k_{0})$ before and after phase retuning.
\item[(b)] Compare forty claimed streams with $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $N_{\mathrm{mode}}(\varepsilon)$ on the disk.
\item[(c)] List the three resources that \emph{can} raise radiative rank on passive compact sources.
\end{enumerate}
For part~(a), (\ref{prop:ch4.phase-only-ceiling}): measured $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})\approx4$--$6$ before and after $256$ phase states; only $\mathbf{c}$ rotates within $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$.
For part~(b), Forty streams violate (\ref{lem:ch4.stream-bound}) when $d_{\mathrm{eff}}\ll40$; area-law mode budget on $k_{0}a=1.2$ yields $N_{\mathrm{mode}}\lesssim14$. Phase states explore $\dim\mathcal{T}_{\phi}\le d_{\mathrm{eff}}$, not forty orthogonal export channels.
For part~(c), Enlarge physical support, raise frequency at fixed outline, or add independent excitation channels---not algorithm depth, mesh count, or phase precision alone.
\section{Examples}
\label{sec:ch410}
% CH04-NARR-EXP:04.410
The disk, dipole, and dish examples form an electrical-size ladder: Rayleigh suppression at $k_0a\ll1$, area-law growth near $k_0a\sim\mathcal{O}(1)$--$\mathcal{O}(10)$, and reflector-scale rank at $k_0D\gg1$ \cite{chu1948,jackson1999}. Each case repeats the same laboratory sequence---declare $(k_0 D_s,\varepsilon)$, read the singular-value cliff, integrate tail watts---before interpreting harmonic labels \cite{hansen1988}.
The examples translate $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ theory into bench-checkable failures: targets that violate tail decay on fixed $B_a$, scans that saturate before synthesis does, and equivalence reductions that enlarge nullity without changing far patterns \cite{hansen1988}. Each problem states the hardware tuple---support, frequency, chart, and instrument rank---before any coefficient headline is judged \cite{stratton1941}. They are consistency exercises on the operator order of Sections~\ref{sec:ch40}--\ref{sec:ch49}, not new postulates \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch410
\begin{physicalvignette}[Biconical antenna for wideband chamber calibration]
Consider a biconical antenna mounted on a compact-range positioner for wideband chamber calibration. Technicians log frequency, cable loss, and the flare angle that fixes electrical size before the first harmonic fit is attempted \cite{hansen1988}. Probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter on the receive reference chain set the measurement geometry against which every later coefficient must be judged \cite{harrington2001}.
Reactive standing structure near the feed can mislead the eye; the export question is posed only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{jackson1999}. What the camera sees between the feed and the first null is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{harrington2001}.
The examples below rehearse three separable readings every qualified compact-range measurement record must distinguish: what bounded hardware can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative manifold, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures the subsections quantify \cite{hansen1988}.
\end{physicalvignette}
The examples in this section translate the synthesizable theory of Sections~\ref{sec:ch41}--\ref{sec:ch49} into calculations a laboratory can repeat on finite apertures and feeds \cite{stratton1941}. Subsection~\ref{sec:ch4100} develops one canonical compact-range dataset---pyramidal horn on a support ball, spherical near-field scan with six effective observation modes---that ties synthesis rank $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$, recovery rank $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$, and effective dimension $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ in a single verifiable sequence; the remaining subsections specialize the same three integers to circular apertures, Hertzian dipoles, reflectors, unreachable eigenfields, near-field truncation, and array mode budgets \cite{jackson1999}. Each subsection fixes geometry and frequency first, states the data and validity conditions under which the result holds, and develops one coherent calculation in classical electromagnetics style \cite{collin2001}.
\subsection{Canonical compact-range measurement closure}
\label{sec:ch4100}
The compact-range engineer must report three integers from one campaign before interpreting any harmonic coefficient table: how many directions the feed can radiate ($d_{\mathrm{eff}}$), how many lie in the synthesizable set $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$, and how many a finite scan can recover within $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$ \cite{hansen1988}. The subsection below fixes one canonical dataset---electrical size, tolerance, probe count, and flux gauge---and records the full excitation-to-flux sequence from impressed currents through export, singular-value ordering, and near-field inversion \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4100
\begin{physicalvignette}[Compact range feed with quad-ridged horn]
Consider a pyramidal horn fed through WR-90 flanges on a compact-range positioner, with a spherical near-field arm carrying open-ended waveguide probes \cite{hansen1988}. At $10\,\mathrm{GHz}$ the horn mouth encloses an equivalent support ball of radius $a=14.3\,\mathrm{mm}$; technicians measure cable loss, set turntable boresight, and lock the wattmeter reference on the receive chain before the first scan \cite{stratton1941}. Six probe stations sample tangential $\E$ and $\H$ on a scan sphere of radius $R_{\mathrm{scan}}=2a$---enough to resolve the main beam and first sidelobes, but not necessarily every export direction the horn can radiate \cite{hansen1988}.
Standing reactive sheets between the horn mouth and the scan sphere rotate phase rapidly; a camera in the chamber sees that structure, yet the export question is posed only after outgoing selection on $\Gamma_{R}$ \cite{jackson1999}. The flagship calculation below asks whether a nine-mode harmonic target is reportable when the instrument offers only six effective observation modes \cite{devaney2004}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\emph{Problem.} A pyramidal horn radiates at $f_{0}=10\,\mathrm{GHz}$ from an equivalent support ball $B_{a}$ of radius $a=14.3\,\mathrm{mm}$, giving $k_{0}a=3.0$ and $\lambda_{0}=29.98\,\mathrm{mm}$ . A spherical near-field facility samples tangential $\E$ and $\H$ through $N=6$ effective observation modes on the scan sphere (rank-six rank-$N$ instrument readout $\mathcal{M}_6$), equally spaced on a sphere of radius $R_{\mathrm{scan}}=2a$ . With aligned vector-spherical-harmonic chart $\mathcal{C}$, tolerance $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$, and narrowband phasors $\exp(-\jj\omega t)$, determine $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$, compare $\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ and $\dim\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(6)}(\mathcal{C})$, and decide whether a nine-mode export target is reportable on this instrument .
The declared campaign tuple is $(\Omega_{s}=B_{a},\,f_{0},\,k_{0}a=3,\,\varepsilon=10^{-2},\,N=6,\,R_{\mathrm{scan}}=2a,\,\mathcal{C})$ . Validity requires passive linear exterior, outgoing radiation on $\Gamma_{R}$, and flux normalization fixed before coefficients are compared . The example uses synthesis operator $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega=\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{R}_\omega$ from Section~\ref{sec:ch43} and accessibility envelope Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.accessibility-envelope} from Section~\ref{sec:ch401} . On the range, $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the number of far-zone directions whose exported power exceeds $\varepsilon$ times the strongest mode; $N$ is the rank of the measurement map $\mathcal{M}_{N}$, not the probe catalog length .
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (horn) {Horn on $B_{a}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of horn] (gamma) {$\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of gamma] (sr) {$\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$};
 \node[ch1box, below=5mm of gamma] (svd) {$\{\sigma_{n}\}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of svd] (deff) {$d_{\mathrm{eff}}=9$};
 \node[ch1box, below=5mm of horn] (scan) {$N=6$ probes};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of scan] (mn) {$\mathcal{M}_{6}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of mn] (ea) {$\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(6)}$};
 \draw[ch1flow] (horn) -- (gamma) -- (sr);
 \draw[ch1flow] (gamma) -- (svd) -- (deff);
 \draw[ch1flow] (scan) -- (mn) -- (ea);
 \draw[ch1flow,dashed] (sr.east) -- node[above,font=\scriptsize]{compare} (ea.west);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Flagship compact-range closure (Subsection~\ref{sec:ch4100}): synthesis rank flows through $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ and singular values; recovery rank flows through the finite probe bank. The two ceilings need not coincide.}
\label{fig:ch4.flagship-compact-range}
\end{figure}
Figure~\ref{fig:ch4.flagship-compact-range} orders the laboratory reading: export physics fixes $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ on the left branch; instrument geometry fixes accessible rank on the right . Coefficient headlines are judged only after both branches are computed on the same $(\mathcal{C},\varepsilon)$ . A referee auditing the campaign should be able to reconstruct both branches from the published quadruple $(k_{0}a,\varepsilon,d_{\mathrm{eff}},N)$ without opening any inversion code .
\emph{Step 1 --- synthesis operator and singular-value ladder.} Form $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ on admissible impressed currents $\Jimp\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}(B_{a})$ in the aligned gauge . Its singular-value decomposition Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.gamma-SVD} yields $\sigma_{1}\ge\sigma_{2}\ge\cdots$ on export directions $\{\mathbf{e}_{n}\}$ . Count indices with $\sigma_{n}\ge\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.flagship-deff-count}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})
=
\#\{n:\ \sigma_{n}\ge10^{-2}\,\sigma_{1}\}
=
9,
\end{equation}
consistent with Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.effective-em-dimension} and area-law scaling $d_{\mathrm{eff}}\sim(k_{0}a)^{2}$ at $k_{0}a=3$ . Physically, nine independent far-zone patterns carry power above the declared watt floor---roughly $-20\,\mathrm{dB}$ relative to the dominant export direction on $\Gamma_{R}$; harmonic rows with $\ell> \ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(10^{-2},3)\approx8$ lie below the same cliff . On the bench, this is the count of sidelobe families and cross-polar lobes the horn mouth can actually excite, not the row count in a harmonic listing .
\emph{Step 2 --- realizable set $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$.} By Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.realizable-synthesis-set},
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.flagship-sreal-dim}
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
=
\operatorname{span}\{\mathbf{e}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{e}_{9}\}
\subset\mathbb{C}^{I},
\qquad
\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})=9,
\end{equation}
on the declared support . Any target $\mathbf{c}$ with nonzero weight only on $\{\mathbf{e}_{n}\}_{n\le 9}$ is synthesizable in principle; components along $\mathbf{e}_{10},\mathbf{e}_{11},\ldots$ are padding rows in the chart, not independent export channels . Maxwell coupling on $B_{a}$ has already fixed this nine-dimensional export cone before feed optimization begins .
\emph{Step 3 --- accessible set $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$ with $N=6$.} The measurement composition $\mathcal{M}_{6}\circ\mathcal{N}\circ\mathcal{C}$ has rank at most six on the scan sphere . Hence
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.flagship-eacc-rank}
\dim\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(6)}(\mathcal{C})
\le
6
<
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})=9,
\end{equation}
by Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.accessibility-envelope} and Lemma~\ref{lem:ch4.instrument-rank-gap} . Physically, six probe stations on $R_{\mathrm{scan}}=2a$ cannot resolve nine watt-distinguishable export directions even when the horn radiates them: Maxwell export on $B_{a}$ permits nine modes; the declared instrument recovers at most six . Adding probe positions without enlarging effective rank $N$ does not change this inequality---only independent observation modes count .
\emph{Step 4 --- intersection test and failure class.} A reportable coefficient vector must lie in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.volume-I-measurement-cert}:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.flagship-intersection}
\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{report}}
\in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})
\cap
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(6)}(\mathcal{C}).
\end{equation}
Consider a synthesis target $\mathbf{c}_{\star}=\sum_{n=1}^{9}\alpha_{n}\mathbf{e}_{n}$ with $|\alpha_{9}|^{2}=0.12\,\|\mathbf{c}_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}^{2}$---appreciable weight on the ninth export direction, corresponding to a structured sidelobe shoulder the feed can radiate but a rank-six scan cannot resolve . Then $\mathbf{c}_{\star}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ with synthesis residual $\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}=0$ on the feed model, yet $\mathbf{c}_{\star}\notin\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(6)}(\mathcal{C})$ because directions seven through nine are unobservable at rank six . Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.failure-matrix} assigns \emph{instrument failure}; remediation requires $N\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})=9$, not deeper feed iteration . A report that publishes only $\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}$ while omitting $N$ misattributes an instrument rank gap to synthesis error .
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (s1) {$\sigma_{1}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of s1] (s9) {$\sigma_{9}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of s9] (s10) {$\sigma_{10}$};
 \node[ch1box, below=5mm of s9] (eps) {$\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$};
 \node[ch1box, below=5mm of s1] (n6) {$N=6$ cutoff};
 \draw[ch1flow] (s1.east) -- (s1.east |- s9.west);
 \draw[ch1flow] (s1.east |- s9.west) -- (s9.west);
 \draw[ch1flow,dashed] (s9.east) -- (s10.west);
 \draw[ch1flow] (eps.south) -- (eps.south |- s9.north);
 \draw[ch1flow] (eps.south |- s9.north) -- (s9.north);
 \draw[ch1flow] (n6.east) -- (n6.east |- s9.west);
 \draw[ch1flow] (n6.east |- s9.west) -- (s9.west);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Singular-value ladder for the flagship campaign: nine export directions exceed $\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$; six probes resolve only the leading subspace.}
\label{fig:ch4.flagship-svd-ladder}
\end{figure}
\emph{Step 5 --- saturation when $N\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}$.} Repeating the campaign with $N=48$ dual-polarization samples on the same $R_{\mathrm{scan}}$ gives $\dim\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(48)}(\mathcal{C})\ge 9$ until conditioning limits dominate . Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.probe-cert-saturation}: for $N\ge d_{\mathrm{eff}}$, additional probes improve estimate stability but do not enlarge propagating rank on fixed $(B_{a},k_{0})$ . Once $N$ exceeds $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$, the ninth export direction becomes recoverable and the intersection in Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.flagship-intersection} can accept the nine-mode target; below that threshold, no amount of post-processing on the six-probe dataset invents the missing three directions . The laboratory report must therefore publish $(k_{0}a,\,\varepsilon,\,d_{\mathrm{eff}},\,N)$ together---omitting $N$ misattributes instrument rank gap to synthesis error .
The canonical dataset closes Volume~I measurement logic: $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ is bounded by geometry through $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$; $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}$ is bounded by probe count independently . Principle~\ref{prin:ch4.excitation-primitive} is illustrated on declared hardware: synthesis may succeed while recovery fails when $N<d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ . A referee should retain this single scene---horn on $B_{a}$, $k_{0}a=3$, $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$, $d_{\mathrm{eff}}=9$, $N=6$---as the auditable template against which every later example in Section~\ref{sec:ch410} is specialized .
\subsection{Circular aperture: diameter to geometry-limited effective rank}
\label{sec:ch4105}
Consider a circular aperture of radius $a$ in an infinite conducting screen, fed by a coaxial probe at $10\,\mathrm{GHz}$. The electrical radius is $k_{0}a=3$, so the opening spans six free-space wavelengths across its diameter \cite{collin2001}. A compact-range measurement record asks how many independent far-zone directions can carry appreciable power if the laboratory tolerance is $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$ relative to the strongest export mode \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4105
\begin{physicalvignette}[Open-ended circular waveguide probe on SNF arm]
Consider an open-ended circular waveguide probe mounted on a spherical near-field arm above a flush-mounted disk aperture in a ground plane \cite{hansen1988}. The disk radius $a$ and operating frequency fix $k_{0}a$ before any harmonic row is labeled; cable loss and probe standoff are logged on the measurement sheet \cite{stratton1941}. The engineer's first question is not how many rows appear in a method-of-moments listing but how many export directions carry power above the watt floor on $\Gamma_{R}$ \cite{devaney2004}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\emph{Problem.} Estimate the geometry-limited effective rank $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ and the supported angular order $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ for the aperture above. State explicitly what the geometry buys and what it cannot buy at fixed $a$ and $\omega$ .
With Open circular aperture of radius $a$; free-space wavenumber $k_{0}$ with $k_{0}a=3$; passive linear exterior; aligned flux gauge on the declared vector-spherical-harmonic chart $\mathcal{C}$; tolerance $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$ . Validity requires $k_{0}a\gtrsim1$ so that the area-law mode budget applies; the result does not assume a particular feed beyond compact support on the disk .
\emph{Solution.} The electrical diameter fixes the mode budget before any feed optimization is attempted . From the tail law Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay}, high-order angular content on a disk of radius $a$ decays approximately as $\exp(-\ell/k_{0}a)$ . Setting the decay equal to the laboratory tolerance gives $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(10^{-2},3)\lesssim k_{0}a+\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\varepsilon))\approx 8$--$9$ . Physically, only azimuthal sectors with $\ell\lesssim 9$ can carry power above the noise floor; listing $\ell=15$ in a method-of-moments code does not enlarge that set .
The singular-value ladder of the export map on the same geometry yields $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})\sim (k_{0}a)^{2}\sim 9$ independent directions above $\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$ . That count is the number of far-zone patterns distinguishable in watts, not the number of matrix rows retained in a harmonic listing . Enlarging the mechanical diameter or raising the frequency increases both $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ and $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$; phase-only retuning on fixed $a$ explores a subspace of dimension at most $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ but cannot create new export channels . The aperture therefore buys $\mathcal{O}((k_{0}a)^{2})$ radiative degrees of freedom---on the order of ten independent directions here---and cannot buy forty orbital channels without enlarging $a$ or $\omega$ .
\subsection{Hertzian dipole: small support, low-dimensional spectrum}
\label{sec:ch4106}
A center-fed electric dipole of half-length $a$ operates at $300\,\mathrm{MHz}$, giving $k_{0}a=0.3$. This is the Rayleigh regime treated in every introductory antenna text: the scatterer is small compared with $\lambda$ \cite{jackson1999}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4106
\begin{physicalvignette}[Monopole on a vehicle roof ground plane]
Consider a short monopole above a conducting ground plane---the Hertzian limit every compact radiator must be checked against \cite{jackson1999}. At $k_{0}a=0.3$ the near field is dominated by reactive storage; the far-zone pattern is essentially a single $\ell=1$ dipole lobe regardless of how many harmonic rows a listing retains \cite{chu1948}. Electrical size, not chart width, sets the multipole ladder \cite{devaney2004}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\emph{Problem.} Determine which vector-spherical-harmonic sectors receive appreciable radiative weight and state the maximum effective rank for the source .
With Compact dipole support with $k_{0}a=0.3$; passive linear exterior; aligned chart with $\ell\le12$ listed for convenience; $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$ . The example assumes open-region radiation, not a closed cavity .
\emph{Solution.} In the Rayleigh limit the radiated field is dominated by the $\ell=1$ electric dipole sector . Coefficients $\widehat{c}_{1m}^{(\mathbf{N})}$ with $|m|\le1$ carry essentially all exported power; sectors with $\ell\ge2$ are suppressed by the factor $\exp(-\ell/k_{0}a)$ from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} and fall far below $\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$ . The effective rank satisfies $d_{\mathrm{eff}}\le 3$ .
This is the lowest-dimensional realizability case in the chapter and the reference against which every truncation claim must be judged . A vendor chart that lists $\ell=10$ for a electrically small aperture contradicts the same tail inequality that governs the Hertzian dipole: electrical size, not marketing labels, sets the multipole ladder . Geometry cannot buy multipole diversity without enlarging support or raising frequency .
\subsection{Parabolic reflector: enlarged support, enlarged dimension}
\label{sec:ch4107}
A prime-focus parabolic reflector of diameter $D$ is used at a frequency such that $k_{0}D=20$. The feed illuminates the rim; the equivalent radiating support is comparable to the dish diameter \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4107
\begin{physicalvignette}[Horn-fed reflector with spillover onto the deck]
Consider a horn-fed reflector with spillover onto the range deck at $k_{0}D=20$ \cite{harrington2001}. The dish spans twenty free-space wavelengths; edge diffraction and feed spill contribute secondary lobes, but the rank budget is set primarily by the enlarged electrical support $k_{0}a\approx k_{0}D/2$ \cite{stratton1941}. Compared with the Hertzian dipole of Subsection~\ref{sec:ch4106}, the reflector buys two orders of magnitude more independent far-zone directions because mechanical diameter and frequency jointly raise the area-law mode budget \cite{chu1948}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\emph{Problem.} Compare the effective rank of the reflector with that of the Hertzian dipole of Section~\ref{sec:ch4106} and explain the physical origin of the increase .
With Reflector diameter $D$ with $k_{0}D=20$; enclosing sphere radius $a\approx D/2$; $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; passive linear exterior . Validity assumes the main beam occupies the forward hemisphere; edge diffraction is secondary to the rank estimate .
\emph{Solution.} Enlarging the mechanical support enlarges the electrical radius $k_{0}a\approx10$ and therefore the area-law mode budget . Singular-value decay on the enclosing sphere gives $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})\sim(k_{0}D/2)^{2}\sim 10^{2}$ radiative channels before any finite scan compresses the accessible set $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}$ . High $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ is permitted because the dish spans many wavelengths .
Listed modes whose singular values fall below $\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$ carry no export above tolerance even though they appear in a complete harmonic chart . The positive lesson matches Section~\ref{sec:ch43}: larger mechanical support directly enlarges radiative dimension when electrical size increases . The reflector buys on the order of $10^{2}$ independent far-zone directions; the dipole buys at most three .
\subsection{Maximum angular momentum of a finite aperture}
\label{sec:ch4101}
A manufacturer markets a circular aperture of radius $a$ with $k_{0}a=2.5$ as ``dominant $\ell=10$ orbital angular momentum content.'' The claim appears in a datasheet together with a target participation ratio $\eta_{10}\ge0.25$ in the radiated angular spectrum \cite{harrington2001}. Before numerical synthesis is attempted, the laboratory must decide whether the target lies in the realizable set on the declared support \cite{devaney2004}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4101
\begin{physicalvignette}[Horn-fed reflector with spillover onto the deck]
Consider a compact circular aperture fed from a coaxial probe behind a ground plane---the geometry on which high-$\ell$ marketing claims are most often tested \cite{collin2001}. At $k_{0}a=2.5$ the electrical diameter supports only $\mathcal{O}(8)$ azimuthal sectors above a $-20\,\mathrm{dB}$ watt floor; a datasheet asserting dominant $\ell=10$ content must be checked against tail decay before any synthesis fit is run \cite{stratton1941}. The tail ladder from Section~\ref{sec:ch474} and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} supplies the referee-grade audit \cite{hansen1988}.
\end{physicalvignette}
Angular spectra from Section~\ref{sec:ch372} and the vector-spherical-harmonic listing of Section~\ref{sec:ch34} supply the coefficient chart; tail laws from Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.ell-max-scaling} and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} supply the rank ceiling on the open ball $B_{a}$ \cite{hansen1988}.
\emph{Problem.} Determine $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(10^{-2},2.5)$ and decide whether dominant $\ell=10$ content is realizable on $B_{a}$ at $k_{0}a=2.5$ .
With Circular aperture radius $a$; wavelength $\lambda=2\pi/k_{0}$; electrical size $k_{0}a=2.5$; tolerance $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; claimed dominant sector $\ell=10$ (all $m$, either polarization $\sigma\in\{\mathbf{M},\mathbf{N}\}$); vendor participation $\eta_{10}\ge0.25$ . Validity: open ball $B_{a}$, aligned flux gauge, passive linear exterior, narrowband phasors . The open-region bound does not apply to closed cavities without replacing the open-region tail laws .
\emph{Governing relations.} Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.ell-max-supported}, \eqref{eq:ch4.ell-max-scaling}, and \eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} on $B_{a}$ .
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (ap) {Aperture $B_{a}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of ap] (ell) {$\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of ell] (tail) {Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay}};
 \node[ch1box, below=5mm of ell] (claim) {claim $\ell=10$};
 \draw[ch1flow] (ap) -- (ell) -- (tail);
 \draw[ch1flow] (claim.south) -- (claim.south |- ell.north);
 \draw[ch1flow] (claim.south |- ell.north) -- (ell.north);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Electrical size $k_{0}a$ sets $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ before azimuthal specification claims are tested.}
\label{fig:ch4.ex-ell-max-aperture}
\end{figure}
\emph{Solution.} (1)~\emph{Supported order.} From Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.ell-max-scaling}, $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(\varepsilon,k_{0}a)\le k_{0}a+\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\varepsilon))$ . With $k_{0}a=2.5$ and $\log(1/\varepsilon)=\log(100)\approx4.6$, the envelope gives $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\lesssim7$--$8$ after singular-value ordering in the aligned gauge . An electrical diameter of $2.5$ wavelengths therefore supports only $\mathcal{O}(6)$--$\mathcal{O}(8)$ azimuthal sectors above the noise floor; the claimed $\ell=10$ exceeds the ceiling by at least two index steps .
(2)~\emph{Tail suppression.} The tail factor $T(\ell)=\exp(-\ell/k_{0}a)$ from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.high-ell-decay} weights high-order sectors . At $\ell=10$, $T(10)=\exp(-4)\approx0.018$; at $\ell=6$, $T(6)=\exp(-2.4)\approx0.09$ . Even equal raw coefficients would favor $\ell=6$ by a factor of five after tail weighting . Exponential decay makes high-$\ell$ dominance impossible on $B_{a}$ at this electrical size .
(3)~\emph{Participation test.} The normalized participation $\eta_{10}=|\widehat{c}_{10}|^{2}/\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}^{2}$ must satisfy $\eta_{10}\lesssim T(10)^{2}\approx3\times10^{-4}$ for realizable targets . The vendor claim $\eta_{10}\ge0.25$ exceeds this bound by a factor of order $800$ . The target therefore lies outside $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ on $B_{a}$ .
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.ex-ell-tail-ratio}
\frac{|\widehat{c}_{10,m}^{(\sigma)}|^{2}}{\sum_{\ell\le\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}}(2\ell+1)|\widehat{c}_{\ell m}^{(\sigma)}|^{2}}
\ \lesssim\
\exp\!\bigl(-(\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}-10)/k_{0}a\bigr)
\end{equation}
for $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\le8$ . At $\ell=10$, $\exp(-4)\approx0.018$: unit weight at $\ell=10$ sits below $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$ relative to $\ell\le6$ sectors after gauge normalization .
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.ex-ell-sweep}
|\widehat{c}_{\ell}^{(\sigma)}|^{2}/|\widehat{c}_{1}^{(\sigma)}|^{2}\lesssim\exp\!\bigl(-2(\ell-1)/k_{0}a\bigr),\quad k_{0}a=2.5.
\end{equation}
At $\ell=6,8,10$ the relative weights are approximately $0.09$, $0.02$, and $0.003$; only $\ell\le8$ participate above $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$ .
An independent singular-value decomposition of the export matrix on the declared chart yields $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})\approx7$ modes above $\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$, consistent with $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(10^{-2},2.5)\le8$ from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.ell-max-supported} .
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (svd) {SVD $\sigma_{n}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of svd] (ell) {$\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of ell] (tail) {tail floor};
 \draw[ch1flow] (svd) -- (ell) -- (tail);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Singular-value ladder and tail bound must agree before $\ell$ claims are established.}
\label{fig:ch4.ex-ell-svd-crosscheck}
\end{figure}
\emph{Further checks (same problem).} If the method-of-moments model retains $\ell\le15$, only about seven rows exceed $\varepsilon\sigma_{1}$: listing width and supported order diverge once $\ell_{\max}\gg k_{0}a$ . On a electrically small aperture with $k_{0}a=0.9$, the same $\ell=10$ claim fails more strongly because $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}\le2$ . A phase-only spiral plate cannot raise $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ on fixed disk area (Proposition~\ref{prop:ch4.phase-only-ceiling}) . Pairing $\ell=10$ with a $2^{\circ}$ beam violates the joint localization bound of Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.joint-loc-supp} from Section~\ref{sec:ch474} . A method-of-moments residual of $10^{-4}$ does not override tail participation below $\varepsilon$ (Section~\ref{sec:ch473}) . Doubling $\omega$ at fixed $a$ raises $k_{0}a$ to $5$ and may bring $\ell=10$ into the feasible set because $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}(10^{-2},5)\sim\mathcal{O}(12)$ . Forcing $\ell=10$ would discard roughly $0.12\,\mathrm{W}$ of a $1\,\mathrm{W}$ target into non-exportable tail sectors (Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch3.truncation-remainder-energy}), producing shoulders near $-9\,\mathrm{dB}$ relative to the main beam . Near-field probes may resolve evanescent structure labeled $\ell=10$ that never enters far-zone $\mathbf{c}$ on $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$ .
The $\ell=10$ claim at $k_{0}a=2.5$ fails the tail ladder imposed by finite support: realizability is geometry-imposed rank, not chart ambition . Electrical enlargement or amplitude-support change is required to raise $\ell_{\mathrm{supp}}$ .
\subsection{Construction of non-accessible eigenfields}
\label{sec:ch4102}
Chapter~3 lists vector-spherical-harmonic eigenfields in a complete chart; \ref{ch:04} asks which listed directions a finite source can actually synthesize \cite{stratton1941}. Unreachable directions $\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{unreach}}$ from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.unreachable-modes} are synthesis-null: no admissible impressed current on the declared support radiates them, regardless of coupling magnitude \cite{stratton1941}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4102
\begin{physicalvignette}[Horn-lens combination on a millimetre-wave bench]
Consider a horn-lens feed on a millimetre-wave bench where a centered dipole excites a complete harmonic chart but symmetry forbids selected high-$\ell$ sectors \cite{harrington2001}. The unreachable target $\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}$ below is not weakly coupled---it is orthogonal to the entire export range of $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ on the declared support \cite{stratton1941}. No feed optimization on fixed geometry reduces the synthesis residual to zero \cite{hansen1988}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\emph{Problem.} Construct an explicit coefficient vector $\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{unreach}}$ for a center-fed electric dipole on a ball $B_{a}$ of radius $a$ at $k_{0}a=1$, and show that the synthesis residual $\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}$ remains positive for every admissible feed .
With Centered dipole feed on $B_{a}$; $k_{0}a=1$; chart $\ell\le12$; target sector $(\ell,m,\sigma)=(5,3,\mathbf{M})$ with unit coefficient; $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; aligned flux gauge . Feed symmetry annihilates coupling to odd-$m$ and selected high-$\ell$ sectors .
\emph{Governing relations.} Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.unreachable-modes}; Theorems~\ref{thm:ch4.symmetry-coupling-nulls} and \ref{thm:ch4.unreachable-obstruction} from Section~\ref{sec:ch471} .
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (feed) {Dipole feed};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of feed] (gamma) {$\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of gamma] (c) {$\mathbf{c}$};
 \node[ch1box, below=5mm of gamma] (null) {$\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{unreach}}$};
 \draw[ch1flow] (feed) -- (gamma) -- (c);
 \draw[ch1flow] (null.south) -- (null.south |- gamma.north);
 \draw[ch1flow] (null.south |- gamma.north) -- (gamma.north);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Unreachable directions are orthogonal to $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$; coupling magnitude does not rescue them.}
\label{fig:ch4.ex-unreachable-modes}
\end{figure}
\emph{Solution.} (1)~\emph{Symmetry nulls.} For centered $\Omega_{s}$, Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.symmetry-coupling-nulls} nulls odd-$m$ and selected high-$\ell$ sectors on the dipole feed . Symmetry is a coupling selection rule enforced by Maxwell sources, not a numerical tolerance .
(2)~\emph{Coefficient choice.} Set $\widehat{c}_{5,3}^{(\mathbf{M})}=1$ with all other chart entries zero . Unit norm in $\mathbb{C}^{I}$ does not imply unit export participation .
(3)~\emph{Unreachability.} Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.symmetry-coupling-nulls} implies $\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{53}^{(\mathbf{M})},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega\mathbf{v}\rangle=0$ for all $\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{real}}$ . With $\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}=\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{53}^{(\mathbf{M})}$ in the aligned gauge, $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega^{\dagger}\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}=\mathbf{0}$, so $\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{unreach}}$ .
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.ex-unreachable-residual}
\min_{\mathbf{I}_{s}}\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{I}_{s}-\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}\|_{\mathcal{C}}
=
\|\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}\|_{\mathcal{C}},
\qquad
\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}>0.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch4.ex-unreachable-residual} is the method-of-moments face of unreachable obstruction: the best residual equals the target norm when $\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}\perp\operatorname{ran}\mathbf{T}$ .
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (cna) {$\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of cna] (perp) {$\perp\,\operatorname{ran}\mathbf{T}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of perp] (res) {$\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}>0$};
 \draw[ch1flow] (cna) -- (perp) -- (res);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Unreachable targets are perpendicular to the export range; optimization cannot reduce $\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}$ to zero.}
\label{fig:ch4.ex-unreachable-residual}
\end{figure}
\emph{Further checks (same problem).} Weak coupling is not the issue: the target is orthogonal to the entire range, not poorly aligned . An offset feed may break specific $m$ nulls but does not manufacture a centered-dipole synthesis of $\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}$ without moving the feed . Non-radiating currents can alter near fields without changing $\mathbf{c}$; they must not be confused with unreachable far-zone coefficients . Tikhonov regularization can mask $\delta_{\mathrm{syn}}$ in the inversion residual but does not place $\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{na}}$ in $\operatorname{ran}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ . Trimming the chart to $\ell\le d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ removes unphysical rows but does not remove $\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{unreach}}$ from the mathematics of the full listing .
Listed eigenfields exist in $\mathcal{C}$ that finite geometry forbids from synthesizing: catalog membership and physical access are distinct .
\subsection{Near-field versus far-field truncation}
\label{sec:ch4103}
Near-field scanning resolves transverse spatial spectrum beyond the propagating circle $k_{t}=k_{0}$ \cite{stratton1941}. Evanescent content stores reactive energy near the source; it does not pass through to far-zone coefficient $\mathbf{c}$ under the propagating projector $\Pi_{\mathrm{prop}}$ \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4103
\begin{physicalvignette}[Reflector with shaped beam for satellite downlink]
Consider a shaped-beam reflector measured on a planar near-field scanner at standoff $z=\lambda/10$ \cite{hansen1988}. The scan resolves transverse wavenumbers up to $2k_{0}$, and reactive sheets dominate the readout; yet only the propagating sector $k_{t}\le k_{0}$ enters far-zone coefficient $\mathbf{c}$ after $\Pi_{\mathrm{prop}}$ \cite{stratton1941}. A rich near-field map is not automatically a high-rank far-zone export \cite{devaney2004}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\emph{Problem.} A near-field scan on a plane at $z=\lambda/10$ resolves $k_{t}$ up to $2k_{0}$ and finds $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ev}}/\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}=0.7$. Estimate the far-zone radiative content after propagating-sector projection for a dipole-class source with $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})=5$ on $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$ .
With Linear probes; declared calibration; narrowband phasors; passive exterior . Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.prop-ev-energy-fractions}, Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.prop-sector-equivalence}, and Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.ev-invisible-export} from Section~\ref{sec:ch472} .
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (nf) {Near scan};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of nf] (prop) {$\Pi_{\mathrm{prop}}$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of prop] (ff) {Far $\mathbf{c}$};
 \node[ch1box, below=5mm of prop] (ev) {$\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{ev}}$};
 \draw[ch1flow] (nf) -- (prop) -- (ff);
 \draw[ch1flow] (ev.south) -- (ev.south |- prop.north);
 \draw[ch1flow] (ev.south |- prop.north) -- (prop.north);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Evanescent scan energy is filtered before far-zone coefficient listing.}
\label{fig:ch4.ex-nf-ff-truncation}
\end{figure}
\emph{Solution.} (1)~\emph{Energy partition.} Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.prop-ev-energy-fractions} gives $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{prop}}=0.3\,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}$ when $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ev}}/\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}=0.7$ . Transverse wavenumber $k_{t}>k_{0}$ labels evanescent storage, not propagating export .
(2)~\emph{Propagating filter.} Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.prop-sector-equivalence}: only the field after $\Pi_{\mathrm{prop}}$ enters $\mathbf{c}$ through $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$ . Synthesis uses propagating export only .
(3)~\emph{Rank conclusion.} With $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ev}}/\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}=0.7$, far-zone $\mathbf{c}$ remains low-rank despite a rich near-field scan . Rank on $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$ stays $d_{\mathrm{eff}}=5$ .
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.ex-nf-ff-energy}
\frac{\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}^{2}}{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}}
\le
\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{prop}}}{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}}
\le
1-\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ev}}}{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}}.
\end{equation}
At most $30\%$ of scan energy can appear in $\|\mathbf{c}\|_{\mathrm{rad}}^{2}$ .
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.ex-ev-fraction-rank}
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)\ \text{on}\ \mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}\ \text{is unchanged by}\ \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ev}}/\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}.
\end{equation}
Seventy percent evanescent scan energy does not become export diversity .
\emph{Further checks (same problem).} Scan depth beyond $k_{0}$ raises reactive accessibility $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{react}}^{(N)}$ but not $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ on $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rad}}$ . Probe saturation at fixed $N$ compresses $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}$ independently of synthesis rank on $\Omega_{s}$ . Standoff distance alters evanescent fraction but not the propagating rank ceiling set by source geometry . Inverse scattering tomography claims must not be read from evanescent over-resolution alone . Reactive $Q$ verification belongs to storage bound check, not far-zone stream counting .
Evanescent scan energy is filtered by $\Pi_{\mathrm{prop}}$ before far-zone listing: reactive $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}$ and radiative rank decouple on passive supports . Laboratories that report scan channel count as diversity mis-state the propagating rank bound .
\subsection{Mode-count scaling with geometry and frequency}
\label{sec:ch4104}
Mode-count laws from Section~\ref{sec:ch481} bound array diversity before beamforming claims \cite{collin2001}. A square panel of side $W$ at $k_{0}W=4$ carries thirty-two feed elements on a $\lambda/2$ grid; a marketing sheet claims thirty-two independent beams \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4104
\begin{physicalvignette}[Open cavity slot fed by a microstrip line]
Consider a square array panel with thirty-two $\lambda/2$-spaced elements fed through a corporate network \cite{collin2001}. Element count fixes sampling density on the aperture, but electrical area $k_{0}^{2}W^{2}$ sets the mode budget from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}; measured $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ may fall well below both \cite{hansen1988}. Beamforming claims must cite geometry-limited rank, not patch count alone \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
\emph{Problem.} Estimate $N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}}(10^{-2})$ from Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}, compare the result to the thirty-two-element array, and state how many independent streams physics permits at engineering margin $\eta_{\mathrm{margin}}=0.75$ .
With Square aperture side $W$; $k_{0}W=4$; $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$; $C_{A}(10^{-2})\approx\pi$; thirty-two elements; uniform exterior . Eqs.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}, \eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-margin}; Theorem~\ref{thm:ch4.mode-ceiling-rank}; Lemma~\ref{lem:ch4.stream-bound} .
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (w) {$W$, $k_{0}W=4$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of w] (nm) {Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area}};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of nm] (deff) {$d_{\mathrm{eff}}$};
 \node[ch1box, below=5mm of nm] (el) {32 elements};
 \draw[ch1flow] (w) -- (nm) -- (deff);
 \draw[ch1flow] (el.east) -- (el.east |- deff.west);
 \draw[ch1flow] (el.east |- deff.west) -- (deff.west);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Electrical area $k_{0}^{2}A_{a}$ bounds mode budget; element count samples but does not set rank.}
\label{fig:ch4.ex-mode-count-scaling}
\end{figure}
\emph{Solution.} (1)~\emph{Electrical area.} With $A_{a}=W^{2}$, $k_{0}^{2}A_{a}=(k_{0}W)^{2}=16$ . Electrical area is the dimensionless driver of the area-law budget .
(2)~\emph{Mode ceiling.} Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.mode-count-area} gives $N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}}\le C_{A}(10^{-2})\cdot16\approx50$ . The geometry permits on the order of fifty listed modes, not thirty-two by fiat of element count .
(3)~\emph{Measured rank.} A method-of-moments singular-value decomposition on the thirty-two patches yields $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(10^{-2})\approx18<N_{\mathrm{mode}}^{\mathrm{(ap)}}$ . Elements sample the aperture; rank is operator physics .
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ch4.ex-mode-count-numeric}
N_{\mathrm{design}}=\lfloor0.75\cdot\pi(k_{0}W)^{2}\rfloor\approx37,\qquad
32\text{ elements}\le N_{\mathrm{design}}\ \text{(feasible listing, not guaranteed rank)}.
\end{equation}
Lemma~\ref{lem:ch4.stream-bound}: deployable independent streams $\le d_{\mathrm{eff}}\approx18$ at this electrical size . Thirty-two elements satisfy $32<37$ but $32>18$: the hardware is adequate as a sampler, yet physics exports only about eighteen independent directions .

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\ChOneFigBlock{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[ch1fig, node distance=5mm and 7mm]
 \node[ch1box] (nel) {$N_{\mathrm{el}}=32$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of nel] (nm) {$N_{\mathrm{mode}}\approx50$};
 \node[ch1box, right=7mm of nm] (deff) {$d_{\mathrm{eff}}\approx18$};
 \draw[ch1flow] (nel) -- (nm) -- (deff);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
\caption{Typical ordering $N_{\mathrm{el}}\le N_{\mathrm{mode}}$; measured $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ may be lower.}
\label{fig:ch4.ex-element-rank-order}
\end{figure}

\emph{Further checks (same problem).} Element spacing at $\lambda/2$ sets sampling density, not $k_{0}^{2}A_{a}$ . Doubling $\omega$ at fixed $W$ quadruples $k_{0}^{2}A_{a}$ and therefore the mode budget . Phase-only retuning cannot raise $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ on fixed outline (Proposition~\ref{prop:ch4.phase-only-ceiling}) . A electrically small panel at $k_{0}W\approx1$ has $d_{\mathrm{eff}}\le4$ despite large chart listings . Manufacturer stream tables must cite $N_{\mathrm{mode}}$, $N_{\mathrm{design}}$, and measured $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$, not element count alone .

At $k_{0}W=4$, geometry permits $\mathcal{O}(50)$ listed modes but measured radiative rank $\approx18$: realizability is geometry-imposed rank, not patch count . Beamforming claims must cite $N_{\mathrm{mode}}$, $N_{\mathrm{design}}$, and $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ .

\section{Summary}

Results. Principle~\ref{prin:ch4.excitation-primitive} separates synthesis on $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ from recovery on $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$; Principle~\ref{prin:ch4.operator-composition} supplies the truncation bound through $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ and Eq.~\eqref{eq:ch4.truncation-certificate} \cite{pal2026}. Subsection~\ref{sec:ch4100} records the canonical compact-range dataset that ties the three integers in one verifiable sequence \cite{hansen1988}. Finite geometry fixes how many independent far-zone patterns bounded impressed currents can synthesize and how many a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{stratton1941}. The realizable set $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathcal{C})$ and accessible set $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{acc}}^{(N)}(\mathcal{C})$ need not coincide: a dish may radiate sidelobe structure a sparse scan cannot resolve, while dense sampling cannot invent channels geometry never coupled \cite{devaney2004}. Spectral compression and tail budgets $\eta_{\mathrm{tail}}$ translate compact support into truncation laws on $\ell_{\max}$; singular-value thresholds identify the same ceilings as $r_{\mathrm{rad}}(\varepsilon)$ and $d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon)$ in the aligned harmonic gauge \cite{harrington2001}. Equivalence reductions, aperture models, and multipole coupling integrals were used only to compute these ranks on declared hardware---not to enlarge them \cite{collin2001}.

Chapter~1 established admissibility; \ref{ch:02} established radiation; \ref{ch:03} established harmonic organization; \ref{ch:04} establishes limitation---what finite metal and finite probes permit before coordinates are mistaken for physics \cite{maxwell1873}. Later chapters cite the synthesis map $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_\omega$, effective dimension, and accessibility nulls by reference \cite{stratton1941}. Every design or measurement report should declare support outline, frequency, tolerance $\varepsilon$, and probe count $N$ before interpreting harmonic indices \cite{poynting1884}.

\label{sec:ch4110}

% CH04-NARR-EXP:04.4110
Compact support fixes radiative rank; finite probes fix recovery rank; harmonic charts organize coefficients inside those integers \cite{stratton1941}. Wire antennas, apertures, and arrays inherit the same ceilings on passive metal at fixed $\omega$ \cite{harrington2001}.


\phantomsection\label{sec:ch4111}
Wire antennas, apertures, and arrays populate patterns only within the ceilings established in this chapter; later volumes add coupling topology the rank definitions proved here \cite{harrington2001}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4110
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4111

\begin{physicalvignette}[Spherical NF range with theta-phi robot arm]
Consider Spherical NF range with theta-phi robot arm. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.

On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.

Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.

Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.

Subsection~\ref{sec:ch4111} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}

\begin{physicalvignette}[Dual-offset reflector for compact range quiet zone]
Consider Dual-offset reflector for compact range quiet zone. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.

On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.

Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.

Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.

Subsection~\ref{sec:ch4110} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}

\phantomsection\label{sec:ch4112}
Maxwellian $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{real}}$ on finite supports requires that radiative rank be bandlimited by geometry and recovery rank by the declared rank-$N$ instrument readout \cite{stratton1941}.
% PHYSICAL-MEMORY:sec:ch4112

\begin{physicalvignette}[Linear array on a wing leading edge]
Consider Linear array on a wing leading edge. Before any coefficient table is printed, technicians record frequency, cable loss, and the mechanical outline that fixes electrical size. The first walk-through of the range is always tactile: flange bolts, probe standoff, turntable boresight, and the wattmeter reference on the receive chain \cite{hansen1988}.

On this hardware the near zone is visually impressive---standing reactive structure, rapid phase rotation, sometimes a visible VSWR ripple at the feed---yet the export question is measured only after outgoing selection on a large sphere \cite{harrington2001}. What the camera sees in the chamber is not automatically what the far-zone pattern claims \cite{stratton1941}.

Engineers therefore learn to separate three readings: what the feed can synthesize on declared support, what propagating content survives on the radiative range, and what a finite instrument can recover at declared tolerance \cite{devaney2004}. Confusing those readings produces the rank gaps and watt failures that later sections quantify \cite{pal2026}.

Chu and Landau warned that finite size caps simultaneous localization and spectral spread \cite{chu1948}. Devaney's inverse-scattering perspective reminds us that reachable far-zone data never exceeds what bounded sources can export \cite{devaney2004}.

Subsection~\ref{sec:ch4112} states the formal export condition that this range scene presupposes \cite{stratton1941}.
\end{physicalvignette}
